CPTE Answer Keys and Explanations

Numbered to match the practice tests in your printed study guide.

Practice Test 1 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is D (Develop a learning plan that targets the identified gap and record her completed activities): Self-assessment exists so a clinician can find a gap and act on it with a documented plan tied to the caseload. Waiting for the college to assign education hands away her own responsibility. A single general course of her choosing may not address the identified gap. Informal advice from colleagues without a written plan leaves no record of learning or progress.
  2. The correct answer is C (Remove the bandage immediately, reassess circulation and sensation, and report the findings to the physician): Severe pain, tingling, coolness, dusky colour and slow refill indicate compromised circulation, so the bandage must come off right away and the physician be informed. Loosening part of it or elevating the leg leaves the constriction in place. Tingling after compression is a warning sign, not an expected effect.
  3. The correct answer is B (Single hop for distance, about 85 percent, found by dividing 140 cm by 165 cm): For distance tests the index is the involved limb divided by the uninvolved limb, so the single hop gives 140/165, about 85 percent. The triple hop is about 92 percent. For the timed hop slower is worse, so the index is 2.20/2.40, about 92 percent, which is also higher. Dividing the uninvolved distance by the involved distance inverts the ratio and produces a value above 100 percent that hides the deficit.
  4. The correct answer is C (Advise gentle nasal suction and semi-upright positioning, because chest percussion does not improve outcomes in bronchiolitis): Bronchiolitis in otherwise healthy infants is managed with supportive care, and chest percussion and vibration have not been shown to help and can cause distress. Clearing the nose improves feeding and breathing. Head-down tilt can worsen reflux and breathing effort, and active breathing techniques need cooperation.
  5. The correct answer is D (What would walking more do for you, and how might it fit into your week?): Open questions that invite the client to explore his own reasons for change are the core of motivational interviewing. The why question sounds judgmental and raises defensiveness. The yes or no question and the request for a commitment close the conversation and put the physiotherapist in the expert role.
  6. The correct answer is D (Explain that checks apply to every staff member and tailor the training to her experience): Competence checks protect clients and apply to all staff, and returning clinicians need to learn what changed. Adjusting the training to her experience respects her background while keeping the standard. Waiving checks or delaying the whole return is not necessary.
  7. The correct answer is A (Lobar consolidation, because dense airless lung transmits sound more readily than normal lung): Consolidated lung is solid and airless, so it carries sound well and gives bronchial breath sounds, increased fremitus and a dull percussion note, which fits four days of cough and fever. Fluid around the lung muffles sound and lowers fremitus. Collapse behind a blocked airway also reduces transmission of breath sounds and fremitus. Bronchospasm produces wheeze with a resonant percussion note.
  8. The correct answer is A (A score of 6.0, because he needs unilateral support to walk about 100 metres): On the Expanded Disability Status Scale, a score of 6.0 means intermittent or unilateral constant assistance, such as a cane, is needed to walk about 100 metres with or without rests. Scores of 4.0 and 5.0 describe walking without aid, and 6.5 requires two supports and a much shorter distance. The cane in one hand and the 100 metre limit place him at 6.0.
  9. The correct answer is D (Withhold massage and arrange urgent medical assessment for a suspected deep vein thrombosis in the calf): Recent major lower limb surgery with a unilateral swollen, warm, tender calf is a classic presentation of deep vein thrombosis. Any massage or stretching could dislodge a clot, so soft tissue work is contraindicated and she needs prompt medical assessment. Waiting for a recheck delays time-sensitive care.
  10. The correct answer is C (Use gentle soft tissue and stretching techniques, avoid deep pressure and teach her to report bruising): Anticoagulants increase the risk of bruising and bleeding into soft tissue even when the INR is therapeutic, so vigorous or deep techniques are avoided. Shorter duration or prior warming does not make firm pressure safe. Gentle techniques treat the tightness, and she is taught to report new or spreading bruising.
  11. The correct answer is B (Spontaneous pneumothorax, because air in the pleural space gives hyperresonance and absent breath sounds): Air in the pleural space makes the chest hyperresonant and silences breath sounds, and tall young men are prone to primary spontaneous pneumothorax. A midline trachea and the absence of shock or severe distress argue against a tension pneumothorax. A giant bulla would be expected in a person with long-standing lung disease rather than a sudden onset in a healthy teenager. Severe asthma silences both sides of the chest, not one.
  12. The correct answer is A (Scores show a good response to loading, so add energy-storage loading and continue graded running): Rising VISA-A scores, falling morning pain and increasing heel raise capacity show good load tolerance, so loading should progress toward energy-storage work and graded running. Tendinopathy pain up to about 5 out of 10 is acceptable if it settles by the next morning, so waiting for zero pain is unnecessary. Reducing loading in favour of passive care ignores the evidence that loading drives recovery. Jumping to full-speed plyometrics skips the graded progression the tendon still needs.
  13. The correct answer is C (36 percent: arm 9, front trunk 18 and the front of one leg 9): In an adult each arm is 9 percent, the front of the trunk is 18 percent and each whole leg is 18 percent, so the front of one leg is 9 percent. Adding 9, 18 and 9 gives 36 percent. Counting the whole leg or the back of the trunk overstates the area.
  14. The correct answer is A (Shortly after each infusion, when circulating factor levels are at their highest point): Factor levels peak soon after an infusion and then fall, so training is timed for the hours after a dose when protection is greatest. Sessions just before the next infusion occur when levels are at their lowest. Spacing sessions between doses or training once weekly does not match the period of best protection.
  15. The correct answer is D (Specific, measurable objectives, with agreed dates for review and feedback during the placement): A useful learning plan states specific, measurable objectives shaped by the student's experience and the placement, and schedules review points so progress can be checked and adjusted. Grading criteria with delayed feedback give no chance to adjust during the placement. Broad goals with no scheduled review cannot be measured. Outcomes copied from the university ignore what was learned in the initial discussion.
  16. The correct answer is B (A thirty degree tilt with pillows, with turns timed after analgesia to limit pain): A thirty degree tilt offloads the sacrum without loading the trochanter, and timing repositioning after analgesia respects comfort. Prognosis does not remove the duty to protect skin and comfort. A ninety degree side lying position loads the trochanter and hurts, and donut devices concentrate pressure on surrounding tissue.
  17. The correct answer is C (Infrapatellar fat pad, because passive hyperextension pinches it and swelling sits beside the tendon): Fat pad impingement causes pain below the patella with hyperextension, puffiness on either side of the tendon and pain relief from avoiding terminal extension, with painless resisted extension. Tendinopathy would hurt with loaded extension, a prepatellar bursa swells over the patella itself, and a plica produces medial snapping with flexion.
  18. The correct answer is D (Pause treatment and contact his physician promptly for investigation, because the pattern may indicate metastatic disease): Night pain unaffected by movement, unexplained weight loss and a cancer history are red flags for spinal metastasis. Treating on a trial basis or waiting delays diagnosis and could cause harm. Communication with the physician should come before further mechanical treatment.
  19. The correct answer is D (Stop and seat him with pursed-lip breathing, then report the exertional desaturation for oxygen assessment): His saturation fell below the commonly used exertional threshold of 88 percent, so the walk should stop, he should sit and use breathing control, and the exertional desaturation should be reported for oxygen assessment. Slowing down and continuing keeps him walking while desaturated. Feeling tolerable does not make a falling saturation safe. Starting oxygen without an assessment or order goes beyond the physiotherapist's role.
  20. The correct answer is C (Ankle-brachial index measurement taken after resting supine for ten minutes): Cramping that settles within minutes of standing, faint pulses and cool feet suggest intermittent claudication from arterial insufficiency, and the ankle-brachial index quantifies limb perfusion. A test for venous thrombosis addresses a different vascular problem. An incline treadmill test is used for neurogenic claudication, which usually changes with trunk position, and this pain does not. The slump test targets neural tissue rather than blood supply.
  21. The correct answer is A (Remove the unit from service, label it as faulty, report it, and use a tested unit): A cracked cord and an overdue inspection both mean the unit may not be electrically safe, so it must be removed from service, labelled, reported and replaced with a tested unit. A protected outlet limits some shock risk but does not make a damaged unit safe or complete the inspection. A surge protector guards equipment, not the client from a faulty cord. A replacement cord does not satisfy the overdue safety inspection.
  22. The correct answer is B (Review the protocol and evidence, then discuss her reasoning with both colleagues and her lead): When feedback conflicts, the clinician should compare it with the protocol and evidence and talk through the reasoning with the people involved, using the clinical lead to help reconcile it. Choosing by rank, splitting the difference, or using preference alone does not test which advice is actually safer.
  23. The correct answer is C (Report the new deficits to her physician promptly, because symptoms past 24 hours suggest relapse): New neurological deficits lasting longer than 24 hours without fever, infection or heat exposure suggest a relapse, which needs timely medical assessment because treatment decisions depend on early recognition. Cooling helps only for temperature-related pseudo-exacerbations, which settle within hours, and none of the usual triggers are present. Waiting two weeks or progressing training would delay care and could worsen function.
  24. The correct answer is A (It is routine and well defined, with predictable outcomes and little need for clinical judgement): Routine, clearly defined tasks with predictable outcomes need little clinical judgement and are better suited to delegation. Past experience by the assistant supports competence but does not change how complex the task is. A stable client lowers risk yet the task may still require interpretation. Short duration does not remove the need to confirm competence.
  25. The correct answer is B (It creates a conflict of interest that may bias recommendations, so decline or disclose it): A gift from a supplier creates an actual or apparent conflict of interest that can undermine trust that recommendations serve the client, so it should be declined or disclosed. A small value does not remove the appearance of influence. Choosing the brace on merit earlier does not prevent the gift from biasing future recommendations. The concern is about the relationship, not how the brace performs.
  26. The correct answer is D (Use a wide base, bend the knees, and keep the client close to your trunk): A wide base, bent knees and keeping the client close keep the line of force near the spine and use the large leg muscles. Pulling on the client's arms shifts the load to the shoulders and away from the legs. Twisting the trunk during a lift raises spinal shear. A narrow stance reduces stability even when the knees are bent and the client is close.
  27. The correct answer is D (Ten to fifteen minutes before exercise, followed by a gradual warm-up before the hard efforts): A short-acting bronchodilator used 10 to 15 minutes before exercise, with a gradual warm-up, reduces exercise-induced bronchoconstriction. Taking it after exercise is rescue use, not prevention. Waiting for symptoms to start delays treatment, and a dose two hours earlier will have lost much of its protective effect.
  28. The correct answer is A (Warm up with active movement and gentle stretching, and avoid applied heat over the insensate areas): Loss of protective sensation means he cannot report excessive heat, so thermal modalities risk an unnoticed burn, which heals poorly in diabetes. Towel layers, water thermometers and checking for discomfort reduce but do not remove the risk. Active movement raises tissue temperature safely and prepares the tissues for stretching.
  29. The correct answer is D (A capsular pattern, because the proportional loss of motion reflects a joint-wide capsule restriction): At the glenohumeral joint the capsular pattern is greatest loss of external rotation, then abduction, then internal rotation, and it points to a joint-wide capsular problem such as adhesive capsulitis, which is more common in people with diabetes. A non-capsular pattern would limit only certain directions, and a rotator cuff problem mainly reduces active rather than passive range.
  30. The correct answer is B (Reflecting on one's own biases and learning from clients about their needs and values): Cultural humility is an ongoing practice of self-reflection and learning from the person in front of you. Memorized facts risk stereotyping, identical treatment ignores real differences in need, and community approval of individual care decisions is not what the concept means and could breach client confidentiality.
  31. The correct answer is C (Reassess the resident personally and revise the plan, because new worsening pain needs professional judgment): A change in condition such as rising pain needs reassessment by the physiotherapist, who remains accountable for the plan. Simply reducing the distance or masking pain with analgesics could hide a new problem. Continuing unchanged ignores the symptom the assistant correctly reported.
  32. The correct answer is A (BMI 38.8, obesity class II, which covers values from 35.0 to 39.9): BMI is weight divided by height in metres squared: 112 divided by 2.89 gives 38.8. Class II covers 35.0 to 39.9, and class III starts at 40. The value of 65.9 results from forgetting to square the height.
  33. The correct answer is C (Call emergency services, keep him seated and still, and help with his prescribed nitroglycerin if available): Chest pressure spreading to the arm with sweating that does not ease with rest suggests a myocardial infarction and needs emergency care while he stays seated and still. Monitoring for ten minutes wastes time that heart muscle cannot spare. Checking glucose is reasonable in a client with diabetes, but pallor and arm radiation point to a cardiac event and must not delay the call. Walking him increases cardiac demand.
  34. The correct answer is A (Withhold the pump and arrange urgent medical assessment for a suspected deep vein thrombosis): Unilateral calf swelling, warmth and pain after surgery with immobility suggests a deep vein thrombosis, and pumps, massage or compression could dislodge a clot. Same-day medical assessment is needed. Waiting 48 hours delays diagnosis.
  35. The correct answer is B (Decline to share, and offer to ask the client about consenting to a limited functional summary): The client's consent covers only the board, so the employer has no entitlement, and even attendance or a claim number is personal health information. The physiotherapist can respect his wishes while offering a practical route: seeking his permission to provide a limited summary of functional abilities.
  36. The correct answer is B (Eight to twelve slow contractions held up to ten seconds, three times daily for three months): Strength training of the pelvic floor uses repeated slow, near-maximal contractions with holds building toward ten seconds, several times daily, for at least three months. Short courses and fast contractions alone do not build strength, very long holds exceed what a weak muscle can sustain, and exercising only during leakage gives too little dose.
  37. The correct answer is C (Hip osteoarthritis, because age, groin pain, restricted internal rotation and limited flexion fit together): Age over 50, groin pain, internal rotation under 15 degrees and reduced flexion form a recognized cluster for hip osteoarthritis. Impingement is more likely in younger active adults. Lumbar referral is unlikely when spinal movements do not provoke symptoms, and trochanteric pain would be lateral and tender on palpation.
  38. The correct answer is C (Pause prosthesis use until the blister heals, and ask the prosthetist to assess socket fit): Redness that faded within about 15 minutes on days 1 to 3 was acceptable, but day 4 shows prolonged redness and a blister over the distal tibia, so the skin has broken down. Wearing over an open area worsens damage, so use is paused until healing, and the socket fit should be checked. Cushioning or dressing alone while continuing to wear the prosthesis keeps loading the injured skin.
  39. The correct answer is D (Begin sitting at the bed edge with monitoring, because a stable low vasopressor dose is acceptable): Consensus guidance on early ICU mobility supports activity in alert patients on stable low-dose vasoactive drugs with acceptable oxygen and ventilator settings, provided monitoring and stop criteria are agreed with the team. Waiting for the infusion to stop delays recovery. Corridor walking is a larger step than he is ready for, and passive range of motion alone underuses what his stability permits.
  40. The correct answer is C (Flex the hip to 90 degrees, then slowly extend the knee, noting resistance or pain): In Kernig's test the hip is flexed to 90 degrees and the knee is slowly extended, and resistance or pain in the hamstrings and back suggests meningeal irritation. Passive neck flexion with involuntary hip and knee flexion describes Brudzinski's sign, while the straight leg raise and slump tests screen for neural tension in lumbar radiculopathy. Movements are gentle and stopped if pain is marked.
  41. The correct answer is C (The supervising physiotherapist remains accountable for the delegated care and the outcomes): Delegating a task does not transfer the physiotherapist's professional accountability. The physiotherapist must monitor, reassess and adjust. The assistant is responsible for performing the task as taught, but accountability for the plan and the decision to delegate stays with the physiotherapist.
  42. The correct answer is B (Superficial medial collateral ligament, because laxity at 30 degrees with stable extension suggests isolated injury): A blow to the outside of the knee loads the medial side in valgus, and laxity at 30 degrees with a stable knee in extension indicates isolated superficial medial collateral ligament injury. Laxity in extension would suggest the posterior capsule or a cruciate ligament is also involved. The lateral ligament is stressed by varus force.
  43. The correct answer is D (Neural tension contributes, because changing a distant joint position altered symptoms at the thigh): Structural differentiation changes tension on the nervous system at a site away from the symptoms. A pain change after releasing neck flexion cannot be explained by local hamstring length, so neural tissue is contributing. The unchanged response to ankle release does not exclude neural involvement.
  44. The correct answer is B (Calmly describe the findings, explain why medical assessment is advised, and invite questions): A calm, honest explanation of what was noticed and why it needs medical attention respects his autonomy and helps him act. Downplaying the reason may delay care, and listing serious diagnoses speculates beyond physiotherapy scope and can cause alarm. Sending a referral without discussion removes him from the decision.
  45. The correct answer is B (Reduced FVC and total lung capacity with an FEV1/FVC ratio that is normal or increased): Restrictive disease such as pulmonary fibrosis stiffens the lungs, so volumes including FVC and total lung capacity fall, while airflow relative to volume is preserved and the ratio stays normal or high. Reduced ratios with hyperinflation and bronchodilator reversibility describe obstructive disease.
  46. The correct answer is C (Arrange same-day medical assessment, because a fever with a hot joint could indicate septic arthritis): A hot swollen joint with fever, chills and intolerance of any passive movement may be septic arthritis, which can destroy a joint within days and needs urgent aspiration and antibiotics. Gout can coexist, but the systemic features mean infection must be excluded first. Waiting, icing or mobilizing would delay care or harm an infected joint.
  47. The correct answer is D (Direct the assistant to pause the task and assess the resident in person soon): A marked change in rigidity and freezing means the client's condition is no longer stable, so the delegated task should stop until the physiotherapist assesses in person. Waiting for a weekly meeting or letting the assistant modify technique leaves a clinical judgement with someone not accountable for it. Freezing is common, but a sudden worsening still needs assessment.
  48. The correct answer is C (Tandem stance with eyes closed near the counter, because removing vision increases the challenge safely): Balance is progressed in small steps by narrowing the base, reducing vision or changing the surface, one variable at a time, with a safe support nearby. Having mastered tandem stance, removing vision is a modest step. The other options jump several levels, adding speed, instability and loss of support together in a client with a fall history.
  49. The correct answer is B (Refer to a foot care provider for callus management and offloading footwear, and reinforce daily inspection): A thick callus over a pressure point in a foot with poor sensation and poor circulation is a pre-ulcer sign that needs professional care and offloading. Moisturizing and waiting does not relieve the pressure. Store-bought insoles may not offload the site and rely on a client who cannot feel early damage. Soaking and filing can cause burns, wounds or infection in an insensate, ischemic foot.
  50. The correct answer is D (Stop touching, speak softly, orient her to the room, and let her recover at her pace): A freeze or dissociative response is a protective trauma reaction, and touch can deepen it. Removing contact and using a quiet voice with simple orienting statements helps her return to the present on her own timeline. Shaking her or carrying on repeats the loss of control. Her steady pulse and regular breathing make a faint less likely than a trauma response.
  51. The correct answer is A (Pain brought on by exertion and relieved by rest, with nausea and shortness of breath): Exertional pain relieved by rest with nausea and shortness of breath follows a cardiac pattern, which can refer to the shoulder and needs medical assessment. Pain relieved by resting the hand on the head suggests cervical root irritation. Pain that follows repetitive arm work resembles the exertional pattern but has no systemic symptoms and is tied to the arm. Pain with reaching that eases with support is a mechanical shoulder feature.
  52. The correct answer is D (Manually assisted cough, with a timed abdominal thrust during his expiratory effort after a deep breath): With paralyzed abdominal and intercostal muscles, he cannot generate expiratory force, so a manually assisted cough supplies the missing expiratory pressure. Huffing alone is too weak, incentive spirometry trains inspiration but does not move secretions out, and percussion alone does not produce the expiratory flow needed to clear them.
  53. The correct answer is C (Reduce knee load briefly, use cold and gentle movement within comfort, then restart activity gradually): An osteoarthritis flare responds best to short-term load reduction, cold for symptom relief and pain-tolerable movement, followed by graded return. Complete rest causes deconditioning and delays recovery. Increasing intensity or applying heat to a warm swollen joint and stretching deeply can aggravate the flare.
  54. The correct answer is B (About weekly, reapplying at the new end range after checking skin and circulation each time): Serial casts are usually changed about once a week, with skin and circulation checked and the ankle recast at the new end range. Three-week intervals leave the skin unchecked and slow progress. Daily changes do not allow tissue adaptation, and stopping after one cast rarely corrects a 15 degree contracture.
  55. The correct answer is B (Measure the ankle-brachial pressure index to rule out significant arterial disease before applying any compression): Faint pulses and cool toes raise concern for arterial insufficiency, and high compression is unsafe if arterial supply is poor. The ankle-brachial pressure index quantifies this before bandaging. Reduced-strength bandaging, elevation testing or wound culture does not exclude arterial disease.
  56. The correct answer is A (Elevation above heart level with active finger pumping and retrograde massage, because reducing edema prevents stiffness): Persistent edema after immobilization stiffens joints and tendon sheaths, so the first priority is edema control with elevation, active pumping and gentle retrograde massage. Heat and forced stretching increase swelling, resisted gripping is too aggressive for a healing fracture, and a wrap that begins at the wrist can restrict outflow from the fingers.
  57. The correct answer is B (Light walking in his room with hand hygiene, avoiding resistance work and the shared gym): A neutrophil count of 0.4 means severe neutropenia, so shared gym spaces carry an infection risk, and a platelet count of 18 contraindicates resistance work because of bleeding and bruising risk. Gentle walking in his room maintains function safely. Having no fever does not make the counts safe, and a mask does not offset the platelet problem.
  58. The correct answer is C (She is not ready, because the mechanical lift transfer is high risk and still unsigned): Policy requires every high-risk item to be signed off before independent work. The lift transfer is high risk and has been observed but not signed, so she is not ready. Equipment cleaning is low risk and documentation is moderate risk, so neither blocks independent work under the stated policy. Observation without sign-off does not meet the requirement.
  59. The correct answer is D (Assess whether he understands and appreciates the treatment, since capacity decides consent): In Ontario, consent depends on the person's capacity to understand the information and appreciate the consequences, and there is no fixed age cutoff for health care consent. A parent's consent is not automatically required for a capable adolescent, and an age of sixteen is not a rule for physiotherapy.
  60. The correct answer is A (Before the first treatment, covering fees, expected visit numbers, and costs the plan may leave unpaid): Informed consent includes financial information, so the client must understand fees, expected visits and the limits of coverage before agreeing to treatment. Waiting until the twelfth visit means the client cannot plan for the shortfall. The insurer decides payment but the physiotherapist is responsible for explaining fees. Waiting until the third visit lets treatment proceed without informed financial consent.
  61. The correct answer is C (Reassess the patient against the triage criteria and keep the place unless findings show greater urgency): Fair triage uses the same criteria for every client, and this patient is stable and progressing, so a request from a referrer alone does not change priority. Reassessing against the criteria keeps the process consistent and still allows earlier access if new findings raise urgency. Moving the patient up or displacing another client is unfair to those waiting.
  62. The correct answer is C (Lower stimulation with a quiet room, one familiar person, short sessions and a calm low voice): During post-traumatic amnesia, agitation is usually driven by overstimulation and confusion, so reducing noise, people and demands and keeping sessions short is the first-line approach. Repeated orientation drills increase frustration, physical holding escalates agitation and risk, and adding staff and demands raises the stimulation that triggers it.
  63. The correct answer is A (Gown, then mask, then eye protection, then gloves pulled over the cuffs of the gown): The accepted sequence is gown, mask or respirator, eye protection, then gloves, with gloves covering the gown cuffs so skin is not exposed at the wrist. Putting gloves on early means they may be contaminated while the other items are adjusted. Hand hygiene before donning is also part of the routine.
  64. The correct answer is C (Postpone vigorous activity, limit sessions to gentle supervised movement, and consult the oncology team about limits): A platelet count this low raises bleeding risk and a low neutrophil count raises infection risk, so vigorous activity is deferred while gentle supervised movement can still maintain function, guided by the oncology team. Cancelling everything gives up safe function maintenance. Moderate cycling is too demanding at these counts, whatever the long-term immune benefits. Resistance work still carries bleeding and strain risk with platelets this low.
  65. The correct answer is D (Step back one resistance level and progress gradually, since the flare outlasted the next morning): After the band change, exercise pain climbed to 7 and 8 out of 10 and next-morning pain rose to 5 and 6, so the tendon is not tolerating the load. Stepping back one level and progressing more slowly respects the pain monitoring model. Keeping the red band ignores the pattern, and waiting for zero pain or switching to full-effort overhead holds is not needed.
  66. The correct answer is A (Compare the recommendation with her own assessment and adjust it, because she remains accountable): AI tools can support planning, but the physiotherapist remains accountable for assessing the client and for every clinical decision. A fall with dizziness calls for assessment before loaded exercise. Telling the client about the tool is good practice but does not replace assessment. A co-signature does not transfer accountability. Weekly review lets problems appear after exposure to an unsafe program.
  67. The correct answer is B (Build a goal around attending the wedding, using pacing, rest breaks and a wheelchair): Palliative rehabilitation focuses on goals that matter to the person within a limited prognosis, so a wedding goal supported by pacing and equipment is realistic. Aiming to restore endurance is unrealistic with advanced disease, while passive-only care and deferring to oncology ignore her stated priority.
  68. The correct answer is A (Activity modifications, rest breaks, and signs to report, without unrelated diagnostic or treatment details): Even with consent, the physiotherapist shares only what the school needs to support her safely: practical changes, rest breaks and warning signs. The full report discloses more than needed. Excluding her from gym is unnecessary and harms participation, and written communication with consent is allowed.
  69. The correct answer is B (Keep her seated or reclined as breathing allows, recheck vitals, and call the medical team now): The table shows a fall in blood pressure from 130/76 to 92/58 as her workload rose, with symptoms, which is an exercise stop criterion and a sign of possible cardiac compromise. Keeping her seated or reclined as her breathing allows, rather than flat, avoids loading a failing heart while the medical team is called and vitals are rechecked. A cool-down walk or restarting exercise would stress a heart that is already failing to maintain pressure.
  70. The correct answer is C (Seek help from her physician and limit practice while impaired, because client safety comes first): A physiotherapist who may be impaired must seek appropriate help and protect clients by limiting or stopping practice until safe. Tapering alone without medical support is unsafe and does not address the current practice risk. A colleague reviewing notes cannot correct impaired judgment during treatment. Waiting until the week ends prolongs the risk to clients.
  71. The correct answer is D (Withhold exercise and notify the surgical team immediately, because findings suggest evolving compartment syndrome): Pain out of proportion that rises with passive stretch and a tense compartment are early signs of compartment syndrome, a surgical emergency. Present pulses do not exclude it, since pulse loss is a late sign. Elevation, cold and further exercise can mask or worsen ischemia, and delay risks permanent muscle and nerve damage.
  72. The correct answer is D (Serratus anterior, because it holds the scapula against the thorax during forward pressing): Serratus anterior protracts the scapula and holds it flat on the rib cage, so its weakness produces winging that appears when pressing forward. Rhomboid weakness tends to show winging at rest with the scapula moving away laterally, lower trapezius weakness affects scapular depression, and supraspinatus does not control scapular position against the thorax.
  73. The correct answer is C (Confirm in writing that her own policy covers private home visits before seeing clients): Employer coverage is tied to employment duties, so private work needs its own coverage, confirmed in writing before the initial visit. A hospital manager's verbal approval does not extend the hospital's policy to private clients. Buying a policy after treating the initial client leaves that visit uninsured and conflicts with the college's expectation of continuous coverage. The hospital policy covers only her employment duties.
  74. The correct answer is B (Use the rating of perceived exertion scale with symptom monitoring instead of a target heart rate): Beta-blockers blunt the heart rate response, so heart rate targets underestimate effort. Perceived exertion combined with symptom monitoring gives a valid guide. A heart rate reserve target still depends on a heart rate that the drug suppresses. Age-predicted formulas ignore the medication. Step counts show volume, not how hard he is working.
  75. The correct answer is B (Offer short, calm mobility sessions with reorientation and family present, because activity helps reduce delirium): Early mobility and reorientation are part of non-drug delirium management and are safe when the client is stable. Delaying all activity removes a key treatment, restraints can worsen agitation and injury, and a demanding session is poorly tolerated by a delirious client.
  76. The correct answer is A (Avoid raising it above shoulder height or lifting heavy loads until the device clinic clears him): New leads are not yet fixed in the heart, so overhead movement and heavy lifting on the implant side are limited for a period set by the device clinic. Full immobilization causes shoulder stiffness and is not advised. Normal exercise does not disrupt pacing, and no restriction at all ignores lead dislodgement risk.
  77. The correct answer is C (Summarize the main points in her own words and ask whether anything was missed): A short summary in her own words checks accuracy, shows she was heard and lets her add anything missing. Restating only the knee findings ignores work and family concerns that may affect recovery. More open questions gather information but do not confirm that the physiotherapist's understanding is accurate. Reflecting feelings shows empathy yet does not check the content she gave.
  78. The correct answer is C (Edit the text to record the dizziness and care actually given, as notes must be accurate): The clinical record must describe what happened at the visit, so an adverse response and omitted education have to be written in, not left to a template default. A verbal report, management approval or a delayed entry does not make the note accurate when it is signed.
  79. The correct answer is D (He is on postoperative day three after a total knee arthroplasty and is receiving anticoagulant prophylaxis): Background gives the context needed to interpret the problem, such as the surgery, timing and relevant treatment. The warm swollen calf is the Situation, the concern about a clot is the Assessment, and the request for bed rest and review is the Recommendation.
  80. The correct answer is A (Neutral position, because it keeps carpal tunnel volume largest and median nerve pressure lowest): Pressure inside the carpal tunnel is lowest with the wrist near neutral and rises with both flexion and extension. A neutral night splint therefore reduces nocturnal symptoms. Positions of flexion, extension or deviation narrow the tunnel and increase median nerve pressure.
  81. The correct answer is D (Within the normal range, with reassessment if he is not walking independently by 18 months): Independent walking is expected between about 9 and 15 months, and absence by 18 months prompts further assessment. A child who is cruising, pulling to stand and meeting other milestones is progressing normally. Baby walkers are discouraged because they do not speed walking and carry injury risk.
  82. The correct answer is A (The cubital tunnel at the elbow, because sustained flexion narrows it and stretches the nerve): Symptoms provoked by sustained elbow flexion, with dorsal ulnar hand numbness, localize the lesion to the cubital tunnel, since the dorsal cutaneous branch leaves the nerve proximal to the wrist. Guyon canal compression spares dorsal sensation. Neck or thoracic outlet sources would be provoked by cervical or shoulder positions, not elbow flexion.
  83. The correct answer is D (Judge benefit as doubtful, because the difference falls below the threshold clients perceive as meaningful): Statistical significance shows a difference is unlikely to be chance, but it does not show the difference matters to clients. A 4 point gain is well under the 10 point threshold, so the clinical benefit is doubtful. The study should not be discarded outright, and baseline score does not rescue the effect size.
  84. The correct answer is C (Errorless learning with a written, illustrated checklist and the same routine practised at fixed times): People with significant memory impairment learn best through errorless, repetitive practice supported by external aids and a consistent routine, because they cannot reliably detect and unlearn mistakes. Trial-and-error and single verbal explanations rely on explicit memory she lacks, and reminders alone do not teach the actual movements.
  85. The correct answer is A (A mechanical lift with the sling selected for the resident and two trained staff): A resident who cannot bear weight or cooperate needs a full mechanical lift with an appropriate sling, which protects both the resident and the staff from injury. Manual lifts remain a hazard even with experienced staff. Stand-assist devices and sliding boards require some weight bearing or trunk control that this resident lacks.
  86. The correct answer is A (1 MHz in continuous mode, because lower frequency penetrates deeper and continuous output heats tissue): Ultrasound at 1 MHz penetrates to roughly 3 to 5 cm, while 3 MHz is absorbed within the first 1 to 2 cm. A thermal effect needs continuous output, since pulsing reduces the average intensity and heating. The other choices pair the wrong frequency or the wrong mode with the goal.
  87. The correct answer is C (Ultralight rigid frame with the rear axle moved forward, to reduce the shoulder load of propulsion): A lightweight rigid frame with the axle set forward reduces rolling resistance and lets him push with a longer, smoother stroke, lowering shoulder load, and his trunk control and independence make the reduced rearward stability acceptable. A rearward axle favours stability for new users, a heavy folding chair keeps the load high, and a power chair is not needed for a young, independent man.
  88. The correct answer is D (Tilt the head toward the left ear while turning the chin toward the right shoulder): The sternocleidomastoid tilts the head to its own side and rotates it to the opposite side, so lengthening the right muscle needs left lateral flexion combined with right rotation. The first wrong pattern repeats the shortened posture, and the other two lengthen only one of the two muscle actions.
  89. The correct answer is D (Hip joint osteoarthritis, supported by painful limited flexion and internal rotation): Groin pain with limited, painful flexion and internal rotation is the typical hip osteoarthritis pattern, and negative sacroiliac provocation tests argue against a pelvic source. Buttock pain with transfers can come from either joint but is less specific. Facet and piriformis sources would not limit hip internal rotation at the joint.
  90. The correct answer is B (Acknowledge power imbalances and adapt care collaboratively, because the client defines whether care feels safe): Cultural safety focuses on power, trust and the client's own experience of care rather than on the clinician's knowledge of a culture. Treating everyone identically ignores differing barriers and past harms. Learning cultural facts helps but can lead to stereotyping, and avoiding the topic leaves the client's concern unaddressed.
  91. The correct answer is B (After touching the first resident or the walker, and before initial contact with the second): The four moments include before initial contact with a client or their environment and after contact with a client or their environment. Both apply here: hands are cleaned after leaving the first resident's surroundings, including the walker, and again before touching the second resident. Waiting until a body fluid exposure or until the second resident is seated misses required moments.
  92. The correct answer is A (Cervicogenic contribution involving C1 and C2, because flexion-rotation range is restricted on the symptomatic side): The flexion-rotation test isolates C1 and C2 rotation, and a range under about 32 degrees or a clear side-to-side difference suggests an upper cervical contribution to headache. Normal values are about 44 degrees. The test does not assess C5 and C6, and a restricted range alone does not indicate vascular compromise.
  93. The correct answer is B (Right motor level C7 and left motor level C8, using the grade 3 rule): On the right, the C7 muscle is grade 4 with normal muscles above, while the C8 muscle is only grade 2, so the level is C7. On the left, the C8 muscle is grade 3 with normal strength above it, and T1 is grade 0, so the level is C8. The lowest normal muscle or any trace contraction is not the criterion.
  94. The correct answer is C (New numbness in the saddle area or new difficulty controlling bladder or bowel function): Saddle numbness and bladder or bowel dysfunction are red flags for cauda equina syndrome, which is a surgical emergency. The other symptoms fit his known mechanical pattern and can be managed with the self-care steps in his plan, with rebooking if they persist.
  95. The correct answer is B (Full name and date of birth, checked against the wristband and asked of the patient): Two independent identifiers that belong to the person, such as full name and date of birth, should be checked against the wristband, preferably by asking the patient to state them. Room numbers and the whiteboard can be wrong when patients move. A diagnosis or a physician name can be shared between patients, and a shared surname makes the full name especially important.
  96. The correct answer is C (Stage 3, mild to moderate bilateral disease with postural instability while remaining physically independent): Stage 3 is bilateral disease with some postural instability and physical independence. Stage 2 is bilateral but without balance impairment, stage 4 involves severe disability with walking still possible, and stage 5 is wheelchair or bed bound. Her abnormal pull test places her at stage 3.
  97. The correct answer is C (Stay with him, assess risk immediacy, arrange urgent crisis support, and share what is needed): A disclosed plan with means indicates serious risk to his life, which justifies limited disclosure to protect him, beginning with staying with him, assessing immediacy and arranging urgent help. A crisis line number and an earlier visit leave him unassessed in the meantime. Asking him to dispose of the medications relies on him alone. Contacting his spouse may help, but it does not replace direct risk assessment.
  98. The correct answer is B (Ask directly about thoughts of self-harm, then connect her with her physician or a crisis service): Hopeless statements warrant a direct, caring question about self-harm, since asking does not increase risk and allows safety steps to start. Linking her to her physician or a crisis service ensures timely follow-up. A general mood conversation, a questionnaire completed at home, or reassurance about pain all leave the immediate safety question unasked, so possible risk stays unassessed.
  99. The correct answer is A (Angle the chair 30 to 45 degrees, remove the near armrest, and bridge the gap): An angle of 30 to 45 degrees with the near armrest removed lets him shift his hips along a short, supported bridge with minimal trunk rotation. A parallel chair with the armrest on blocks the path, a right-angle set-up lengthens the distance and demands more trunk and arm power than he has, and leaving the armrest on leaves a barrier.
  100. The correct answer is A (Monitor heart rate, blood pressure and vital capacity because autonomic instability and respiratory weakness may progress): Guillain-Barre syndrome can progress to respiratory muscle failure and unstable blood pressure and heart rate, so these must be watched during any activity. High-intensity exercise risks overwork weakness, and aggressive end-range stretching can injure flaccid muscle. Complete rest is not needed, since gentle, supervised activity is appropriate.
  101. The correct answer is D (Three or more positive tests, because single tests are unreliable and a cluster is more accurate): Individual provocation tests have limited accuracy, so clinicians use a cluster, and three or more positive tests out of five with lumbar sources excluded supports a sacroiliac joint source. One or two positives are not enough to implicate the joint. Requiring all five is more demanding than the evidence supports and would miss many true cases.

Practice Test 2 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is B (Review the program verbally with large-print drawings, then use teach-back to confirm understanding): Adherence fell from five days to one while he struggled to read, so the format is a likely barrier. Demonstrating, using large clear drawings and checking understanding by teach-back addresses both vision and comprehension. Resending the same text, shortening it without checking or adding a checklist still depends on reading small print.
  2. The correct answer is B (Stop the walk, seat him, and reassess symptoms and heart rate before deciding on next steps): His heart rate passed the prescribed limit and he now has new symptoms, so exercise should stop and he should rest while the physiotherapist reassesses, with emergency steps ready. Slowing down or finishing the session continues exertion beyond the limit. Standing arm exercises keep a load on a client who needs rest.
  3. The correct answer is A (A lift rated for his weight with a bariatric sling, because load limits protect both parties): Equipment must be rated above the client's weight, and a properly sized bariatric sling prevents sling failure and skin injury. Standard devices may exceed their safe working load. Manual lifting by a group is unsafe for staff and the client, and a client who cannot bear weight cannot use a sit to stand device.
  4. The correct answer is B (Continue supported recovery until dyspnea settles, then restart at a lower intensity, because symptoms remain high): A Borg score of 7 with accessory muscle use and a heart rate of 118 means he has not recovered, so he should rest in a forward-lean position with pursed-lip breathing and restart at a reduced load. Immediate resumption at the same load risks greater desaturation. Leaning forward helps ventilation by fixing the shoulder girdle, so lying flat would worsen breathlessness. One distressing bout does not make him unsuitable for the program.
  5. The correct answer is A (Give one short, simple instruction at a time, matched with a gesture or demonstration): Working memory and language processing are reduced in moderate dementia, so one-step cues paired with a gesture or demonstration reduce overload. A calm tone helps, but a two-step instruction still exceeds what she can hold in mind. Detailed explanations add more to process, and a written card depends on reading and comprehension skills that are also impaired.
  6. The correct answer is A (Part practice of the forward trunk lean, then reintegration into the whole sit-to-stand task): His failure is limited to one phase, the forward weight shift, so isolating that component and then reintegrating it into the full task is the efficient approach. Whole-task repetition alone does not target the missing component, practising the already-intact lifting phase misses the problem, and passive lifting removes the active learning.
  7. The correct answer is C (Ask him to state his full name and birth date and compare them with the chart): Two clients with similar names is a recognized identification risk, and a person who answers to a called name has not confirmed who he is. Asking him to state his own full name and birth date and comparing them with the chart is a reliable two-identifier check. A yes or no question can draw a nod from someone who has not understood, and matching the schedule to the name he answered to repeats the same weak assumption. Starting the program before identity is confirmed risks giving the wrong client the wrong care.
  8. The correct answer is D (Call emergency medical services for transport to hospital, because he may have bacterial meningitis): Fever, painful neck flexion, photophobia and drowsiness together suggest bacterial meningitis, which can deteriorate within hours. A drowsy client should not travel by private car, so emergency medical services are called and the physiotherapist stays with him and reports the findings. A physical exam of the cervical spine or a routine appointment would delay life-saving antibiotics.
  9. The correct answer is A (Progressive resistance training with impact activities such as stair climbing, because bone adapts to loading): Bone responds to loads that are higher in magnitude or rate than usual, so progressive resistance work and impact activity give the strongest stimulus. Swimming and cycling are good for fitness but load bone little. Stretching does not load bone, and extra walking duration adds little beyond what she already does.
  10. The correct answer is A (Stationary cycling and seated resistance work, with foot inspection before and after each session): Loss of protective sensation means repetitive impact or friction can cause an unnoticed ulcer, so non-weight-bearing or seated activity with a foot check around every session is safest at the start. Weekly clinic checks are too infrequent to catch a new pressure area, since he cannot feel one developing. Long steep treadmill walking and daily jogging apply high repeated load to insensate feet, even with good shoes.
  11. The correct answer is B (Pause the treatment calmly, adjust the technique with the student, and hold a fuller debrief afterwards): Patient safety takes priority, and the harm from the technique is ongoing, so the supervisor acts during the treatment while keeping the correction respectful. Waiting for a later debrief leaves the shoulder at risk. Taking over with a public explanation or filing an incident report overreacts to a correctable teaching moment.
  12. The correct answer is C (Treat it as elevated and add pain education, because 30 or more is clinically relevant): A Pain Catastrophizing Scale score of 30 or more is commonly regarded as clinically relevant, and her stated fear matches that result. Pain education and graded activity target catastrophic beliefs and sit within physiotherapy practice. Limiting movement reinforces fear, and the score reflects a psychological response rather than evidence of tissue damage. Deferring physiotherapy delays helpful care, and calling the score ordinary concern ignores the cutoff.
  13. The correct answer is B (The client judges whether care felt respectful, so the physiotherapist examines power imbalances and personal bias): Cultural safety is defined by the experience of the person receiving care and requires the clinician to reflect on power, privilege and bias. Learning facts about a culture describes cultural awareness or competence and risks stereotyping. Identical treatment ignores differences in history and access, and asking the elder to teach her culture shifts the work onto her.
  14. The correct answer is A (Encourage continued coughing while staying beside him to watch for worsening airway obstruction and reassure him): A person who can cough forcefully and speak has a mild obstruction, and their own cough is the most effective way to clear it. Thrusts and blows are reserved for a severe blockage where coughing, speech and breathing fail. Blind finger sweeps can push food deeper. Staying close allows quick action if the cough weakens or he cannot speak.
  15. The correct answer is C (Tell the client promptly what happened, express regret, and outline the plan to limit harm): Clients are entitled to prompt, honest information about events that affect their care, along with an expression of regret and a plan for what happens next. Here the swelling shows harm occurred, so the care plan must change to limit it. Apologizing while leaving the exercises as they are fails that duty. Waiting for a full review delays information the client needs, and handing the message to someone else in writing undermines trust.
  16. The correct answer is D (Stop pulling on the arm, teach safe handling at the trunk, and keep passive flexion pain-free): Traction through a flaccid arm and forced passive range beyond the pain-free arc stretch the unsupported capsule and cause subluxation pain, so the first step is to remove those forces and teach staff and family safe handling. Strengthening a flaccid cuff is not possible, and sustained immobilization or more pulley work adds stiffness or further stress.
  17. The correct answer is B (Refer for emergency medical assessment because these findings suggest cauda equina syndrome): Urinary retention, saddle numbness and bilateral leg weakness are classic red flags for cauda equina syndrome, which needs emergency decompression to protect bladder, bowel and sexual function. A scheduled MRI or a urology visit within the week would delay surgery beyond the critical window. A urine test treats the urinary complaint alone and misses the neurological cause.
  18. The correct answer is B (Apprehension eases and more rotation is tolerated, because the force supports the anterior restraints): The relocation test is positive when a posteriorly directed force reduces apprehension, because it holds the humeral head back and unloads the lax anterior capsule and labrum. Increased pain over the top of the shoulder would point toward cuff or subacromial irritation instead. Worsening apprehension would not fit a force that supports the anterior structures.
  19. The correct answer is A (Saddle numbness with urinary hesitancy and bilateral leg weakness, suggesting possible cauda equina compression): Saddle anesthesia, bladder change and bilateral weakness point to cauda equina syndrome, which needs urgent medical referral and excludes manipulation. Unilateral radicular signs are a precaution that requires careful technique selection, not an absolute contraindication. Brief morning stiffness and sitting-related pain are common mechanical findings.
  20. The correct answer is B (Contact his physician promptly for further investigation, because the pattern suggests possible malignancy): A cancer history, unremitting night pain and unexplained weight loss are red flags for spinal metastasis. These require prompt medical referral before any exercise trial. Treating first with positional testing, graded activity or mobilization delays diagnosis and could be harmful.
  21. The correct answer is A (75 percent, which is 5 percentage points below the clinic's target of 80 percent): Goal attainment is 60 divided by 80, which equals 75 percent. The target is 80 percent, so the clinic is 5 percentage points short, and being near the target does not meet it. The share who did not meet their goal is a different measure, and dividing discharges by successes inverts the ratio and gives a figure above 100 percent.
  22. The correct answer is C (Greater trochanteric pain syndrome involving the gluteal tendons, which causes lateral hip pain with side-lying): Lateral hip pain, tenderness at the greater trochanter and pain lying on the side point to gluteal tendinopathy, now grouped under greater trochanteric pain syndrome. The full painless hip rotation argues against intra-articular disease such as osteoarthritis, impingement or a stress fracture. Radiculopathy would add neurological signs that are not described.
  23. The correct answer is B (The 8-point gain falls short of the 9-point threshold, so clinical improvement is not yet shown): The change is 46 minus 38, which equals 8 points, one point below the stated 9-point threshold, so the improvement is not yet clearly meaningful. Being above the scale midpoint or improving at each visit does not establish that the change exceeds the threshold. The week 6 gain is only 6 points and also below it.
  24. The correct answer is B (To allow an emergency response and reconnection if the video link fails or the client deteriorates): A virtual care plan must include a way to reach the client and to direct help if the connection drops or the client becomes unwell. Identity checks, billing and follow-up decisions are separate steps with their own information. Emergency details are collected before the session because they cannot be obtained afterward in a crisis.
  25. The correct answer is C (A near miss, reported through the clinic's incident system so contributing factors can be reviewed): A near miss is an event that could have caused harm but was caught before reaching the client. Reporting near misses lets the clinic find system causes such as dial handling between clients. Disclosure to the client is for events that reach them, and keeping it personal removes the learning the team needs.
  26. The correct answer is C (Accept only tasks she can safely perform, seek pediatric mentoring, and decline tasks beyond her competence): Registrants must practise within their competence, which can be extended with education, support and supervision while declining what remains unsafe. Registration covers the profession and is not limited by age group. Employer staffing needs do not shift the duty to recognize personal limits.
  27. The correct answer is A (Remove the pillows and place a shoulder roll so the neck stays slightly extended): Anterior neck burns shorten with healing, and a flexed posture promotes a flexion contracture. A shoulder roll with no pillow keeps the neck in slight extension and opposes that pull. Flexion, fixed rotation or simple neutral alignment all leave the burned skin shortened or fail to counter the contracture force.
  28. The correct answer is D (Between 113 and 132 beats per minute, calculated from his measured peak): Sixty percent of 188 is about 113 and seventy percent is about 132, so the target range is 113 to 132 beats per minute. Using a measured peak is preferred in children with heart disease because age-based formulas are unreliable. The other ranges use the wrong percentages or the wrong peak.
  29. The correct answer is D (Avoid direct ice, use another approach such as elevation and compression, and inspect the skin): A history of cold urticaria is a contraindication to cold application, so another method of managing swelling is chosen, and elevation and compression fit current acute soft tissue guidance. Insulating the ice, shortening the exposure or switching to cool water still cools the skin and can trigger a reaction in a child who has already had welts.
  30. The correct answer is B (Defer pool entry until the incision has fully healed and the surgeon agrees): An incomplete, scabbed incision is an infection portal, and pool water cannot be assumed to be clean. Entry should wait until the wound is fully closed and the surgeon has cleared it. Dressings leak, brief exposure is still risky, and staple removal alone does not mean the skin is healed.
  31. The correct answer is B (Complete the PAR-Q+ follow-up questions, and obtain physician clearance if the follow-up responses require it): A yes to the chest pain question directs the person to the PAR-Q+ follow-up questions, and those responses determine whether medical clearance is needed. Starting exercise anyway skips the screen. A maximal test is not a physiotherapy screening step, and a signed consent does not remove the duty to screen.
  32. The correct answer is A (Identify her limits, seek mentorship in the area, and supervise within her competence): Practising within one's competence includes recognizing limits and getting guidance, which also models good practice for the student and keeps the placement available. A single evening of reading does not close a real experience gap. Relying on the student reverses the supervisory role. Handing the placement away is cautious but gives up a chance to build skills with support that mentorship provides.
  33. The correct answer is D (Measure blood pressure lying and after standing, because dizziness on rising suggests orthostatic hypotension): Dizziness on standing in the context of two falls points toward a cardiovascular cause, and orthostatic blood pressure is quick, safe and changes the plan. Her TUG and Berg are near normal so more balance testing or hazard review would not address the likely cause. If blood pressure drops, she should be referred to her physician before exercise progression.
  34. The correct answer is D (Bill the actual time provided and raise the practice with the manager, recording the discussion): Invoices must reflect the services and time actually delivered, and billing for time not provided is misrepresentation that breaches professional standards. Because the graduate knows the practice is happening, raising it with the manager and recording that she did so is the appropriate response. Going straight to the college skips the clinic's own process, a private talk alone leaves the practice in place, and staying silent lets inaccurate billing continue.
  35. The correct answer is C (Advise him not to practise, document the response, and inform the physician of the symptom return): Symptoms provoked by light exertion mean he is not ready to progress, even with a note written before this finding. The physician should know so the clearance can be reconsidered. Allowing practice or repeating the trial ignores the symptom response, and complete rest until symptom free is no longer the usual approach.
  36. The correct answer is D (S1, which also supplies the gastrocnemius and soleus that are weak on heel raises): The Achilles reflex is mediated mainly by S1, the same root that supplies the plantarflexors. The patellar reflex is L3 and L4, and dorsiflexion is mainly L4 and L5. Together the weak heel raises and reduced reflex support an S1 problem.
  37. The correct answer is D (Upper limb tension test A, because it is one of the four cluster items): The cervical radiculopathy cluster contains upper limb tension test A, reduced cervical rotation toward the involved side, a positive distraction test and Spurling's test. Upper limb tension test B is a related test but is not one of the four items. The shoulder abduction relief sign is a helpful finding that is not in the cluster, and the Hoffmann sign screens for upper motor neuron signs rather than nerve root irritation.
  38. The correct answer is B (Privately describe the observed behaviour and its effect soon afterwards, and invite her view): Effective feedback is specific, about observable behaviour and its impact, given privately and promptly, and it invites a two-way conversation. General impressions are hard to act on, and waiting for months lets the problem continue and weakens the link to events. Giving orders and a deadline without asking for her view treats the colleague as a subordinate and invites defensiveness rather than change.
  39. The correct answer is C (It lacks findings, interventions and the client's response, so care cannot be reconstructed): The purpose of a record is to show assessment findings, what was done and the response, so that care can be understood later. Common abbreviations are acceptable if standard, fault opinions do not belong in clinical notes, and there is no minimum paragraph length.
  40. The correct answer is D (Active cycle of breathing technique, because breathing control periods alternate with deep breaths and huffing): Active cycle of breathing alternates breathing control, thoracic expansion exercises and the forced expiration technique, so the relaxed breathing periods limit bronchospasm and fatigue. Repeated forced coughing increases dyspnea and can collapse airways. Percussion and head-down drainage do not include an active rest component and place more burden on a fatigued patient.
  41. The correct answer is C (Selection bias, because clients who chose the program may differ in motivation or health): When participants pick their own group, the groups can differ before treatment starts, so a difference in outcome may reflect who chose the program. The other biases are real but are not suggested by the way groups were formed in this study. Random allocation is the design feature that protects against this problem.
  42. The correct answer is C (Stop using the adhesive pads, document the reaction, and switch to a hypoallergenic electrode option): A reaction that follows the outline of the pad and worsens across sessions points to contact dermatitis from the adhesive or gel, so the offending product is stopped, the reaction documented, and a hypoallergenic alternative chosen. Moisturizer, alcohol cleaning or small shifts of the same pad keep the allergen on the skin and can add irritation, so the cause is never removed.
  43. The correct answer is D (A positive scapular assistance test, suggesting scapular dyskinesis contributes to the symptoms): Manually assisting scapular upward rotation and posterior tilt during elevation and finding that pain eases is the scapular assistance test, which implicates altered scapular motion in the symptoms. The retraction test applies a different manoeuvre and measures strength, the lateral slide test measures static position, and Hawkins-Kennedy uses passive shoulder internal rotation.
  44. The correct answer is D (Withhold exercise today and contact her physician or heart failure team the same day): Rapid weight gain, new edema and orthopnea suggest fluid overload, which needs same-day medical review before exercise. A normal saturation does not exclude decompensation. Compression or fluid restriction without assessment delays care and may be unsafe.
  45. The correct answer is B (Use protected weight bearing with a walker, avoid resisted hip work, and call the oncologist): Worsening load-related and night pain with cortical thinning signals an impending pathological fracture. Weight bearing is protected, resisted exercise that loads the femur is avoided, and the oncologist is contacted promptly for assessment. Routine follow-up or waiting for a scheduled appointment delays time-sensitive care, and resisted strengthening raises the fracture risk.
  46. The correct answer is A (Warm water near 40°C for 4 minutes, alternating with cold water for 1 minute): A contrast bath uses a longer warm phase and a short cold phase, commonly a 4 to 1 ratio, with warm water around 38 to 43°C. Reversing the ratio makes the treatment mainly a cold bath, and equal periods lose the warm emphasis. Water near 47°C risks burns and ice water is too cold for the hand.
  47. The correct answer is A (A rating of 11 to 14, described as light to somewhat hard on the scale): Ratings of 11 to 14 span light to somewhat hard effort and are the range usually prescribed in cardiac rehabilitation when heart rate cannot guide intensity. Beta blockers blunt heart rate, so a percentage of maximum heart rate would misjudge her effort. Ratings of 15 and above are too hard for early rehabilitation, while ratings of 11 or below give little training stimulus.
  48. The correct answer is D (Highlight the rising falls beside the balance scores and ask the team to review them first): The table shows balance and transfers improving while falls rose from 0 to 2, so a test score alone does not show she is safe when unsupervised. The team should look at when and why she fell before discharge. Dismissing the falls, doubting the measure or changing the aid without evidence does not address the cause.
  49. The correct answer is A (Reassess the dizzy client personally and remove that client from the delegated class until cleared): New dizziness means the client's status has changed and is no longer predictable, so the task is not suitable for delegation until a physiotherapist reassesses. Asking the assistant to judge fitness to join, or to monitor the client, hands over a clinical decision that belongs to the physiotherapist. The other five stable clients can stay in the class.
  50. The correct answer is C (Ten years from the last entry, because the college standard counts from final documented contact): The college standard requires adult records to be kept for ten years from the last entry, so a chart last updated six years ago must still be retained. Seven years is a common business-records period but does not govern clinical records. Counting from discharge or the final invoice misstates the starting point, which is the last documented entry.
  51. The correct answer is A (Stop the test and have him sit, because systolic pressure fell as workload increased): His systolic pressure rose to 168 mmHg at 5 METs then fell by 16 mmHg at 6 METs while workload increased. A drop of more than 10 mmHg despite an increase in workload is a recognized criterion to stop an exercise test even without symptoms. A heart rate below the age-predicted maximum or a lack of symptoms does not override this finding.
  52. The correct answer is B (Pace tasks across the day and sit during dressing and food preparation to save energy): Energy conservation combines prioritizing, planning, pacing and positioning: spreading tasks through the day and sitting for tasks that can be done seated reduces the total energy cost. Front-loading every task uses up her energy early. Dropping valued roles is a last resort, and adding exertion before her busiest period would increase fatigue.
  53. The correct answer is B (The regional home care coordinator, who determines eligibility and arranges publicly funded in-home rehabilitation visits): Provincial home and community care programs assess eligibility and assign publicly funded visits, so the regional coordinator is the right contact. A family physician can support the referral but does not determine eligibility or fund the visits. A public health unit focuses on population health programs, and a private agency delivers services once they are authorized rather than deciding who qualifies for public funding.
  54. The correct answer is D (Withhold the information and obtain the worker's consent before disclosing to the employer): Health information, including functional restrictions, may be shared with an employer only with the worker's consent or where the law requires it. Urgency for scheduling does not create a legal exception, and even a confirmation of no change discloses information.
  55. The correct answer is B (Soft callus formation, where fibrocartilage bridges the gap but the fracture is not yet stable): Fibrocartilage bridging with persistent tenderness and no radiographic bone bridge marks the soft callus stage that follows the first few days of inflammation. Hard callus appears later when woven bone mineralizes and gives stability. Remodelling begins only after union, over months.
  56. The correct answer is D (Stop the session, keep him lying with the neck supported, and call emergency services immediately): Worsening headache, repeated vomiting, drowsiness and unequal pupils are red flags for intracranial bleeding or raised pressure, a medical emergency requiring immediate transfer by trained responders who can manage his airway and protect the neck. Watching him for half an hour or resting him for an hour wastes critical time. Private transport by a relative leaves him unmonitored if he deteriorates further.
  57. The correct answer is C (Bronchodilator, hypertonic saline, PEP airway clearance, then the treadmill session for aerobic training): The bronchodilator opens the airways and reduces the risk of bronchospasm from hypertonic saline, the saline hydrates and loosens secretions, and airway clearance then moves them before exercise. Giving saline without a bronchodilator first risks wheeze, and exercising before clearing thick secretions makes aerobic work harder. Exercise supports but does not replace the prescribed airway clearance.
  58. The correct answer is D (Stop forced expirations and guide relaxed breathing control upright, because bronchospasm needs to settle first): Wheeze with desaturation during forced expirations suggests bronchospasm, so the first step is to stop the provoking manoeuvre and settle breathing with relaxed breathing control. Head-down positioning and percussion can add to bronchospasm and dyspnea. Deep expansion breaths continue the stress on narrowed airways. Once she has settled, the physiotherapist reassesses and informs the nurse or physician about a bronchodilator.
  59. The correct answer is D (Pause the classes and consult her obstetric provider, since placenta previa with bleeding contraindicates exercise): Placenta previa after 28 weeks with ongoing bleeding is a recognized contraindication to exercise in Canadian prenatal guidance. Buoyancy does not protect against bleeding, and heart rate limits do not address the risk. Changing to a gentler activity such as yoga or walking leaves the same contraindication in place, since the concern is the bleeding placenta and not the type of exercise.
  60. The correct answer is A (Rest the forearm on a lap tray, so the arm weight no longer drags the shoulder): A tray or trough supports the arm so the flaccid shoulder is not pulled down and the capsule is not stretched. Slings immobilize the limb and encourage poor posture and contracture, pulleys load an unstable joint, and pulling on the hand is a common cause of subluxation and pain.
  61. The correct answer is D (Three criteria are met, so septic arthritis is likely and urgent medical assessment is needed): The Kocher criteria are fever above 38.5 degrees Celsius, non-weight-bearing, ESR above 40 mm/h, and white cell count above 12 ×10⁹/L. This girl meets fever, non-weight-bearing and the white cell count, but her ESR of 32 does not qualify, giving three of four. Three criteria carry a high probability of septic arthritis and need urgent medical assessment.
  62. The correct answer is A (He is fixing his shoulder girdle to recruit accessory muscles, which indicates increased work of breathing): Leaning forward with the arms braced fixes the shoulder girdle so the sternocleidomastoid, scalene and pectoral muscles can lift the rib cage. This tripod posture, together with pursed lips and a long smoking history, is a classic sign of increased work of breathing in obstructive lung disease. Rib pain, cardiac filling and chest wall stretching do not explain pursed-lip breathing or this pattern of progressive breathlessness.
  63. The correct answer is D (Stage II, where swelling does not resolve with elevation and tissue fibrosis is developing): Stage II is defined by swelling that no longer reduces with elevation alone, with increasing fibrosis and less pitting. Stage I swelling still resolves overnight and pits easily, and stage 0 has no visible swelling. Stage III brings elephantiasis and skin changes such as papillomas, which this client does not have.
  64. The correct answer is B (Wash with soap and water, allow gentle bleeding, then report it and seek urgent assessment): Immediate washing with soap and water and allowing gentle bleeding reduces contamination without damaging tissue. Prompt reporting and medical assessment matter because post-exposure treatment is time sensitive. Finishing the session first or waiting for the client's results delays that window. Scrubbing or squeezing injures the skin and does not lower the chance of infection.
  65. The correct answer is A (Stop compression and prosthetic training, and report findings to the surgeon the same day): Spreading redness, purulent drainage, warmth, pain and fever indicate a probable wound infection, which in a person with diabetes can progress quickly. Compression and loading should stop and the surgeon should be told promptly. Tightening a wrap, training and applying heat all stress or inflame an infected limb.
  66. The correct answer is D (Hip flexion AROM 100 degrees, TUG 14 seconds, chair stand 8 repetitions, abduction MMT 4-): The visit 3 column gives 100 degrees, 14 seconds, 8 repetitions and 4-. Each wrong entry swaps in one value from an earlier visit, such as a slower walking time, a lower repetition count or a smaller range of motion. A single misread value would misrepresent the client's current status in the legal record.
  67.
