The correct answer is B (Co-create a shorter program tied to an existing daily habit, with a reminder he chooses): The log shows adherence falling each week with barriers of fatigue and forgetting, so the best response is a collaborative, simpler plan linked to a habit with a self-chosen cue. Adding exercises worsens the burden. Warnings and waiting do not remove the barriers.
  68. The correct answer is C (Describe where and why touch will occur, ask permission each time, and invite her to pause): Consent is ongoing and can be withdrawn, so the physiotherapist explains each touch and checks again before every new area. Inviting her to pause returns control to her. Reading facial expression alone is unreliable, since survivors may freeze or hide discomfort. A one-time consent or an uninterrupted procedure does not allow her to say no in the moment.
  69. The correct answer is C (Individual one-hour sessions, four days weekly for four weeks, using large-amplitude movements): LSVT BIG is delivered intensively, with one-to-one one-hour sessions four days a week for four weeks, and it retrains the sense of effort by exaggerating amplitude. Fast small movements reproduce his hypokinesia, and low-frequency group or home programs and heavy resistance work do not match the protocol.
  70. The correct answer is B (Anterior cruciate ligament, because increased forward tibial translation with no firm end point reflects its deficiency): The Lachman test at 20 to 30 degrees detects anterior tibial translation that is increased with a soft end feel in an ACL rupture. The history of a pop and rapid swelling fits. Posterior cruciate injury increases posterior translation, and collateral or meniscal injuries do not usually produce this pattern of forward translation.
  71. The correct answer is D (Hold the belt and guide her in a controlled way onto the nearest surface): With the bed directly behind her, the safest response is to use the belt to guide her in a controlled way onto the bed, which avoids both a fall and a floor recovery. Trying to hold up a falling adult or pulling her upright risks injury to her and to the physiotherapist. The floor is appropriate only when no surface is reachable.
  72. The correct answer is D (Sustained, graded pressure held on the tender point until tenderness eases, usually thirty to ninety seconds): Sustained pressure on a tender trigger point, held until the discomfort eases, is the standard approach for this finding. Deep friction suits tendon or ligament adhesions, percussion is used for secretion clearance or stimulation, and light gliding strokes target circulation rather than a focal taut band.
  73. The correct answer is A (Positive right Trendelenburg sign, indicating right gluteus medius weakness, because the left pelvis drops): In single-leg stance, the abductors of the stance leg hold the opposite pelvis level. A drop of the left pelvis during right stance means the right gluteus medius is failing. The trunk lean is a compensation that shifts the centre of mass over the stance hip and does not make the test negative.
  74. The correct answer is B (Bill accurately and appeal for further authorization with clinical justification, declining the owner's instruction): Misrepresenting treatment as an assessment is fraudulent even if the intent is to help the client. The ethical route is to bill honestly and advocate for more coverage using documented progress and goals. Ending care solely at the limit ignores her clinical need, and silently absorbing costs does not address the owner's improper instruction.
  75. The correct answer is A (Notify the privacy officer and document it, so the client is informed as the law directs): Sending personal health information to the wrong person is a privacy breach even when the recipient says it was deleted. The physiotherapist should report internally, record what happened and make sure the client is told as provincial privacy law and the clinic's breach procedure require, at the earliest reasonable opportunity. Postponing that decision leaves the client unaware, and quietly correcting the mistake removes the clinic's chance to assess harm and meet its duties.
  76. The correct answer is B (Slow lifting and lowering at high load, about three seconds each way, with low repetitions): Heavy slow resistance uses high loads moved slowly through the range, usually about three seconds up and three down, with low repetitions, to build tendon stiffness and capacity. Explosive jumps are a later energy-storage stage, high-repetition light work builds endurance, and long isometric holds are mainly used to reduce pain.
  77. The correct answer is D (Help her lie down with legs elevated and monitor blood pressure until symptoms resolve): Sudden low blood pressure after hard exercise, with pallor and lightheadedness, is post-exercise hypotension made more likely by a diuretic and a vasodilating drug. Lying with legs elevated restores venous return and cerebral perfusion. Continued walking may worsen the pooling, and hypoglycemia or hyperventilation do not explain the low reading.
  78. The correct answer is D (Report her reasonable suspicion promptly to the child protection agency, since proof is not required): Child protection legislation requires anyone, including health professionals, to report reasonable grounds to suspect abuse promptly, and good-faith reports are protected. Waiting for proof or for the parent to act delays protection, and the college is not the body that investigates child welfare.
  79. The correct answer is A (Stop the activity, sit him upright, help him use his reliever, and call 911 if worse): Short phrases, a respiratory rate of 32 and audible wheeze signal a significant exacerbation. Stopping exercise, upright positioning and prompt use of his prescribed reliever are the first steps, with emergency help called if there is no improvement. Delaying medication or lying flat worsens the work of breathing.
  80. The correct answer is D (The gallbladder, with diaphragm irritation referring pain to the shoulder through the phrenic nerve): The phrenic nerve arises from C3 to C5, so diaphragmatic irritation from a gallbladder source is felt at the shoulder tip. Meal-related pain, nausea and right upper abdominal tenderness point to this organ. A facet or tendon source would change with neck or arm movement, and the cardiac pattern is exertional and usually left-sided.
  81. The correct answer is A (Below the cutoff of 48, which meets the usual criterion for ICU-acquired weakness): A Medical Research Council sum score below 48 out of 60 is the commonly used threshold for ICU-acquired weakness. The score does not separate myopathy from polyneuropathy; that needs electrophysiology. Because 44 is under the cutoff, rehabilitation should be tailored to marked weakness.
  82. The correct answer is D (Seek same-day medical assessment, as these signs could indicate infection, and document the call): A hot, swollen joint with fever after surgery raises concern for a surgical site or joint infection, which needs same-day medical assessment rather than a physiotherapy appointment. Recognizing the infection signs but waiting for an appointment within the week still delays time-critical care, and attributing the swelling to overuse misses the fever. The call should be documented.
  83. The correct answer is A (Capacity is presumed and judged per decision, so his understanding of this treatment is checked): Capacity is presumed in adults and is assessed for each specific decision, based on whether the person can understand the information and appreciate the consequences. A diagnosis or a relative's opinion does not remove capacity. A substitute decision-maker is involved only after the physiotherapist finds he lacks capacity for this decision.
  84. The correct answer is C (Record the change with date, time, and signature in the shared chart, and tell the nurse): A change in transfer assistance affects safety right away, so it belongs in the shared record with date, time and signature, and the nurse should hear it directly. A whiteboard is not a legal record. Private notes and a weekly summary leave other staff using outdated instructions for days.
  85. The correct answer is D (Provide high-contrast large-print instructions and review each exercise verbally while demonstrating): Central vision loss makes small detail hard to see, so large, high-contrast print combined with spoken explanation and demonstration uses her remaining senses. Small labels and unsupervised videos depend on detailed vision, and a single verbal description places a heavy load on memory.
  86. The correct answer is D (Alert the physician or emergency team immediately, because pain with passive stretch signals compartment syndrome): Escalating pain despite analgesia, pain on passive stretch of the toes, a tense compartment, and paresthesias indicate acute compartment syndrome, a surgical emergency needing immediate medical action. Delay risks permanent nerve and muscle loss. Elevation can reduce arterial perfusion, and exercise or ice will not relieve the pressure.
  87. The correct answer is B (The largest fall is about 18 percent, which is consistent with exercise-induced bronchoconstriction): The lowest value is 2.30 L against a baseline of 2.80 L, a fall of 0.50 L, or about 18 percent. A fall of 10 percent or more after a standardized challenge is the usual criterion for exercise-induced bronchoconstriction, so a 20 percent requirement is too strict. The 82 percent figure is the fraction of baseline that remains, not the fall. Chest tightness in a known asthmatic with a fall of this size points to airway narrowing rather than a cardiac cause.
  88. The correct answer is D (Pain does not necessarily equal tissue damage, and gradually resuming valued activities is safe): Persistent low back pain often reflects sensitization and fear-related behaviour rather than ongoing tissue damage. Reassurance and graded return to activity reduce disability. Messages linking pain to damage, posture or degeneration reinforce avoidance.
  89. The correct answer is A (Decline, explain medication is a nursing task, and ask her to give it before therapy): Administering medication is outside physiotherapy practice, even when an order exists and a nurse asks. The best step is to explain this politely and ask the nurse to give the dose, then start gait training once it takes effect. Handing the task to an assistant only passes the same out-of-scope act to someone with less authority. Cancelling the whole session is an overreaction when the dose is due in ten minutes.
  90. The correct answer is C (Reduced breath sounds, stony dullness to percussion and decreased tactile fremitus at the left base): Fluid between the pleural layers damps sound conduction, so breath sounds and tactile fremitus are reduced and percussion is stony dull. Bronchial breathing with increased fremitus indicates consolidation, hyperresonance indicates air, and fine crackles point to interstitial disease.
  91. The correct answer is C (Low-load submaximal exercise with rest days, because overwork can speed weakness in partly denervated muscle): Muscle that is partly denervated tolerates heavy loading poorly, and weakness or cramping lasting beyond a day signals overwork. Moderate, submaximal exercise with adequate recovery preserves function without accelerating loss. Complete avoidance of active exercise is not supported, and eccentric overload is especially fatiguing for weakened muscle.
  92. The correct answer is B (Decline to share details without consent, and offer to pass the brother's message to the client): Under Canadian health privacy law, such as PHIPA in Ontario, a capable client controls who receives his health information, and he has declined this. Verifying identity or limiting the detail does not replace consent. Physicians are bound by the same rules, so redirecting the call does not fix the problem.
  93. The correct answer is C (L5, because toe extension and dorsiflexion weakness fit with reduced sensation over the foot dorsum): Weak great toe extension and ankle dorsiflexion with reduced sensation on the top of the foot form the L5 pattern. Normal knee extension and patellar reflex argue against L3 and L4, and normal plantarflexion, Achilles reflex and lateral foot sensation argue against S1.
  94. The correct answer is B (Twenty minutes daily at about 128 beats per minute, eighty percent of his symptom threshold): Standard protocols prescribe sub-threshold aerobic exercise at about 80 percent of the heart rate at which symptoms worsened, here 0.8 times 160, or about 128. Exercising at or near the threshold risks provoking symptoms, and 60 percent is a low dose that gives too little stimulus to promote recovery.
  95. The correct answer is A (Proximal hip and thigh weakness, with calves enlarged by fat and connective tissue replacing muscle): Walking the hands up the thighs is the Gowers maneuver, which compensates for weak hip and knee extensors. Firm, enlarged calves are pseudohypertrophy, where degenerating muscle is replaced by fat and connective tissue, a classic sign of Duchenne muscular dystrophy. Spinal muscular atrophy and inflammatory myopathy also cause proximal weakness, but neither produces firm calves from fatty replacement. Neuropathy causes distal weakness rather than this rising pattern.
  96. The correct answer is B (Volar glide of the carpals, because a convex surface glides opposite to the roll): The proximal carpals are convex and move on the concave radius. In extension they roll dorsally, so the convex-concave rule says they glide volarly, and a volar glide mobilizes toward extension. A dorsal glide would follow the rule for a concave surface, and traction or radial glides do not target the direction of lost extension.
  97. The correct answer is A (A multicomponent program of walking, strength and balance, three times weekly, with simple routines): Exercise that combines walking, strength and balance is supported for improving mobility and reducing falls in people with dementia, and simple repeated routines suit learning limits. Games alone do not target mobility. Range of motion alone is too light, and intervals are not the only effective approach.
  98. The correct answer is B (Do not enter the arrangement, because paying for referrals undermines independent clinical judgment): Fee splitting or payment for referrals is prohibited because it ties the referral decision to money rather than client need. Disclosure, a smaller percentage or a different category of client does not make the payment acceptable. A written agreement only documents the problem.
  99. The correct answer is D (Complete ligament testing and apply the Ottawa rule, because rapid swelling suggests bleeding into the joint): Swelling within an hour suggests hemarthrosis, commonly from cruciate rupture, fracture or patellar dislocation, so a full ligament examination and fracture screening with the Ottawa knee rule come first. Aspiration is outside physiotherapy practice. Strengthening or delayed assessment would risk missing a fracture or a significant ligament injury.

Practice Test 3 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is C (Ask directly about thoughts of suicide, then follow the clinic safety protocol and arrange same-day help): Statements that others would be better off without her are a warning sign of suicide risk, which is common in chronic pain. Asking directly does not increase risk and allows a safety assessment and same-day connection to appropriate help. Delaying action or only reassuring her could leave her unsafe.
  2. The correct answer is D (Medial patellofemoral ligament, because it is the primary restraint to lateral patellar translation in early flexion): The patellar apprehension test passively translates the patella laterally at about 20 to 30 degrees of flexion, and guarding indicates insufficiency of the medial patellofemoral ligament, which is torn in most lateral dislocations. The lateral retinaculum is the structure that tightens on that glide, and the other structures do not restrain lateral patellar glide.
  3. The correct answer is D (Respiratory acidosis with partial metabolic compensation, because CO2 is high and bicarbonate has begun to rise): A low pH with a raised PaCO2 identifies a respiratory acidosis from inadequate ventilation. The raised bicarbonate shows the kidneys are compensating, but the pH is still below normal, so compensation is partial. Metabolic acidosis would show a low bicarbonate, and an alkalosis does not fit a pH of 7.31.
  4. The correct answer is C (Slow the pace to hold breathlessness near 3 to 4, with short bouts and rests): A rating of 7 is well above the moderate range of about 3 to 4 used for chronic lung disease, so intensity is lowered and delivered as interval walking with rests. Pursed-lip breathing helps recovery but does not make a rating of 7 acceptable. A saturation of 90% during exercise does not by itself prohibit aerobic training, so stopping and referring is excessive. Harder bursts would push breathlessness even higher.
  5. The correct answer is A (18 breaths per minute, which falls inside the usual resting adult range of 12 to 20): Nine breaths in 30 seconds doubles to 18 breaths per minute, which sits inside the normal resting adult range of 12 to 20. Reporting the half-minute count as the rate would wrongly suggest slow breathing, and doubling twice would wrongly suggest distress. Healthy older adults do not breathe meaningfully slower at rest, so no age adjustment makes 18 high. The quiet breathing agrees with a normal rate.
  6. The correct answer is B (Poorly controlled diabetes with a recent urinary infection, because bacteria can seed vertebrae through blood): Diabetes with poor glucose control and a recent urinary infection are classic risk factors, since bacteria spread through the bloodstream to the vertebral bodies. Heavy lifting is a mechanical risk and does not explain infection. A steadily rising pain pattern is not specific to infection, and occupational strain does not cause fever.
  7. The correct answer is C (Stage 1, intact skin with non-blanchable redness and no loss of skin thickness): Intact skin with bright red discoloration that does not blanch is stage 1. A deep tissue injury would look maroon or purple, stage 2 involves a break in the epidermis or a blister, and unstageable injuries have a base obscured by slough or eschar. None of those features is present here.
  8. The correct answer is C (Assessment for noninvasive ventilation and assisted cough techniques, because hypoventilation and weak cough are developing): Morning headaches with a falling FVC indicate nocturnal hypoventilation, and a weak cough raises the risk of retained secretions. Noninvasive ventilation and mechanical or manually assisted cough are the evidence-informed responses in ALS. High-load respiratory training and aerobic conditioning do not restore respiratory muscle strength lost to motor neuron degeneration.
  9. The correct answer is B (At minute 12, when her saturation first dropped to 87 percent, below the set limit): The stop criterion is a saturation below 88 percent, and the first such reading is 87 percent at minute 12. Earlier values are lower than resting but still above the limit, and a later recovery does not cancel the instruction to stop once the limit has been crossed.
  10. The correct answer is D (Objective facts, the client's condition, actions taken and people notified, without blame or speculation): The chart is a clinical record, so it should hold factual observations, the client's status, care provided and notifications. The incident report is an administrative quality tool and is kept separate, so it is not referenced or attached. Opinions about cause or fault do not belong in the chart, and leaving out details defeats continuity of care.
  11. The correct answer is B (Use diagonal figure-of-eight turns with greatest tension distally, decreasing proximally, and anchor above the knee): Diagonal figure-of-eight turns spread pressure evenly and avoid a tourniquet effect. Tension is highest at the distal end and falls proximally so fluid moves away from the end of the limb, and the wrap must extend above the knee so it stays in place and does not constrict. Circular turns can act as a tourniquet and the wrap is usually reapplied every few hours.
  12. The correct answer is A (Movement retraining with mirror or verbal feedback to control knee alignment during squatting and stepping tasks): Normal strength with abnormal alignment during function points to a motor control problem, so skill training with feedback is the first priority. Further strengthening adds little when strength is already adequate. Plyometrics demand control she has not yet shown, and stretching does not address the faulty movement pattern.
  13. The correct answer is A (Withhold manipulation and arrange urgent medical assessment, because the findings suggest a serious neurological condition): Bilateral leg numbness with saddle sensory loss is a red flag for cauda equina compromise, which needs emergency medical evaluation. Being authorized to manipulate does not mean it is indicated, and consent cannot make a contraindicated treatment appropriate. Delaying referral or trialling manual therapy risks permanent neurological harm.
  14. The correct answer is C (Progressive isometric hamstring holds in mid range, advancing toward longer muscle lengths as discomfort allows): At 10 days with mild discomfort on resisted testing, the muscle needs graded loading that stays below a provocative level. Isometric work in mid range is tolerable and builds into lengthened positions. Maximal eccentrics, ballistic stretching and sprinting are later stages that risk reinjury if started before strength and pain tolerance allow.
  15. The correct answer is B (Review scheduling, labelling and handover for system weaknesses while supporting the physiotherapist who reported it): A learning culture looks for system contributors such as scheduling, labelling and handover, because individual vigilance alone fails under similar conditions. Supporting the person who reported encourages others to report. Punishment and quiet closure both discourage reporting, and a reminder email does not examine why the process allowed the near miss.
  16. The correct answer is A (Provide what the funding decision reasonably needs and withhold unrelated sensitive notes unless the client agrees): Consent for payment purposes covers information necessary for that purpose, and custodians should disclose no more than needed. Sensitive information not relevant to the funding decision stays protected unless the client agrees to share it. Intake consent does not authorize everything, but it also does not expire after a single request.
  17. The correct answer is C (Adjust his seating to align pelvis, trunk, and head, then confirm it suits the swallow plan): A stable, aligned sitting position supports safe swallowing, and the physiotherapist's skill is in setting that position while the speech-language pathologist leads the feeding assessment. Choosing textures is outside physiotherapy scope. Observing only wastes the joint session, and standing activities during a meal would add a choking risk.
  18. The correct answer is B (Keep him sitting upright, loosen tight clothing or binders and check the catheter for kinks): This is autonomic dysreflexia, and sitting upright lets gravity lower the blood pressure while the noxious trigger, often a full bladder or kinked catheter, is removed. Lying flat raises pressure further. Emergency services are needed if the pressure stays high after these steps, but the immediate measures come first.
  19. The correct answer is D (Stop spinal loading and refer for prompt medical assessment, because a vertebral compression fracture is likely): Long-term corticosteroid use, age over 70, focal spinous tenderness and sudden pain after minor force make a vertebral compression fracture the leading concern. Loading is held and prompt medical assessment arranged so imaging can be done. Referral on a routine timeline while continuing to load risks worsening the fracture. Core work and extension exercise are reasonable only after a fracture has been excluded.
  20. The correct answer is D (Provide night ankle-foot orthoses and a daily stretching and strengthening program to hold the gain): The goal range has been reached and held, so the priority is maintaining it with night orthoses, stretching and strengthening. More casting would push beyond the goal, and stopping all treatment risks relapse as the child grows. A residual grade of 1+ does not justify surgery.
  21. The correct answer is B (Side-flex and rotate the neck toward the painful side, extend gently, then apply light axial compression): Spurling's test narrows the intervertebral foramen by combining extension, side-flexion and rotation toward the symptomatic side before light axial compression. The test stops when familiar arm symptoms appear. Traction and shoulder abduction relief are used to ease radicular symptoms, not provoke them.
  22. The correct answer is C (Dynamic aerobic exercise for 30 to 60 minutes on most days, because it lowers resting pressure): Hypertension Canada recommends dynamic aerobic exercise, such as walking, cycling or swimming, for 30 to 60 minutes on most days to lower resting pressure. Resistance training can be added but is not the main prescription. Handgrip holds and maximal sprinting are not recommended as primary strategies.
  23. The correct answer is A (Inhale slightly deeper than usual, then exhale actively for three to four times as long): PEP therapy uses a slightly larger than normal tidal inhalation followed by an active but unforced exhalation, with an expiratory phase three to four times as long as the inspiration. This holds the airways open and moves secretions toward larger airways. Forceful exhalation can collapse floppy airways, and equal-length shallow breaths do not generate a sustained expiratory pressure benefit.
  24. The correct answer is D (Pause exercise and arrange prompt medical assessment for a possible vertebral fracture, since imaging is needed): Sudden severe thoracic pain with midline tenderness after minor loading in a person with osteoporosis suggests a vertebral compression fracture, which requires imaging and medical care before exercise. Modifying the class, mobilizing, or masking pain with TENS could displace or worsen an unstable fracture.
  25. The correct answer is A (Speak in short phrases, pause for interpretation, and address the client directly): Short segments let the interpreter convey content accurately, and speaking directly to the client keeps the relationship between the two of them. Long speeches lead to summarizing and lost detail, third-person talk undermines rapport, and interpreters must not give clinical advice because that is the physiotherapist's role.
  26. The correct answer is C (Prone with a pillow under the hips and the head end of the bed tipped down): The posterior basal segments drain with the patient prone and head down, using a pillow under the hips for comfort. Left side-lying head-down drains the lateral basal segment, and supine head-down drains the anterior basal segments. Upright leaning forward drains upper lobe segments and does not use gravity to move lower lobe secretions.
  27. The correct answer is A (Explain her capacity limits and propose a waitlist or transfer to a therapist with openings): Practising within personal limits includes recognizing when workload prevents safe, timely and thorough care and saying so. Shortening other sessions or working while overextended lowers the quality of care for everyone. Delegation to an assistant requires appropriate supervision and an acuity-matched plan, which this complex new injury would not meet.
  28. The correct answer is A (Use low-grade mobilization and postural exercise, avoiding thrust techniques because bone fragility raises fracture risk): Long-term corticosteroids and osteoporosis lower bone strength, so thrust and strong end-range loading carry fracture risk. Gentle mobilization and exercise can still reduce stiffness and improve posture. Lowering the force of a thrust does not make it safe, and kyphosis from vertebral change cannot be stretched out.
  29. The correct answer is D (Reassuring education about recovery, with graded activity and active exercise rather than a collar or rest): Fear avoidance, catastrophic beliefs and sleep disruption are risk factors for chronic whiplash disorders. Evidence supports reassurance, activity and exercise rather than collars or prolonged rest. Passive care alone fosters dependence, and imaging is not indicated without red flags or neurological change.
  30. The correct answer is A (Implied consent, because her actions after the explanation show agreement to the examination): Implied consent is inferred from a client's actions after an adequate explanation, such as positioning for the examination. Expressed consent is spoken or written, and she gave neither. Attending an appointment does not authorize every procedure, so the physiotherapist should still document the explanation and her behaviour.
  31. The correct answer is B (Disclose the clinic's financial interest and explain that comparable products can be bought elsewhere): A financial interest in a recommended product is a conflict of interest that the client has a right to know about, along with the freedom to shop elsewhere. A discount or a waiting room sign does not give the client individual, informed disclosure. Clinical judgment alone does not remove the appearance of bias.
  32. The correct answer is B (Arrange a professional interpreter, because accuracy and confidentiality are better protected): Professional interpreters are trained in accuracy, neutrality and confidentiality, which matters most for sensitive topics. A child interpreter may omit or alter information and puts the mother in an uncomfortable position. Apps are not reliable for clinical content, and splitting the language use invites errors.
  33. The correct answer is A (Week 7, because 30 minutes plus six weekly additions of 20 minutes equals 150 minutes): Week 1 is 30 minutes, and each later week adds 20, so week n equals 30 plus 20 times (n minus 1). Setting that equal to 150 gives n equals 7. Week 6 reaches only 130 minutes, and week 5 reaches 110 minutes. Week 8 would reach 170 minutes.
  34. The correct answer is D (Snug around the waist over clothing, gripped from underneath with an underhand hold): A gait belt sits snug at the waist over clothing so it will not slide upward, and an underhand grip lets the physiotherapist support the client's weight without the hand slipping off. An overhand grip can slip when the client drops weight. A belt on the lower chest restricts breathing and can cause injury when pulled. Holding only the buckle gives no secure hold on the belt.
  35. The correct answer is D (None until the worker consents, with the consent stating what may be shared and with whom): Health information about a worker is protected by provincial privacy law and professional standards, and an employer is a third party. Disclosure needs the worker's consent that identifies the information and the recipient. Even attendance and restrictions are personal health information, and payment for care does not transfer ownership of the record.
  36. The correct answer is C (Add loaded neck and shoulder strengthening and graded activity, as control has improved but disability remains): Rotation (55 to 70 degrees) and craniocervical flexion hold (22 to 28 mmHg) have improved, but pain and disability have plateaued since week 4, which suggests the program should progress in load and function and address activity avoidance. Repeating the same low-load exercise adds little, a collar encourages disuse, and passive treatment alone is not supported.
  37. The correct answer is A (Arrange urgent emergency assessment, because these signs suggest a central cause such as stroke): Direction-changing gaze-evoked nystagmus, a normal head impulse test and inability to sit unsupported form the central warning pattern in acute continuous vertigo, especially with vascular risk factors. This needs emergency assessment for posterior circulation stroke. Peripheral inner ear disorders produce nystagmus that keeps one direction and an abnormal head impulse test. Exercises or a routine appointment would delay time-critical care.
  38. The correct answer is A (Call 911 now and stay with him, because unrelieved pain suggests a possible heart attack): Pressure that spreads to the arm with sweating and nausea, and does not ease with rest and nitroglycerin, is treated as a possible myocardial infarction until proven otherwise. Heart and stroke guidance is to call 911 when pain persists after rest and an initial dose, rather than repeating doses and waiting. Taking vital signs is useful but must not delay the call.
Case CHB1

Jayden, a 16-year-old male rugby player, was struck on the side of the head in a tackle 6 days ago. He was removed from play, assessed by a physician, and diagnosed with a sport-related concussion. His headache and fogginess lasted 3 days. Today he reports no symptoms at rest, has attended school normally for 2 days, and asks to return to full practice on Saturday.

  1. The correct answer is C (Light aerobic activity such as walking or stationary cycling, staying below symptom-provoking intensity): Current Canadian guidance begins the graded return with light aerobic activity at an intensity that does not provoke symptoms, once symptoms have settled at rest and school has resumed. Sport-specific drills and contact come in later stages. Prolonged strict rest is no longer recommended after the first day or two.
  2. The correct answer is C (Return to the previous stage for at least 24 hours, because exertion provoked a symptom increase): A symptom increase during a stage means the exertion exceeded his current tolerance, so he steps back to the previous stage for at least a day before trying again. Skipping ahead ignores the provoked symptoms. A full week of rest and a restart from the beginning is more than the protocol requires.
  3. The correct answer is A (Hold contact play until he is fully back at school and medically cleared to return): Return to learning must be complete before return to full sport, and a physician or nurse practitioner must clear contact play. His ongoing need for academic accommodations shows he has not fully recovered. Helmets and mouthguards do not prevent concussion, and a shortened match still exposes him to contact.
  4. The correct answer is B (Explain risks and alternatives, answer her questions, and obtain and document informed consent before needling): Informed consent for a procedure that breaks the skin requires a discussion of the material risks, benefits and alternatives, with the client's decision documented. A request alone, a generic intake form or a screen for contraindications does not meet that standard. Consent is required for any treatment, not only surgery.
  5. The correct answer is A (Community ambulator, because his speed of about 0.83 m/s exceeds the 0.8 m/s threshold): Gait speed is distance divided by time, so 10 m in 12.0 s is about 0.83 m/s. In the commonly used classification, speeds below 0.4 m/s indicate household ambulation, 0.4 to 0.8 m/s limited community ambulation and above 0.8 m/s community ambulation. The earlier visits show the trajectory but the most recent result determines the current category.
  6. The correct answer is A (Explain the purpose, offer a chaperone and shorts for draping, and obtain his own consent): A capable adolescent gives his own consent after a clear explanation, and a chaperone and draping are offered up front to protect his dignity, not held back until he asks. A parent is not automatically required in the room or to sign when the young person understands the examination. Palpation near the pubic ramus is within physiotherapy scope when it is justified and explained.
  7. The correct answer is B (Accept her own consent, because capacity depends on understanding the decision, not a set age): In Canadian provinces, consent depends on capacity, which is judged by whether the person understands the information and the consequences, not by a fixed age. This client explained the benefits and risks accurately, so her own consent is valid. Waiting for a parent or relying on implied consent would not respect her demonstrated capacity.
  8. The correct answer is C (Share the score with his physician with consent, because depression this severe needs medical assessment): A PHQ-9 of 17 falls in the moderately severe range, and low mood is a barrier to recovery that a physician needs to assess. Sharing the result with consent keeps care coordinated. Waiting for pain to improve delays help, and a social work referral or discharge from active care does not address the clinical concern.
  9. The correct answer is C (27 percent, adding 18 for the anterior trunk and 9 for the whole arm): In an adult the anterior trunk counts as 18 percent and one whole arm as 9 percent, giving 27 percent. Counting the arm as 18 treats it like a leg, using 4.5 counts only half an arm, and 36 for the trunk includes the back, which is not burned here.
  10. The correct answer is D (Capacity is decision-specific, so assess whether she understands and appreciates the risks of this decision): Capacity is assessed for each decision at the time it is made, and a diagnosis of mild dementia does not remove it. The physiotherapist who proposes the walker checks whether she understands the information, appreciates how it applies to her, and can weigh and communicate a choice. Waiting for a physician is unnecessary for this routine decision. Recalling the fall does not prove she appreciates the risks, and a screening score cannot determine capacity.
  11. The correct answer is A (Keep a calm tone, step back toward the door, and activate the clinic's alert): When aggression is escalating, the priority is personal safety: stay calm, create distance, keep an exit and bring in help using the workplace violence procedure. Moving toward him closes the distance and can provoke further aggression even if the tone is firm. Blocking the doorway restrains the client and can escalate the confrontation. Debating the invoice keeps the therapist within reach of a possible assault.
  12. The correct answer is C (Plantar fascia, through the windlass mechanism that raises the arch as the toe extends): Extending the great toe winds the plantar fascia around the metatarsal head like a windlass, tightening the fascia at its calcaneal origin. Reproducing medial heel pain with this movement supports plantar fasciopathy. The Achilles tendon and tibial nerve are not tensioned by toe extension in this way.
  13. The correct answer is C (The nursing staff, because skin checks and repositioning routines across all shifts are nursing care): Continuous skin checks and repositioning across day and night shifts are nursing responsibilities, and the physiotherapist should report the finding to the nurse. Occupational therapy contributes seating and cushions, and dietetics supports healing, but neither runs the 24-hour routine. A turning schedule does not require a physician's order.
  14. The correct answer is A (Invite her to phone or attend for assessment, and record the exchange in her chart): Her consent covered reminders only, and a possible infection needs a hands-on assessment. The exchange is part of the clinical record and should be documented. Giving an opinion by text or asking for more photos extends the unsecured channel, and deleting the image removes record content.
  15. The correct answer is C (Report the blood pressure drop and sedative to the physician and teach slow position changes): A systolic drop of more than 20 mmHg on standing suggests orthostatic hypotension, and a sedative adds to fall risk, so the prescriber should review them while the physiotherapist teaches safe transitions. Physiotherapists do not stop medications. A walker and strengthening may help later but do not address the findings, and discharge would leave the risk unmanaged.
  16. The correct answer is B (Thank the colleague, ask for a specific example, and plan one change to try): Receiving feedback well means listening, clarifying with a concrete example and committing to a small, testable change. Defending the current approach, avoiding the topic, or collecting other opinions first all delay learning and signal that the feedback was not welcomed.
  17. The correct answer is C (Eleven follow-up visits, after deducting break time and assessments from the day): Eight hours is 480 minutes. Subtracting the 60 unscheduled minutes leaves 420, and removing two 45-minute assessments (90 minutes) leaves 330 minutes, which is 11 visits of 30 minutes. Fourteen ignores the assessments, thirteen ignores the unscheduled hour, and twelve treats the assessments as shorter than they are.
  18. The correct answer is C (K1, a household ambulator who walks at a fixed cadence on level surfaces): K1 describes someone who transfers and walks on level surfaces at a fixed cadence, typically within the home. K2 requires the ability to cross low-level barriers such as curbs and uneven ground, which this client lacks. K0 means no ability or potential to ambulate with a prosthesis, and K3 requires variable cadence and most barriers.
  19. The correct answer is A (Stop the treadmill, seat him with support, and recheck pressure after rest before deciding next steps): A sharp pressure rise with a new headache after stroke is a red-flag response, so exercise stops and he is seated and rechecked before anyone decides whether to continue or to notify the physician. Slowing the belt or continuing keeps loading an unstable pressure, and a rise of this size is not an expected exercise response.
  20. The correct answer is B (Use a towel or sheet to cover the area, exposing only the treated gluteal region): Draping should expose only the area being treated and keep the rest covered, with the client informed and agreeing to the technique. Exposing both buttocks and thighs shows more than treatment needs. Treating through a thick sheet defeats the purpose of the technique. Asking the client to hold clothing aside is awkward and still exposes more than necessary.
  21. The correct answer is A (A speech-language pathologist, because assessing and managing swallowing safety is part of that profession's role): Coughing and a wet voice after sipping water suggest a swallowing problem, and formal swallowing assessment and management belongs to speech-language pathology. A dietitian becomes useful after safe textures are identified, and an occupational therapist helps with feeding set-up. A respiratory therapist is not the usual lead for swallowing assessment.
  22. The correct answer is C (Ask what he can use, then provide large high-contrast print or audio instructions on his phone): Accessible information is offered in a format the client can actually perceive, and asking him what works avoids assumptions. Large high-contrast print or audio can be used independently at home. Emailing the same small-print handout assumes zooming is workable, which is slow and unreliable with central vision loss. A video alone may be hard to see, and memory of a one-time reading is unreliable.
  23. The correct answer is B (Refer to a physician for possible axial spondyloarthritis, because inflammatory back pain features are present): Young age, insidious onset, prolonged morning stiffness that improves with movement, and night pain in the second half of the night are inflammatory features that call for medical assessment for axial spondyloarthritis. Positive provocation tests do not distinguish mechanical from inflammatory sacroiliac pain. Mechanical treatments would delay diagnosis.
  24. The correct answer is C (Pain education with graded exposure to feared movements, because fear avoidance is sustaining his disability): His beliefs and avoidance, not neurological loss or red flags, are keeping him disabled, so education that reframes the scan findings combined with graded exposure to feared tasks is the priority. Passive manipulation and activity restriction reinforce the belief that his spine is fragile. Repeating imaging adds nothing, since the scan already shows common age-related change.
  25. The correct answer is B (A clinical record audit that compares charted documentation against an agreed practice standard): An audit measures practice against a set criterion, here goal recording and repeated outcome measurement, using the clinic's own records. A satisfaction survey would ask clients, benchmarking would compare with other organizations, and a trial would test the effect of an intervention.
  26. The correct answer is A (Wrist slightly extended, MCP joints flexed 70 to 90 degrees, IP joints straight, thumb abducted): The intrinsic-plus (safe) position protects the collateral ligaments and counters the claw deformity that dorsal hand burns encourage. The MCP joints are flexed, the IP joints kept straight, the wrist slightly extended and the thumb abducted. The other positions reproduce the claw, tighten the collaterals or fail to protect the web space.
  27. The correct answer is C (Review the plan with the assistant and clarify that progression decisions rest with the physiotherapist): Progressing exercise is a clinical decision that belongs to the physiotherapist, even when the result is favourable. The best action is a clear conversation about roles and the plan, with the physiotherapist making any progression. Formal discipline is out of proportion, and allowing independent decisions leaves the problem in place.
  28. The correct answer is D (Right peripheral vestibular loss, because an abnormal impulse response fits a unilateral peripheral lesion): A corrective saccade after a rapid head turn to one side shows that the vestibulo-ocular reflex is impaired on that side, here the right. Peripheral nystagmus beats away from the damaged ear, which agrees with left-beating nystagmus. A normal impulse test would instead raise concern for a central cause, and abnormal turns on only one side argue against bilateral loss.
  29. The correct answer is A (Advise rest with elevation and refer him promptly to his physician for assessment and diagnosis): An acutely red, swollen and exquisitely painful first toe joint needs a medical diagnosis, because gout, infection and other conditions look alike and are treated differently. Mobilization, heat and ultrasound can aggravate an acute inflammatory joint and delay appropriate care.
  30. The correct answer is D (Decline release until she holds signed client authorization or a valid court order, replying in writing): A lawyer's statement that the client agreed is not valid consent. Health information may be released only with the client's authorization or under legal compulsion such as a court order. Releasing partial data or confirming details with a third party still discloses information improperly.
  31. The correct answer is A (Sedation and orthostatic dizziness raise his fall risk, so check pressure and provide close assistance): Opioids commonly cause sedation, slowed responses and orthostatic hypotension, all of which increase fall risk during mobilization. Checking blood pressure and giving close assistance addresses this directly. Constipation is real but does not govern the session, and opioids do not impair wound healing in the way suggested.
  32. The correct answer is D (Use short concrete words and show each activity on a doll first): Preschool children think concretely and learn best from simple language, demonstration and play. Abstract explanations of healing are beyond her stage. Speaking only to the mother excludes the child from her own care, and asking for a long repeat-back overloads her memory.
  33. The correct answer is C (Explain what is known, admit uncertainty, and commit to working toward his goals): Honest prognostic talk shares evidence, acknowledges what cannot be known and offers partnership, which maintains hope without false promises. Physiotherapists do discuss expected recovery within their scope, so deferring the question entirely or postponing the discussion leaves him without support, and a general optimistic statement may prove untrue.
  34. The correct answer is A (Touch plantar sites such as the hallux and metatarsal heads with her eyes closed, avoiding callus): The standard screening applies the 10 gram monofilament briefly to plantar sites such as the hallux and the metatarsal heads, with the eyes closed so vision does not guide the response. Callused skin gives falsely reduced sensation and is avoided. Holding the filament for many seconds is not the technique, and watching the contact removes the blinding. Testing the dorsum does not detect loss of protective sensation.
  35. The correct answer is A (Advise stopping prosthetic wear and contact the prosthetist to modify the socket relief): Redness over a bony prominence that persists beyond about twenty minutes after the prosthesis is removed signals excessive pressure and a risk of tissue breakdown. The socket needs modification, so wear should be paused while the prosthetist reviews it. Extra socks can raise pressure on the crest, and continuing or increasing wear risks an ulcer.
  36. The correct answer is D (Stop the client's activity, keep the leg supported, and notify the physiotherapist at once): New calf pain, swelling and warmth after joint replacement may signal a deep vein thrombosis, which is outside the support worker's role to assess and calls for immediate physiotherapist involvement. Continuing exercise or applying heat could worsen the risk. Modifying the exercise or deferring review delays the decision that only the physiotherapist can make.
  37. The correct answer is B (Call emergency medical services right away and note the time the symptoms first began): Sudden facial droop, slurred speech and arm weakness are the FAST signs of stroke, and the time of onset determines eligibility for emergency treatment. Improvement does not make this safe, because a transient episode can precede a major stroke. Detailed screening, phone advice or waiting all delay the emergency response.
  38. The correct answer is B (Advertising must be truthful and verifiable, so outcome claims need supporting evidence): Regulatory standards require advertising to be accurate, verifiable and not misleading, and outcome promises or unsupported superlatives cannot be substantiated. Listing every fee in an ad is not generally required, and colleges do not typically pre-approve each ad. Truthful, evidence-based claims about results can be acceptable.
  39. The correct answer is A (Defer exercise and promptly contact her physician, because the findings may indicate lupus kidney involvement): Facial puffiness, foamy urine and a new raised blood pressure in someone with lupus suggest lupus nephritis, a serious complication that needs prompt medical evaluation. Continuing the joint program or starting exercise delays that assessment. Sun protection and rest are sensible lupus advice but do not address possible organ involvement.
  40. The correct answer is A (Use the ceiling lift and bariatric sling with two trained staff, after checking each rating): Every component of a lift system has its own safe working load, and the lowest rating governs. The standard sling is rated below his weight, so the bariatric sling is required, and a dependent client who cannot bear weight should not be moved manually. Staff should be trained on the equipment and check ratings before use.
  41. The correct answer is D (Defer exercise and arrange prompt medical assessment, because findings suggest an acute exacerbation with possible infection): Increased purulent sputum, fever, tachycardia and a saturation below her usual level suggest an acute exacerbation, possibly infectious. Exercise should be deferred and she should be assessed medically. Airway clearance alone does not address a possible infection, and supplemental oxygen would mask a deteriorating condition rather than make training safe.
  42. The correct answer is C (A supervised group exercise and education class, which serves several clients at once using evidence-based content): Group exercise and education is an established way to deliver evidence-based osteoarthritis care to several stable clients in one supervised session. Very short visits cannot deliver adequate assessment or exercise progression. A single phone consultation or a mailed package with one call offers advice but no supervised exercise or progression, which the evidence supports for this condition.
  43. The correct answer is C (Pause the walk, coach pursed-lip breathing, and increase oxygen flow per the titration protocol before restarting): At minute six the saturation is below her lower target limit, so the walk is paused, recovery is supported with pursed-lip breathing, and oxygen flow is increased following the titration protocol so she can continue training safely. Continuing risks greater desaturation. Cancelling the whole session is unnecessary because titration usually restores a safe range, and removing oxygen would worsen hypoxemia.
  44. The correct answer is D (Discuss the findings directly with each other, comparing the tasks and observations behind each conclusion): Differences between professionals are usually explained by different tasks, surfaces, fatigue or time of day, and a direct conversation clarifies this respectfully. Escalating immediately, leaving the physician to arbitrate, or quietly replacing a colleague's finding skips the collaboration needed for a shared plan.
  45. The correct answer is D (Report it to a supervisor or the college, because duty to the public outweighs friendship): Billing for services not provided is dishonest and a professional conduct matter, and registrants are accountable to the public before personal loyalties. She has direct evidence, so she does not need to wait for proof or for a private cure. Informing only the insurer bypasses the clinic and the college, which are responsible for addressing the conduct.
  46. The correct answer is A (Tingling in both hands, because paresthesias in the extremities are a high-risk factor): Under the Canadian C-spine rule, paresthesias in the extremities are a high-risk factor that mandates radiography. The high-risk age threshold is 65 years or older. Simple rear-end collisions, ambulation at the scene, and delayed onset of neck pain are low-risk factors that allow range of motion testing but do not require imaging.
  47. The correct answer is B (Clear the corridor of stored equipment so a wide, unobstructed walking route is available): The barrier is the clutter narrowing the route, so the accessible solution removes it for everyone. Guiding her through a gap leaves the hazard in place and risks a fall. Taking away her walker removes her independence, and treating her in a corridor compromises privacy and the quality of care.
  48. The correct answer is D (Springy block, suggesting an intra-articular obstruction such as a displaced meniscal fragment): A springy rebound before full range is the classic springy block end-feel and indicates something inside the joint, such as a torn meniscus fragment, blocking the movement. An empty end-feel has no tissue resistance and is driven by pain, and a capsular end-feel feels firm and leathery rather than springy. A bony end-feel is hard and abrupt, which does not match the rebound described.
  49. The correct answer is A (Positive pivot shift, indicating anterior cruciate ligament insufficiency, because the subluxed tibia reduces in flexion): The pivot shift reproduces anterolateral rotatory instability: the lateral tibial plateau sits subluxed in extension and reduces with a shift at about 30 degrees of flexion. It is highly specific for anterior cruciate ligament deficiency. The other tests and structures do not produce a reduction shift of this kind.
  50. The correct answer is B (Hold treatment and notify her physician today about the INR and bruise, because bleeding may continue): An INR above target with an expanding bruise and a tight ankle suggests ongoing bleeding, so treatment is held and the physician is told promptly. A firm wrap, loading or heat could worsen the bleed or hide compartment pressure. Standard sprain care is secondary until anticoagulation is reviewed.
  51. The correct answer is B (A structured neuromuscular warm-up with strength, balance and landing technique, performed at least twice weekly): Multicomponent neuromuscular warm-up programs that train strength, balance, agility and landing mechanics have the strongest evidence for lowering lower limb and knee ligament injuries in youth female soccer players. Static stretching before activity does not reduce injury rates. Routine bracing and added running volume lack comparable support.
  52. The correct answer is C (Active straight leg raise, positive, suggesting impaired load transfer through the pelvic girdle): Lifting the leg actively and noting that pelvic compression relieves the heaviness is the active straight leg raise, which indicates impaired load transfer in pelvic girdle pain. The thigh thrust and FABER tests use passive positions, and a passive straight leg raise screens for nerve root tension. The compression improvement points to the pelvis.
  53. The correct answer is C (Stop treatment, assess the burn and provide appropriate wound care, ensuring the client's immediate safety): Client safety comes first, so the injury is assessed and treated before any other step. Disclosure, the incident report and notifying the manager are all required, but they follow once the client is cared for. Delaying wound care to complete paperwork or explanations would risk further harm.
  54. The correct answer is B (Stop the activity, position the client safely, and assess vital signs while calling for help): Chest tightness with light-headedness may signal a cardiac event, so client safety and emergency steps come before teaching. Stopping the exercise, positioning the client and getting help with vital signs is the priority. Feedback to the student follows once the client is safe.
  55. The correct answer is A (Provide clear education and reassurance about the good prognosis, and set small graded activity goals): High distress and negative recovery expectations predict poor outcomes after whiplash, so early education and graded activity are important. Rest and manipulation do not address her beliefs, and imaging is not indicated without red flags; distress alone does not suggest hidden injury.
  56. The correct answer is C (Speak privately with the colleague, express concern, and encourage use of support services): Because no harm has occurred and the pattern fits workload strain, a private supportive conversation is the best first step and can lead to workload review and support. An immediate college report is premature without evidence of unsafe practice, and silently correcting notes hides the problem. Acting behind the colleague's back removes her chance to ask for help.
  57. The correct answer is A (Stop, shift his weight sideways, then step over a floor line or use a rhythmic cue): External visual or auditory cues, combined with a weight shift to unload one leg, are the best way to restart movement during freezing. Waiting without a cue often prolongs the freeze. Leaning forward and rushing the first step raises the risk of a fall, as the earlier falls show. A standard walker that must be lifted can worsen freezing rather than prevent it.
  58. The correct answer is A (Stop the task and look for unmet needs such as pain, hunger or toileting): Sudden behaviour change in dementia often signals an unmet need, and here hunger and pain are clues in the stem. Pushing on risks harm, and requesting sedation or only changing rooms does not address the cause. The physiotherapist should stop, reassess and involve the nurse about pain and nutrition.
  59. The correct answer is D (Pain-free isometric plantarflexion, then double-leg heel raises, keeping pain at or below 3 out of 10): Early rehabilitation of a muscle strain uses pain-monitored loading, starting with isometrics and progressing to light isotonic work. Maximal eccentric work and heavy resistance overload the repair too soon. Aggressive prolonged stretching into strong pain can disrupt healing fibres.
  60. The correct answer is C (Right posterior canal benign paroxysmal positional vertigo, because the response is latent, torsional, brief and fatigable): In posterior canal BPPV the Dix-Hallpike on the affected side produces upbeating, torsional nystagmus with the top pole beating toward the undermost ear, after a short latency, lasting under a minute and fading on repetition. Horizontal canal disease produces horizontal nystagmus on the supine roll test. Central causes tend to have no latency, persist and do not fatigue.
  61. The correct answer is C (A significant reversible response, because FEV1 rose by more than 12 percent and 200 mL): An increase in FEV1 of more than 12 percent and at least 200 mL after a bronchodilator shows reversible airflow obstruction, which supports asthma. Here FEV1 rose by 0.5 L, about 21 percent, which exceeds both limits. Repeat-trial variation is far smaller than 0.5 L, and the 25 percent and 1 L cutoffs are not the accepted criteria.
  62. The correct answer is D (To control edema and mould the residual limb into a shape that suits the socket): A shrinker applies gentle, even compression that reduces edema and encourages a conical, mature limb shape for prosthetic fitting. It does not boost arterial flow in a limb with vascular disease, and it gives no meaningful resistance for strengthening. Warmth and dryness are side benefits, not the purpose.
  63. The correct answer is C (Raise the monitor so its top edge sits at or just below eye level): Setting the top of the screen at or slightly below eye level at about arm's length lets the neck stay neutral while the eyes look slightly down. A closer monitor or a downward gaze keeps the neck flexed, and lowering the chair changes the seat and arm height without correcting the screen position.

Practice Test 4 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is B (Pause and confirm the client's identity, the treatment side and the parameters against the plan): A final verification immediately before treatment, often called a time-out, confirms the right client, right side and right treatment, and it is especially needed after interruptions. Visual checks of a setup done earlier, a contraindication form from a past visit, or the client's agreement do not confirm that the plan still matches this person. It takes under a minute and prevents wrong-site and wrong-parameter errors.
  2. The correct answer is A (Fit exercises into daily routines, demonstrate movements, and coach her to cue rather than force): Caregiver training works best when exercises are built into familiar routines and the caregiver learns to demonstrate and cue calmly. Constant correction and forced sessions raise distress, and observing from a distance gives no real support for carryover.
  3. The correct answer is A (Pause his independent use, offer supervised practice, and recheck competence before resuming his assignments): A worker's stated discomfort is a safety signal and needs a response, even when others rate his skill highly. Pausing, supervised practice and a repeated competence check address the concern directly. Reassurance or continued assignment ignores it, and removing the unit for everyone is out of proportion.
  4. The correct answer is C (Clarify the discrepancy with the referring surgeon and confirm the side with the client first): A conflict between the order and the clinical findings must be resolved before any treatment, by contacting the person who wrote the referral and confirming the side with the client. Treating either knee on a guess risks wrong-site treatment. Amending someone else's order is outside the physiotherapist's role, and the discrepancy should be documented.
  5. The correct answer is C (Refit the crutches below the armpits, teach hand loading and report nerve symptoms to the physician): Weight borne through the axilla compresses the radial nerve and causes crutch palsy. The cause is corrected by refitting so the tops sit about two finger widths below the armpits and teaching her to load through the hands, and the neurological signs should be reported. Padding hides the pressure without removing it, and resuming at the same height repeats the injury.
  6. The correct answer is D (Call emergency services using the location and number on file, and stay on the video link): Sudden slumping and slurred speech suggest a new stroke, which requires emergency services immediately and continued observation until help arrives. Testing with a repeat exercise or checking by phone wastes time. A same-day doctor visit is too slow for a time-critical emergency.
  7. The correct answer is A (The other-site physiotherapist's full chart view on March 6, because the log shows no care purpose): Access must match a legitimate purpose. A physiotherapist from another site who opened the full chart with no treating relationship has no apparent care purpose and is the likely snooping event. The billing clerk and receptionist viewed only the administrative information their roles require. The assistant works under delegation and is part of the circle of care for the treatment shown.
  8. The correct answer is C (Cystocele, descent of the bladder and anterior vaginal wall into the vagina): Anterior vaginal wall descent with urinary hesitancy relieved by pressing the front wall is a cystocele. A rectocele affects the posterior wall and causes difficulty emptying the bowel. Uterine prolapse involves the cervix, and an enterocele involves small bowel descending between the vagina and rectum.
  9. The correct answer is D (Fulcrum on the greater trochanter, stationary arm along the trunk, moving arm along the lateral femur): For hip flexion the goniometer axis sits over the greater trochanter, the stationary arm follows the lateral midline of the trunk, and the moving arm follows the lateral femoral midline toward the lateral epicondyle. The other placements use the wrong landmarks and would record pelvic motion or knee angle instead of hip motion.
  10. The correct answer is A (Consult his physician about bleeding risk and consider non-needling techniques such as manual pressure instead): Anticoagulation raises the risk of significant bleeding or hematoma with any needling, and a therapeutic INR is intended to prolong clotting, not to make the procedure low risk. Checking with the prescribing physician and using non-invasive options is the safest course. Modifying the needle or depth does not remove the risk.
  11. The correct answer is D (Begin early pain-limited movement and weight bearing with support, because controlled loading speeds recovery): For most ankle sprains, a short period of protection followed by early pain-limited motion and weight bearing gives faster functional recovery than prolonged immobilization or non-weight bearing. Jumping and cutting drills are late-stage goals that require restored strength and control first.
  12. The correct answer is A (Use the Borg rating of perceived exertion scale because the beta-blocker blunts the heart rate response): Beta-blockers blunt the heart rate response, so heart-rate-based targets underestimate his true effort. Perceived exertion on the Borg scale still reflects the workload while the drug limits heart rate. Formulas that rely on age-predicted maximum are inaccurate in medicated clients, and a fixed number of beats above rest does not match the way the drug works.
  13. The correct answer is C (Immobilize as a suspected scaphoid fracture and repeat imaging, because early films can miss it): Initial radiographs can miss scaphoid fractures, so clinical signs such as snuffbox tenderness and pain on axial thumb compression justify immobilization and repeat imaging or advanced imaging. Missed fractures risk nonunion and avascular necrosis of the proximal pole. De Quervain tenosynovitis and contusion do not explain the axial compression pain.
  14. The correct answer is A (High risk with total 12, so reposition at least every two hours on a pressure-redistributing mattress): The subscores add to 12 (3 + 2 + 1 + 2 + 3 + 1), which falls in the high-risk band of 10 to 12. This calls for repositioning at least every two hours on a pressure-redistributing surface. Lower categories under-treat the risk, and a steep head-of-bed angle adds shear.
  15. The correct answer is B (Prioritize two key stretches with the parents and build them into dressing and bath time routines): A program the family cannot do will not work, so it should be shortened to the highest-value stretches and fitted into routines the parents already have. Repeating an unachievable program ignores the barrier. Three weekly clinic visits add travel burden, and young siblings should not be responsible for stretching a child with spasticity.
  16. The correct answer is C (Stop the examination, avoid cervical manipulation, and arrange emergency transfer for suspected cervical artery injury): New severe headache, facial numbness, dizziness and a small pupil after neck trauma suggest dissection of a cervical artery with possible Horner syndrome, which is an emergency. Provocation testing is not a valid screening tool and can cause harm. Continuing movement or waiting for a routine visit delays urgent care, and whiplash does not explain the neurological signs.
  17. The correct answer is C (Move her to a floor mat and stay close, because falling from the plinth is risky): Her aura gives about a minute of warning, and the main risk is injury from a fall or from the plinth. Helping her to a mat on the floor away from furniture and staying with her prepares for a seizure safely. Rails on a plinth can cause entrapment injuries, an aura does not by itself justify an emergency call, and continuing assessment wastes the warning time.
  18. The correct answer is B (Use the employee assistance program and debrief with a supervisor, because structured support reduces moral distress): Structured support such as confidential counselling and a debrief helps clinicians process grief and guilt and keeps them functioning safely. Returning to full duty without support or avoiding similar cases leaves distress unaddressed. Reviewing notes alone may be reasonable later but does not replace emotional support.
  19. The correct answer is C (Imaging is not needed to make the diagnosis, since these clinical findings are sufficient): Knee osteoarthritis can be diagnosed clinically in an older adult with activity-related pain, brief morning stiffness and typical examination findings. Radiographs correlate poorly with symptoms and are reserved for atypical presentations or surgical planning. Magnetic resonance imaging and blood tests add little when the picture is classic.
  20. The correct answer is D (Release the findings and treatment dates her consent covers, and ask her before sharing more): Consent covers only what the client agreed to, which here is findings and treatment dates. Additional information needs her permission, even when a third party pays. Releasing the full file, or most of it, goes beyond her consent, while refusing everything ignores the release she did sign.
  21. The correct answer is C (About 60 to 90 minutes after a dose, when her mobility is at its best): The table shows her best Timed Up and Go and no freezing about 60 to 90 minutes after the dose, which is the peak 'on' period and the best time for high-quality, safe gait practice. Training in the late wearing-off state raises fall and freezing risk, and 30 minutes is before the full effect has built.
  22. The correct answer is B (Low-level disinfection, because items in contact with intact skin are classified as non-critical): The Spaulding classification places items that touch only intact skin in the non-critical category, which requires cleaning followed by low-level disinfection between clients. Semi-critical items contact mucous membranes or non-intact skin, and critical items enter sterile tissue. Higher levels are not needed for plinths, goniometers and lead wires.
  23. The correct answer is C (Remove the splint, offload the heel, and report the finding to the nursing team): Redness that does not blanch signals early pressure injury, and neuropathy hides the pain that would normally warn him. The pressure source must be removed and the team informed right away. A foam dressing under the same splint keeps the pressure on the heel, and waiting until tomorrow lets the injury progress. Massaging reddened skin can cause deeper tissue damage.
  24. The correct answer is D (Train at 24 mmHg with ten-second holds for ten repetitions, progressing only when control is maintained): Craniocervical flexion training begins at the highest stage she can hold with good control, using ten-second holds, and progresses once control is steady. Starting at a stage where control fails reinforces superficial muscle use, and a much lower or shorter dose does not challenge the deep flexors.
  25. The correct answer is B (Reassess the client personally and hold the delegated program until the rising heart rate is explained): The resting rate has risen steadily across the weeks and now exceeds the stop threshold in the plan, so the client's status has changed and the physiotherapist must reassess. Feeling well does not rule out decompensation. Modifying the program through the assistant or waiting delays assessment.
  26. The correct answer is A (Recline him with legs elevated, recheck pressure, and consider an abdominal binder before retrying): Loss of sympathetic vasoconstriction below the lesion causes blood pooling and orthostatic hypotension, so he should be reclined with the legs raised and monitored. An abdominal binder and compression stockings then support a gradual, staged return to upright sitting. Forward leaning and pushing through do not restore perfusion, and abandoning upright training prevents adaptation.
  27. The correct answer is A (An open question about his workday, because it invites his own description): Open questions cannot be answered with one word, so they invite the client to describe his own experience and build trust. Closed and multiple-part questions collect data efficiently but tend to produce short replies, and a leading question risks shaping his story before he has told it.
  28. The correct answer is D (Keep straps as set and ask the team about changes, because strap length determines hip position): The strap lengths were set by the treating team to hold the hips flexed and gently abducted, so any change alters the position that keeps the femoral head reduced. Parents should not adjust straps themselves. Forcing extra abduction can compromise the blood supply to the femoral head, and repeated removal or loosening interrupts the position the harness is meant to maintain.
  29. The correct answer is C (Weakness of the right hip abductors, mainly gluteus medius, which cannot hold the pelvis level): In single-limb stance the stance-side hip abductors stop the opposite pelvis from dropping, so a left pelvic drop in right stance means the right gluteus medius is weak. The left abductors are working on the swing side during this phase. Gluteus maximus weakness typically produces a backward trunk lean, and quadriceps weakness affects knee control rather than pelvic level.
  30. The correct answer is B (Increase fluid and salt intake before and during play, because his sweat contains high sodium): Children with cystic fibrosis lose excess sodium in sweat, so heat and exercise raise the risk of dehydration and salt depletion, and extra fluid and salt are advised before and during play. Waiting until after the game leaves him depleted while playing, and water alone does not replace the lost sodium. Exercise can supplement but should not replace the prescribed airway clearance.
  31. The correct answer is B (Confirm the assistant's competence through documented training and demonstration, and define how she will be supervised): Before delegating, the physiotherapist must be satisfied that the person is trained and competent for that specific task and that supervision and reassessment are in place. A physician's authorization is not the standard, and employer sign-off or client consent cannot substitute for verified competence.
  32. The correct answer is A (Stand, walk 3 metres at a comfortable pace with the walker, turn, return and sit): The standard Timed Up and Go has the person rise from a standard chair, walk 3 m at a comfortable and safe pace, turn, return and sit, using their usual walking aid. Testing without the aid or at maximal speed changes what is measured and can be unsafe. Using a different distance makes results incomparable with published reference times.
  33. The correct answer is C (It shows general preparation but does not confirm competence for each specific task assigned): A diploma indicates general education, but competence for a specific task in a specific setting must be verified by the physiotherapist, usually by observation. Accountability for delegated care stays with the physiotherapist who assigns the task. Program accreditation does not remove that duty.
  34. The correct answer is A (Stop and seat her, because saturation under the protocol limit needs monitoring and physician review): The reading at minute five is 86%, below the stated stopping limit, so the test ends and she sits while saturation is monitored until it recovers. A desaturation this size after COVID-19 should be reported so the physician can investigate before further exertion testing. Continuing, breathing coaching on the move, or restarting after a brief pause all keep her exercising while desaturated.
  35. The correct answer is C (Functional capacity evaluation, because it objectively tests physical abilities against defined job demands): A functional capacity evaluation measures performance across tasks such as lifting, carrying and positional tolerance, and compares results with job demands. Questionnaires measure perceived disability, a single strength test is too narrow, and work conditioning is an intervention for building capacity, not a method of determining it.
  36. The correct answer is D (Acknowledge the concern, offer another clinician or a chaperone, and let him choose how to proceed): Client-centred care treats a stated discomfort as a reason to offer options and return control to the client. He decides among another clinician, a chaperone or continuing. Pushing on because of his pain, or narrowing the options to suit the clinic, overrides his autonomy. Requiring him to justify his feelings adds pressure. The aim is a safe assessment that he has agreed to.
  37. The correct answer is B (Posterior cruciate ligament injury, because a resting posterior sag that corrects with anterior pull is characteristic): A posterior tibial sag at 90 degrees after a dashboard-type injury points to posterior cruciate ligament injury, and the anterior pull simply reduces the sag.
