A normal dial test makes a posterolateral corner injury unlikely. A difference between knees is not a normal variation, and anterior translation beyond the other side is absent.
  38. The correct answer is C (Arrange urgent medical assessment for a possible cardiac cause, because the pain is exertional and non-mechanical): Exertional pain that resolves with rest and comes with nausea, in a man over 60 with no mechanical reproduction, is a pattern of possible angina. Prompt medical assessment is required before any trial of manual therapy or exercise. Delaying with trial treatment risks missing unstable coronary disease.
  39. The correct answer is C (Keep oxygen equipment well away from heat, flames and sparks, because oxygen makes combustion more intense): Oxygen is not flammable itself but makes fires burn faster and hotter, so heat sources, flames and smoking must be kept away from any oxygen equipment, including concentrators and tubing. Switching the machine off or lengthening the tubing does not remove the hazard.
  40. The correct answer is D (Continue strengthening until quadriceps strength reaches 90 percent, since residual deficits raise reinjury risk): Return-to-sport decisions rest on a battery of criteria, and a quadriceps deficit of 18 percent is still linked with higher reinjury risk even when hop symmetry looks good. Good hop scores do not replace strength. A brace does not compensate for weakness, and the absence of giving way does not show that strength has recovered.
  41. The correct answer is C (Coordinate analgesia with nursing, then coach deep breathing with a splinted incision and sitting up): Pain is limiting his inspiration and driving postoperative basal atelectasis, so pain control comes first so that deep breathing can be effective. Splinting and upright positioning then help re-expand the bases. Percussion targets secretions he does not have, and walking or CPAP without addressing pain is less effective.
  42. The correct answer is D (Stop forceful passive stretching and report the findings promptly so imaging can be arranged): Falling range with a warm, painful, swollen elbow and a hard end-feel suggests heterotopic ossification rather than simple contracture. Forceful stretching can worsen it and risk fracture, so it stops and the team is told for imaging. Splinting, heat or deeper stretching all increase the force across an abnormal joint.
  43. The correct answer is B (Stationary arm toward the greater trochanter, moving arm toward the lateral malleolus): For knee flexion the axis lies at the lateral femoral epicondyle, with the stationary arm along the lateral midline of the thigh toward the greater trochanter and the moving arm along the lower leg toward the lateral malleolus. Pointing the moving arm at the fibular head shortens the segment and misstates the angle. The medial malleolus is on the wrong side of the leg for a lateral axis. Aiming the stationary arm at the iliac spine runs off the thigh midline.
  44. The correct answer is C (Reassure him and coordinate a graded return with modified duties plus an activity program): Evidence supports early graded return to work with modified duties, combined with education that addresses fear of movement. Waiting for zero pain prolongs absence. Full duties immediately may be too much for his fear and deconditioning, and imaging does not guide return in non-specific pain.
  45. The correct answer is B (Remove the heat, cool the area, assess and document the burn, and report per clinic policy): Blistering with a mottled pattern indicates a thermal burn, so heat is stopped, the area cooled, and the injury assessed, documented and reported. Calling blisters an expected response hides an incident that needs reporting. Tolerating warmth does not exclude damage, and reapplying heat, even with more toweling, risks further harm.
  46. The correct answer is D (Offer fewer in-person visits with a brief home program and virtual follow-up, because it suits him): A flexible plan with fewer visits, a short home program and remote check-ins respects his work and childcare demands while allowing monitoring. A weekly evening visit or a fixed weekly class still requires the weekly attendance he has said he cannot manage. A text-message program with no follow-up gives no way to check technique, progression or safety.
  47. The correct answer is A (Offload the heel with a pillow under the calf and report the skin change to nurses): The skin is intact but discolored and painless in a client on prolonged bed rest, which fits early pressure damage, so the heel is floated off the mattress and the change is reported to nursing. Heel slides add friction and shear to compromised skin, and a padded boot does not remove the pressure or replace the need to report. Heat on skin without normal sensation risks a burn and does not relieve the pressure that caused the damage.
  48. The correct answer is B (Explain that the evidence is limited, discuss options, and decide together using her values): A systematic review is only as strong as the studies it includes, and this one has small, biased, inconsistent trials. Evidence-informed practice combines the research with clinical expertise and the client's values, so honest discussion is best. Neither blind acceptance nor automatic rejection nor overriding her preferences fits.
  49. The correct answer is C (Hip flexed to 90 degrees with slight adduction, then a force down the femoral shaft): The thigh thrust test places the hip at 90 degrees of flexion with slight adduction and applies a posteriorly directed force through the femur to shear the sacroiliac joint. The abducted and externally rotated position describes the FABER test, and the extended hip with the opposite knee flexed describes Gaenslen test. A lateral force at 45 degrees does not shear the joint.
  50. The correct answer is A (Environmental factor, because it is an external condition that limits her participation at work): In the ICF, environmental factors include workplace policies, supports and attitudes that sit outside the person. The inability to lift is an activity limitation, shoulder pain and weakness are impairments, and her anxiety is a personal factor. Only the absence of modified duties is an environmental barrier.
  51. The correct answer is D (Return her to supine, elevate the legs and alert the nurse, because this suggests autonomic instability): A sudden rise in heart rate with a large drop in blood pressure and pallor on sitting points to autonomic instability, which is a recognized and potentially dangerous feature of acute Guillain-Barre syndrome. Lying her flat with the legs raised restores perfusion, and telling the nurse brings in medical review right away. Reclining alone without notifying anyone and then resuming sitting leaves an unstable autonomic problem unreported. Slowing the trial keeps her upright, and checking respiratory status is worthwhile later but does not treat the hypotension happening now.
  52. The correct answer is B (Refer for a standing posteroanterior spinal radiograph to measure the Cobb angle): A persistent rib hump on forward bend with a scoliometer reading of about 7 degrees or more suggests structural scoliosis and warrants radiographic confirmation. A rapidly growing premenarchal girl is at higher risk of progression, so waiting six months is unsafe. Exercise alone does not confirm or exclude the diagnosis, and equal leg lengths remove one postural explanation rather than supporting reassurance.
  53. The correct answer is D (Support the leg without moving it and arrange urgent medical review, since dislocation is likely): A pop on rising from a low seat, severe pain, inability to bear weight and a shortened, internally rotated leg are the classic signs of a posterior prosthetic dislocation. The leg is supported in the position found and urgent medical review is arranged, because reduction is a medical procedure. Attempting traction or reduction risks nerve and vascular injury, and asking her to move the hip tests a joint that is probably out of place. Moving her to bed and waiting for a routine surgical round delays care that is time sensitive.
  54. The correct answer is C (Explain the risks of unencrypted email, obtain and document consent, and confirm the address): Clients should understand the risks of electronic communication and agree to it, and the agreement should be recorded. An exercise sheet can still reveal health information, and a personal account or a skipped discussion does not meet that standard.
  55. The correct answer is B (Grade 1+, because a catch is followed by minimal resistance through less than half the range): Grade 1+ on the Modified Ashworth Scale is a catch followed by minimal resistance through the remainder of the range, which is less than half of it. Grade 1 has a catch and release at the end of the range only. Grade 2 shows a more marked increase through most of the range, and grade 3 makes passive movement considerably difficult.
  56. The correct answer is B (Link the content to real tasks and allow practice with feedback in the actual work setting): Adults learn best when content is relevant to their work and they can practise with feedback in context. A single lecture, private reading or delayed practice offers little chance to apply or check skills.
  57. The correct answer is D (Trial a posterior walker, because it encourages upright trunk and hip extension during stepping): A posterior walker sits behind the child, encouraging a more upright trunk, hip extension and a forward gaze compared with an anterior walker, which promotes leaning. Lowering the handles increases forward lean, and wrist weights add load without improving alignment. Giving up walking removes a valuable activity when equipment adjustment can address the pattern.
  58. The correct answer is C (Groin pain reproduced by passive internal rotation, with loss of motion in that direction): Painful, limited internal rotation is one of the most sensitive findings in hip osteoarthritis, which restricts flexion, abduction and internal rotation in the capsular pattern. A positive straight leg raise and slump test implicate neural tissue. Lateral tenderness with resisted abduction fits greater trochanteric pain syndrome, and catching with full rotation suggests a flexor or labral problem.
  59. The correct answer is B (Partner with local health authorities and community groups to propose transportation or outreach clinic options): Advocacy works on the systems that create the barrier, and partnering with health authorities and community groups can produce transportation or outreach solutions. Asking clients to solve transportation themselves or referring them farther away keeps the barrier in place. Reducing visits to one a year gives up care rather than improving access.
  60. The correct answer is D (Gastrocnemius shortening, because dorsiflexion improves when the knee is flexed to 90 degrees): Gastrocnemius crosses the knee, so knee flexion slackens it and dorsiflexion increases from 3 to 17 degrees on the right, while the left is nearly equal in both positions. Soleus or capsular restriction would limit dorsiflexion in both positions, and bony impingement would produce anterior pain and a hard end-feel.
  61. The correct answer is B (A workplace label with the product name, handling precautions, and safety data sheet reference): A product moved into a secondary container needs a workplace label giving the product identifier, safe handling information and a reference to the safety data sheet. A photocopied supplier label may be out of date for a diluted product. A name and date or a pictogram alone leave out the handling information workers need.
  62. The correct answer is B (She may need more personal space, so offer a more comfortable distance): Leaning away and moving the chair as the physiotherapist sits close is a common sign that a person wants more personal space. Her polite, detailed answers show she is willing to talk and is following the questions, which argues against discomfort with the topic, fatigue or a hearing problem. Offering a more comfortable distance costs nothing and respects her. Changing the questions, adding a break or speaking louder from the same distance would not address the cue she is actually giving.
  63. The correct answer is A (Reduce impact load, manage the effusion, and regress to a lower activity level until swelling settles): A new moderate effusion with lost flexion after a load increase indicates the knee did not tolerate the progression, so load should be reduced and swelling managed before progressing again. There was no injury, so urgent imaging is not the first step, and heavier resistance would add more irritation. Mild swelling may be tolerated, but this effusion is moderate.
  64. The correct answer is A (Ask him to describe in his own words what will happen, through the interpreter): Having him restate the plan through the interpreter tests actual understanding, which valid consent requires. A nod can mean politeness rather than comprehension, an interpreter's signature does not show that he understood, and a translated form alone skips the conversation about his questions.
  65. The correct answer is B (Walk 15 minutes after dinner on Monday, Wednesday and Friday for the next two weeks): A specific, measurable, achievable and time-bound goal with a set cue and frequency gives the best chance of building a habit. Vague goals and motivation-dependent plans are hard to follow. A large daily target after months of inactivity is likely to fail and discourage her.
  66. The correct answer is C (Stage 1 pressure injury: intact skin with non-blanchable redness, so pressure relief should start immediately): Intact skin with redness that does not blanch under pressure is the definition of a stage 1 pressure injury, and offloading starts at once. Blanchable redness would indicate reactive hyperemia. Purple or maroon discoloration and partial skin loss describe more advanced injuries that are not present here.
  67. The correct answer is D (Decline, because the power imbalance persists after recent discharge and consent does not remove his duty): The power imbalance of a therapeutic relationship does not end the day a client is discharged, and regulatory standards restrict relationships with recent clients. Her consent, a signed statement or a transfer of her file does not remove the physiotherapist's duty to keep the boundary. Reporting her to the college is also wrong, because a former client making contact is not misconduct, and the duty to decline rests with him.
  68. The correct answer is B (About 55 percent, found by dividing the drop of 16 by the first total): The first six months total 29 falls and the last six total 13, a drop of 16, and 16 divided by 29 is about 55 percent. The 45 percent figure is the share remaining, 13 over 29. Dividing by the combined 42 gives about 38 percent, and comparing single months gives 80 percent, which ignores the other months.
  69. The correct answer is D (Intensity and repetition, increasing active, challenging practice well beyond twenty repetitions per session): Experience-dependent plasticity depends on high-dose, active and challenging practice, and about twenty mostly passive repetitions twice a week falls far short of the dose used in motor training. Reaching and grasping are real functional tasks, so the program already respects task specificity. The stem does not describe a carryover problem, and plasticity remains possible months after stroke, so neither transference nor a closed window explains the shortfall.
  70. The correct answer is D (Exercise at an intensity where she can hold a conversation, and stop with dizziness or pain): The talk test is a practical intensity guide in a healthy pregnancy, and warning symptoms such as dizziness or pain mean stopping and seeking advice. Pregnancy alters heart rate response, so a fixed pre-pregnancy number is unreliable. Activity is generally encouraged, and breathlessness is not a goal.
  71. The correct answer is C (Observe her performing the task with a client, then document the check before independent assignment): Competence is shown by performance, so the physiotherapist observes the task with a real client and documents the result. A verbal description, a certificate or a colleague's report each give partial information but do not show how she performs the task here.
  72. The correct answer is B (Abductor pollicis longus and extensor pollicis brevis, because both lie within the first dorsal compartment): A positive Finkelstein test with tenderness over the first dorsal compartment implicates abductor pollicis longus and extensor pollicis brevis, the tendons of De Quervain tenosynovitis. Extensor pollicis longus lies in the third compartment, and the radial wrist extensors lie in the second. Flexor carpi radialis sits on the palmar side and does not pass the radial styloid.
  73. The correct answer is A (Report the postural blood pressure drop to her physician and teach her slow position changes): A systolic drop of 28 mmHg with symptoms is orthostatic hypotension, a medical and medication-related cause of falls that must be communicated before exercise is intensified. Safe transfers can be taught meanwhile. Strength work, hip protectors and a home assessment are all useful later but do not address this reversible and urgent cause first.
  74. The correct answer is B (Name a custodian to hold the records securely, and notify clients how to request them): A named custodian is accountable for safeguarding the records and answering access requests, and telling clients how to reach that person keeps both privacy and access intact. A storage firm only holds boxes and cannot decide who may see a record. Destroying discharged charts or discarding originals after the lease ends ignores the legal retention period and the clients' continuing need for their records.
  75. The correct answer is A (It shows who viewed or changed each record and when, so inappropriate access can be detected): An audit trail is a detection tool: it lets the privacy officer see which user accessed or altered a chart and when, which exposes snooping and supports investigations. Backups, encryption and role-based restrictions are separate safeguards that prevent loss or access but do not record who actually did what.
Case CHB2

A 21-year-old rower started a new training block. Two days ago he completed 150 eccentric-emphasis leg presses in a single session. Today he reports severe bilateral thigh and calf pain, marked swelling, difficulty walking, and urine the colour of cola. His heart rate is 98 beats per minute and his temperature is 37.4 degrees Celsius.

  1. The correct answer is B (Cola-coloured urine, because it suggests myoglobin release from extensive muscle breakdown): Dark cola-coloured urine reflects myoglobinuria from muscle breakdown and is not part of ordinary soreness. Delayed onset soreness peaks at 24 to 72 hours and often affects both limbs, and a mild heart rate rise is nonspecific. Swelling and severe disability are also out of proportion to typical soreness.
  2. The correct answer is A (Stop exercise and arrange urgent medical assessment, including creatine kinase testing, because rhabdomyolysis is possible): Severe muscle pain and swelling with cola-coloured urine after unaccustomed eccentric exertion suggests exertional rhabdomyolysis, which can injure the kidneys and needs urgent blood tests and fluids under medical care. A referral timed within the week is the right idea at the wrong speed for a client who is already unwell. Light rowing or a recovery ride adds muscle breakdown, and dehydration alone does not explain dark urine with swollen, painful limbs. Cooling the legs does not treat the underlying problem.
  3. The correct answer is B (Increase load gradually while monitoring symptoms, because a rapid jump in eccentric volume risks recurrence): After exertional rhabdomyolysis the return to training should be graded, starting with low volume and intensity and progressing as symptoms, strength, and recovery allow. Pain is an unreliable guide, high-volume eccentric work is the usual trigger, and prolonged isometric-only training is unnecessary and deconditions him.
  4. The correct answer is A (Add short active breaks and recess play across each school day to approach 60 minutes): The guidelines call for about 60 minutes of moderate to vigorous activity every day, and spreading short bouts through the school day lets all children reach toward that target. A weekly class, even a long one, supplies activity on one day only. A single daily class of 30 minutes delivers only half the target and leaves the rest of the day inactive. Shifting the whole amount to parents does not answer the school's request to raise activity during the school day.
  5. The correct answer is D (Refer promptly for imaging, because an acute full-thickness cuff tear needs an early surgical opinion): Acute loss of active elevation with full passive range and an inability to hold the arm up after a fall suggests a traumatic full-thickness rotator cuff tear. Outcomes are better with early repair, so prompt physician referral for imaging is needed. Loading or waiting could allow tendon retraction.
  6. The correct answer is B (An early sign of conflict through withdrawal, calling for a private conversation about the concern): Withdrawal from meetings and shortened communication after a disagreement are common early signs that conflict is developing. A private, curious conversation is the constructive response. Treating it as agreement or as misconduct skips the chance to understand the concern and resolve it early.
  7. The correct answer is B (Why did you choose a 3 rather than a 0, and what would move it higher?): Asking why the score is not lower draws out the client's existing strengths, and asking what would raise it builds a plan from his own ideas. His importance is already high, so working on importance is a wasted step, and asking for a number of exercises moves to planning before confidence is addressed.
  8. The correct answer is C (Sputum that turned green and doubled in volume, with breathlessness worsening over the past two days): An exacerbation is an acute worsening of breathlessness, cough or sputum beyond usual daily variation, and a larger volume of purulent sputum together with worsening breathlessness over days fits that definition. A small drop in saturation by itself is within measurement variation. A cold without any change in her breathing and a single day of mild extra breathlessness with unchanged clear sputum do not yet show the sustained pattern that calls for prompt review.
  9. The correct answer is B (An occupational therapist, because hand function and classroom seating adaptations are within that profession's core role): Pencil grasp, hand endurance and seating in the classroom are core occupational therapy concerns for a child with cerebral palsy. A speech-language pathologist addresses language and communication rather than motor grip. A social worker and a psychologist may support the family, but neither directly adapts the child's hand function or seating.
  10. The correct answer is B (A boom-and-bust pattern, so introduce paced walking with fixed daily limits that increase gradually): The chart alternates big walking days with low-activity days, and pain is highest on the low-walking days that follow, which is the boom-and-bust pattern seen when activity is driven by how she feels. Pacing, with a set time limit that rises slowly, evens out activity and reduces flares. Stopping walking or discharging her for inconsistency does not address the pattern.
  11. The correct answer is A (0.70, dividing the higher ankle pressure by the higher arm pressure): The ankle-brachial index uses the higher of the two ankle pressures on the tested side divided by the higher of the two arm pressures. Here 98 divided by 140 is 0.70, which falls in the mild to moderate range of peripheral arterial disease. Using the lower pressure for either value shifts the result by a few hundredths and can change classification.
  12. The correct answer is D (Five holds of 45 seconds at about 70 percent of maximal effort, with rest between): The commonly used isometric dosage for tendinopathy pain is about five holds of 45 seconds at roughly 70 percent of maximal voluntary effort with rests between holds. Short maximal holds, very light repeated holds and very long low-effort holds do not produce the same analgesic effect.
  13. The correct answer is B (College standards and client safety still apply, so she should raise the concern with the employer): Registrants remain accountable to their regulatory college for safe, competent care and accurate records wherever they work. Employers set expectations, but those cannot override professional standards, so the correct step is to raise the concern and seek a workable solution. Documenting an objection and complying anyway leaves the risk to clients in place.
  14. The correct answer is C (Advise a medical assessment before he starts, because a yes response needs further screening): A yes to chest pain during activity on the PAR-Q+, with no physician review, needs further screening by a qualified health professional before he starts. A normal resting blood pressure does not exclude coronary disease, and a trial walk exposes him to the very activity that provokes the symptom before it is assessed. Signing the declaration only records his own responsibility and does not replace the screening the form calls for.
  15. The correct answer is A (Both her Berg and Timed Up and Go results fall in the fall risk range): A Berg score of 43 is below the commonly used cutoff of 45, and a Timed Up and Go time over about 13.5 seconds also suggests elevated fall risk, so both measures are in the risk range. Reading only one of them as abnormal ignores the other result in the table. Her pain rating did not change between screenings, so pain cannot account for the decline in all four performance measures.
  16. The correct answer is D (Contact the occupational therapist to agree on one recommendation before the next visit, then explain it): Both professions may comment on home safety, so the aim is a single, agreed recommendation. Talking with the occupational therapist resolves the discrepancy and spares the client the burden of choosing between expert opinions. Insisting, deferring without discussion, or leaving the decision to the client all leave the conflict unresolved.
  17. The correct answer is C (Scheduled short rests before fatigue builds, because preventing peaks preserves energy for the evening): The chart shows fatigue climbing steadily when he did not rest, but staying low on the day with three planned breaks. Energy conservation in multiple sclerosis relies on pacing: resting before fatigue peaks rather than after it. Pushing through fatigue or waiting until it is severe leads to long recovery times, and one midday break does not prevent the afternoon rise.
  18. The correct answer is C (Hold compression and report signs of spreading infection to the physician the same day): Spreading erythema, warmth, increasing pain and fever suggest cellulitis, so compression is held and the physician is told promptly. Continuing the routine plan delays treatment, and debridement does not treat spreading soft tissue infection. Extra compression over an infected, painful limb is inappropriate and does not explain the fever.
  19. The correct answer is D (Explore attendance barriers and adapt the program, because the dose received was too low for change): She attended only 6 of 24 sessions, so the minimal change in her scores reflects a low dose rather than failure to respond. Exploring why she missed sessions and adapting delivery gives the program a fair chance. Progressing intensity assumes she has been training, discharge is premature, and a medication review alone ignores the clear adherence problem.
  20. The correct answer is B (Frequent short walks with deep breathing, because early mobility lowers lung and clot complications): Obesity and sleep apnea raise the risk of pulmonary and thromboembolic complications after surgery, so frequent short walks with deep breathing are the priority. Walking at a gentle pace does not stress the staple line, so bedside exercises alone give up the benefit of early mobility. One daily walk is too infrequent to protect against those complications, and early abdominal resistance work is not appropriate over a healing surgical site.
  21. The correct answer is D (Speak directly to the client, use short segments, and pause for interpretation): Speaking directly to the client in short segments keeps the relationship with the client and lets the interpreter convey each point accurately. Addressing the interpreter excludes the client. Long blocks lead to omissions, and the interpreter should translate, not select content.
  22. The correct answer is B (Restore full passive knee extension and control effusion, because extension loss disrupts gait and quadriceps function): Early after ACL reconstruction, full extension and a calm knee are the priority because a persistent extension deficit alters gait and inhibits the quadriceps. Resisted curls stress a hamstring graft donor site, jogging is far too early, and forced flexion overpressure risks irritating the joint and graft.
  23. The correct answer is B (Pace and plan tasks with rests between them, and breathe out during the effortful part): Planning the routine and building in rests before she becomes breathless spreads the energy cost across the morning, and breathing out during the effortful part prevents breath holding and eases dyspnea. Resting only after breathlessness has set in lets her reach exhaustion before recovering. Breathing in sharply during effort reverses the pattern that reduces breathlessness. Adapting only the shower ignores that her exhaustion comes from the whole routine.
  24. The correct answer is C (Discuss the risks, document how the dual role will be managed, and keep boundaries clear): In small communities dual relationships are sometimes unavoidable, so the aim is to manage them transparently rather than ban them. Bartering treatment for services blurs the clinical relationship and secrecy hides the conflict. Open discussion and documentation protect both parties.

Practice Test 5 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is C (A capsular pattern, because external rotation is restricted most, then abduction, then internal rotation): The glenohumeral capsular pattern is external rotation most limited, then abduction, then internal rotation, usually with a firm end feel. Together with diabetes and insidious onset, this fits adhesive capsulitis. A different order of loss, or uneven loss with a soft or springy end feel, would point to cuff, bursal or instability problems instead.
  2. The correct answer is A (Hold limb exercises and arrange urgent medical assessment for possible thrombosis, because findings are suspicious): Unilateral calf swelling and ache after major lower limb surgery, with mild tachycardia, raises suspicion for deep vein thrombosis, so exercise and massage should stop until it is assessed. Vigorous calf work or massage could dislodge a clot, and expected postoperative swelling does not usually look markedly asymmetric.
  3. The correct answer is B (Stress urinary incontinence, caused by weak urethral support or sphincter function under raised abdominal pressure): Leakage with effort such as running, sneezing and jumping, without a preceding urge, is the classic story of stress incontinence. Urgency incontinence is preceded by a sudden need to void. Overflow incontinence produces continual dribbling with poor emptying, and functional incontinence reflects barriers to toileting rather than a bladder or sphincter problem.
  4. The correct answer is C (Keep the task personally for now, because the client's fluctuating blood pressure makes the response unpredictable): Delegation requires a stable and predictable client as well as a competent assistant. Fluctuating blood pressure and a recent fall make this client unsuitable even with a capable assistant, so the physiotherapist keeps the task. Experience and pairing address the assistant's competence, not the client's instability.
  5. The correct answer is B (Decline the trip, because accepting could reasonably appear to influence her future equipment recommendations): A gift of substantial value from a supplier creates a perceived conflict even when no commitment is stated, so the safest professional response is to decline it. Disclosing afterward or offsetting the value with a donation does not remove the appearance of influence. Her sincere intention to stay objective does not change how the gift looks to clients.
  6. The correct answer is C (Explain the treatment again in plain language and use teach-back to confirm real understanding): Consent is valid only when the client understands the nature, risks and alternatives, and trust or a signature does not show this. Plain language with teach-back checks comprehension. A family member cannot consent for a capable adult, and a longer form adds reading load for someone with limited literacy.
  7. The correct answer is D (Explore her concerns, then adapt standing exercises beside a sturdy counter to build her confidence): Fear of falling lowers self-efficacy and leads to avoidance, which in turn raises fall risk through weakness. Exploring the fear and graduating the challenge in a protected set-up builds confidence while keeping the balance training. Removing standing work or limiting her to supervised sessions reinforces avoidance and dependence.
  8. The correct answer is A (Use teach-back with an interpreter, include his wife, and give pictorial instructions with fewer exercises): Limited language and cognitive impairment require several adaptations together: an interpreter, teach-back to check understanding, a caregiver who can cue him, and a short pictorial program. Translating the same sheet leaves the volume and memory demands unchanged. Withdrawing the home program removes his chance to practise.
  9. The correct answer is A (The toes rest on the floor for balance only, carrying almost none of her body weight): Toe-touch weight-bearing means the foot contacts the floor for balance and proprioception while transmitting negligible load. A set percentage of body weight describes partial weight-bearing, and pain-guided loading describes weight-bearing as tolerated, both of which are different orders and would overload a fresh fixation.
  10. The correct answer is C (Phone promptly with a structured report of situation, background, assessment, and a request for urgent review): Tachycardia, low blood pressure, desaturation and new confusion suggest acute deterioration, so the physician needs a direct call in a structured format such as SBAR with a clear request. Charting alone or waiting for rounds delays care. A message is not urgent enough, and continuing treatment is unsafe.
  11. The correct answer is B (Upper trunk, because deltoid, infraspinatus and biceps draw mainly on the C5 and C6 roots): The weak muscles all draw on the C5 and C6 roots, and a forced sideways neck movement with a depressed shoulder stretches the upper trunk. A lateral cord lesion would spare abduction and external rotation, and a posterior cord lesion would weaken triceps, which are normal here. Lower trunk injury affects the hand muscles and C8 and T1 function.
  12. The correct answer is B (1420: Phoned Dr. Singh re wound drainage; told to hold exercise today, nursing check requested): A good entry records the time, who was contacted, what was reported and what was decided, so any team member can follow the plan. The alternatives omit the surgeon's instruction, misstate who made the call, or describe advice to the client without documenting communication with the team.
  13. The correct answer is A (Have her complete the PAR-Q+ follow-up questions that correspond to her one yes response): A yes on the general health questions directs the person to the follow-up questions that match that answer, and only a yes there leads to contact with a qualified exercise professional or physician. Referring her straight away skips that step, and repeating the whole form with a physician present adds a requirement the process does not have. Normal vital signs do not replace the screening questions, since they cannot show what the form is designed to find.
  14. The correct answer is D (Supervised, monitored exercise with education and risk factor counselling over weeks, then independent maintenance exercise): Outpatient cardiac rehabilitation combines supervised, monitored exercise with education and risk factor management over several weeks, and then prepares the person for a lifelong independent program. Supervision during the hospital stay is only the earlier inpatient phase and he has already gone home. Unsupervised booklet-based walking lacks the monitoring this phase provides, and delaying physical training for a year would waste the period when rehabilitation helps most.
  15. The correct answer is A (Knowledge of performance, because it describes how he moved rather than the outcome): Augmented feedback about the movement pattern itself, such as trunk lean or shoulder elevation, is knowledge of performance. Knowledge of results concerns the outcome, intrinsic feedback comes from the learner's own senses, and her comments follow each trial, so they are terminal rather than concurrent.
  16. The correct answer is A (Repeated prone press-ups, ten repetitions every two hours, while the symptoms continue to centralize): A directional preference is shown by centralization with extension, so the matching direction is repeated frequently. Flexion would be selected only if flexion centralized the pain. Bracing alone does not use the directional response and restricts movement unnecessarily.
  17. The correct answer is C (Complete supervised training with a skilled mentor, because competence must be shown before independent use): Adding a new technique requires education, supervised practice and demonstrated competence before independent use. Choosing low-risk clients still exposes them to an unskilled provider, and video review lacks feedback on hands-on skill. Employer approval does not establish that she is competent.
  18. The correct answer is C (A score of 4, so DVT is likely and urgent diagnostic imaging is needed): Major surgery within 12 weeks, localized deep-vein tenderness, calf swelling more than 3 cm greater than the other side and pitting edema confined to the symptomatic leg each score one point, giving 4, and no point is subtracted because no alternative diagnosis is as likely. In the two-level Wells model a score of 2 or more means DVT is likely, so she needs prompt imaging and medical review, not continued mobilization.
  19. The correct answer is B (Two education sessions and twelve supervised neuromuscular exercise sessions delivered over about six weeks): GLA:D combines patient education with supervised, individualized neuromuscular exercise, delivered as two education sessions and twelve exercise sessions over about six weeks. A program with different session numbers and a shorter span does not match the standard format. It is also not a single class with an unsupervised home plan, and exercise is a core component rather than something added only when manual therapy fails.
  20. The correct answer is B (Check the ingredients, apply a small test patch, and review the skin before wider use): A previous skin reaction to a similar product calls for checking the ingredients, applying a small test patch and inspecting the skin before wider use. Applying the full dose and waiting for a rash at home leaves him exposed to the reaction the history warns about. Changing brands assumes the cause without testing it, since the active drug may be the trigger. A thin layer over the painful area is still a full application, so it does not test his tolerance.
  21. The correct answer is D ($107, because the plan pays 80 percent of the $535 total and stays under its maximum): Total fees are $110 plus 5 times $85, which is $535. The plan pays 80 percent, or $428, which is below the $500 maximum, so the client owes the remaining $107. The cap only changes the result if 80 percent of the total exceeds $500.
  22. The correct answer is C (Set an aim for explaining treatment duration, test one small change, then measure again): The survey already identifies the specific weak area, so the cycle should start with a focused aim, a small test of change and re-measurement. A high overall score hides the gap, general training is not targeted, and collecting more data before acting delays an improvement the data already supports.
  23. The correct answer is C (Brief light walking intervals with rest breaks, progressed slowly according to symptoms and heart rate response): Reduced oxygen-carrying capacity limits tolerance, so short light intervals with rests are used and progressed by symptoms and heart rate. Exercise does not correct hemoglobin, and a long continuous bout is poorly tolerated at this level. Complete deferral is unnecessary because gentle activity is safe and prevents further deconditioning.
  24. The correct answer is A (Demonstrate the technique, then have each colleague practise it on a simulated client with feedback): A psychomotor skill is learned by seeing it done, practising it, and getting feedback in a safe setting. Slides, videos and descriptions give information but do not let learners practise or be corrected before they handle a real client.
  25. The correct answer is B (Squeeze test, compressing the tibia and fibula together at mid-calf while noting syndesmosis pain): The squeeze test loads the syndesmosis by compressing the fibula toward the tibia above the ankle, and pain at the distal joint suggests injury to the tibiofibular ligaments. The anterior drawer and talar tilt tests assess the anterior talofibular and calcaneofibular ligaments, and the Thompson test assesses Achilles tendon continuity.
  26. The correct answer is C (Altered mental status and seizures reflect inflammation of brain tissue, not only of the meninges): Encephalitis is inflammation of the brain parenchyma, so disorientation, personality change, seizures and sometimes focal deficits dominate, whereas meningitis mainly produces headache, fever and neck stiffness. Both can coexist as meningoencephalitis, and either needs urgent medical management. Fever and headache alone do not separate the two conditions.
  27. The correct answer is B (Slow her pace or pause with pursed-lip breathing until the rating returns toward 3 to 4): The modified Borg scale guides intensity, and a rating of 7 against a target of 3 to 4 means the workload is too high. Slowing or pausing with pursed-lip breathing brings the symptom back into the target range so training can continue. Ending the session is not needed unless symptoms or vital signs worsen. Maintaining the pace keeps the same workload that produced the high rating, and resuming at that pace after a vital sign check returns her to the load that caused it.
Case CHB3

Mrs. Dalila Haddad, 67, fell on an outstretched hand five weeks ago and had a distal radius fracture treated with closed reduction and a cast. The cast was removed today. She reports burning pain out of proportion to the injury. The hand is swollen, shiny and cooler than the other hand, and light touch from her sleeve is painful.

  1. The correct answer is C (Complex regional pain syndrome, because pain, skin and swelling changes exceed what the fracture alone explains): Burning pain out of proportion to the injury, edema, temperature and skin colour or texture change, and pain from light touch are the core features of complex regional pain syndrome, which often follows distal radius fracture and casting. Carpal tunnel syndrome would produce median nerve paresthesia without skin and temperature change. Delayed union and ordinary stiffness do not explain pain from light touch.
  2. The correct answer is A (Report the findings to her physician for review and keep movement gentle and within tolerance): Burning pain, swelling, shiny skin and sensitivity to light touch suggest complex regional pain syndrome, which needs medical diagnosis and early coordinated management, so the physician is told promptly while movement stays gentle. Home stretching with a four-week gap misses the window when early treatment helps most. Treating the swelling as simple overuse ignores the skin and pain changes, and further immobilization tends to worsen this condition.
  3. The correct answer is C (Graded desensitization and active use of the hand within tolerance, with swelling control and relaxed movement): Rehabilitation for this syndrome centres on graded exposure to touch, active use within tolerance, edema control and relaxation, so the nervous system relearns normal signals. Passive mobilization done to her rather than by her tends to provoke pain and does not retrain the sensitized system. Prolonged rest promotes stiffness and sensitization, and avoiding the hand entirely while strengthening the arm leaves the affected area untreated.
  4. The correct answer is A (Stop at 40 degrees or first firm resistance, with the goniometer fulcrum at the olecranon): Passive rotation is taken only to the surgeon's limit or first firm resistance, whichever comes first, and the goniometer fulcrum sits at the olecranon with the forearm as the moving arm. Going to end range would exceed the protocol, and active rotation loads the repair. Changing to 90 degrees of abduction alters the protected position.
  5. The correct answer is B (Thirteen hours daily, about 72 percent, so explore barriers and agree a plan to increase wear): Ninety-one hours divided by seven days is 13 hours, and 13 divided by 18 is about 72 percent of what was prescribed. Because bracing effectiveness depends on dose, the physiotherapist works with the girl on the heat and social barriers rather than accepting the shortfall. Changing the prescription belongs to the prescribing team.
  6. The correct answer is B (Legg-Calve-Perthes disease, avascular necrosis of the femoral head, usually seen in boys aged four to eight): A painless, gradually worsening limp with restricted abduction and internal rotation in a young boy is typical of Perthes disease. Transient synovitis is short-lived and follows a viral illness. Slipped epiphysis affects adolescents, and dysplasia is generally found in infancy, so neither matches this age and course.
  7. The correct answer is D (Correct the claims to match treatment time and notify the insurer, because billing must be accurate): Claims must accurately reflect the services and time actually provided, and the physiotherapist is personally responsible for the accuracy of billing in her name. Inaccurate billing can amount to fraud, so the existing claims are corrected and the insurer is told. Passing the decision to the owner leaves overbilling in place, and treating the claim as a payment for a visit misstates what was billed. Fixing only future claims leaves the earlier overbilling uncorrected.
  8. The correct answer is C (Ten years after the client's 18th birthday, because the client was a minor then): For a client who was a minor at the last entry, the retention period runs ten years from the client's 18th birthday, so this record is kept until about age 28. Counting from the last entry alone would allow destruction while the client is still a teenager. Parental consent authority ending at 18 is unrelated to how long the record must be kept.
  9. The correct answer is B (Return him to sitting or lying, report the drop, and progress gradually with the team): His systolic pressure fell from 126 to 96 mmHg within a minute of standing, a drop of 30, and his diastolic pressure fell from 77 to 60, with symptoms, which is consistent with orthostatic hypotension. He should be returned to a safe position, the finding reported, and mobilization progressed gradually. Continuing to stand or march risks a fall.
  10. The correct answer is C (Axial spondyloarthritis, such as ankylosing spondylitis, with an inflammatory back pain pattern): Young age, insidious onset, buttock pain, night or early-morning waking, prolonged morning stiffness and improvement with exercise but not rest are the hallmarks of inflammatory back pain. Disc herniation, stenosis and mechanical strain are all mechanical conditions that get worse with activity and ease with rest, the opposite of this pattern.
  11. The correct answer is B (Effective communication is part of the duty to accommodate, so the clinic should arrange the interpreter): For a person whose first language is a signed language, written English may not give equal access to exercise instructions and consent discussions. Communication supports are part of the duty to accommodate, and cost is weighed only against undue hardship, not convenience. Limiting the interpreter to one visit leaves later teaching and consent unsupported, and relying on a family member raises accuracy and privacy concerns.
  12. The correct answer is A (Exertional pain relieved by rest suggests cardiac ischemia, so stop testing and arrange urgent medical review): Pain that climbs with effort and eases within minutes of stopping is the classic exertional angina pattern, so testing stops and urgent medical review is arranged. Recognizing angina and then continuing to walk, even at lower intensity, still risks a cardiac event. Muscle fatigue does not produce chest tightness and arm heaviness that track exercise intensity, and a resting reading of 148/90 mmHg does not explain symptoms that appear only with effort.
  13. The correct answer is D (Rapid chest wall oscillation loosens and thins secretions so they travel toward the central airways): High-frequency chest wall oscillation delivers rapid pulses of pressure to the chest wall, producing oscillating airflow that reduces mucus viscosity and moves it toward the central airways, where a huff or cough clears it. It does not replace huffing or coughing. Stenting the airways open describes PEP therapy, and steady compression is not how the vest works.
  14. The correct answer is D (Sensory ataxia, because balance depends on vision when proprioceptive input from the legs is lost): A positive Romberg sign means balance is steady with vision but fails when vision is removed, which points to loss of position sense, as with diabetic neuropathy. Cerebellar ataxia causes unsteadiness with the eyes open as well. Vestibular and weakness problems do not produce this vision-dependent pattern.
  15. The correct answer is B (The Valsalva manoeuvre causes sharp spikes in blood pressure from raised intrathoracic pressure): Holding the breath while straining is the Valsalva manoeuvre, which raises intrathoracic pressure and produces sharp rises in arterial pressure, a particular concern in hypertension even when it is treated. Short lifting sets do not lower oxygen saturation to unsafe levels. A drop in venous return and a change in heart rate do occur, but they are not the effect that makes this habit most risky for a person with high blood pressure.
  16. The correct answer is A (Continuous walking for 10 to 20 minutes at an exertion rating of 11 to 13): Stable heart failure clients who tire after five minutes start with short continuous walking at a light to somewhat hard effort, then build duration and later intensity. A thirty minute session is too long for someone who fatigues at five minutes, and efforts rated 15 or interval work at that level are too demanding for a deconditioned start. Progression of time comes before intensity.
  17. The correct answer is C (Acknowledge the flare, temporarily return to the previous 15 minutes, and plan a slower increase): A flare after a rapid increase signals the progression was too fast, so the load is reduced to a tolerated level and increased more slowly, while she stays active. Complete rest leads to deconditioning and fear, and pushing through repeats the boom and bust pattern. Passive care does not build tolerance.
  18. The correct answer is B (By the end, each participant will demonstrate the three-step cue for safe sit-to-stand on a colleague): A measurable objective states what the learner will do, under what condition and by when, in terms an instructor can observe. A presenter's activity describes the teaching rather than the learning, and feeling confident cannot be observed directly. A satisfaction survey is measurable but captures opinion of the session, not whether the staff can now perform the skill.
  19. The correct answer is A (Stop the assessment and arrange urgent medical evaluation, because these symptoms exceed physiotherapy scope): New double vision and difficulty swallowing with neck pain may signal a vertebrobasilar or other neurological event, which needs urgent physician evaluation and is outside physiotherapy scope. Screening tests cannot clear a client who already has these symptoms, and soft tissue treatment or a delayed family physician visit leaves a possible emergency unaddressed. The assessment is stopped and urgent referral arranged.
  20. The correct answer is C (Report objective findings, label the client's statements as reported, and avoid opinions about fault): Reports must be objective, factual and verifiable, with the client's statements labelled as reported. Advocacy, speculation about motives and rounding of measurements all compromise accuracy and the physiotherapist's credibility.
  21. The correct answer is D (Raise speed or incline modestly, since heart rate stays below the limit with light effort): His heart rate stays under the prescribed ceiling and he rates effort as light, so a modest increase in speed or incline is appropriate. Reducing duration misreads a steady plateau as excess intensity, and holding the program unchanged ignores the room he has to progress. Pushing to the limit in one large step adds more load than a gradual change for a client with hypertension.
  22. The correct answer is D (Stabilize the scapula and passively elevate the internally rotated arm, positive if familiar pain appears): The Neer test passively elevates the arm in forward flexion with internal rotation while the scapula is held still, and it is positive if familiar pain is reproduced. Resisted thumb-down elevation describes the empty can test, flexing the shoulder and elbow to 90 degrees then internally rotating describes the Hawkins-Kennedy test, and slowly lowering the abducted arm describes the drop arm test.
  23. The correct answer is B (It sounds like losing that ability has been very painful and frustrating for you): Reflecting feelings means naming the emotion behind the words so the client feels understood. The statement beginning with 'at least' minimizes her loss, the advice about small tasks moves to problem solving too soon, and the question about home exercises redirects to information gathering and skips the emotion.
  24. The correct answer is A (Pain up to about 5/10 is acceptable if it settles by the next morning): The widely used pain monitoring model accepts exercise pain up to about 5/10 provided it settles by the next morning and does not trend upward over weeks. Demanding zero pain is unnecessarily restrictive, and pain that stays high or keeps rising suggests the load is too great.
  25. The correct answer is C (Return her to sitting, stop standing practice and report the orthostatic drop to the physician): A symptomatic fall of 28 mmHg systolic on standing meets the criteria for orthostatic hypotension, which is common in Parkinson disease and raises fall risk. She should be seated, standing practice stopped and the finding reported so medications can be reviewed. Slowing down or resuming after a short rest continues exposure to the problem, and charting the reading alone leaves the physician unaware.
  26. The correct answer is C (Apply a brace or wrap, then begin gentle active ankle movement and walking as tolerated): With a negative Ottawa assessment and the ability to bear weight, the injury is managed with external support and early active movement. A cast delays recovery, forceful end-range stretching irritates injured ligament, and manipulation does not realign a sprained joint. Bruising alone does not require immobilization.
  27. The correct answer is B (Remove gloves and gown, clean hands, leave the room, then remove eye protection and mask): The most contaminated items, the gloves and gown, are removed first and hands are cleaned before touching anything else. The mask and eye protection are removed outside the room, away from the client, so that the face is not touched while contaminated items remain on the body. Removing everything in the hallway carries contamination out of the room.
  28. The correct answer is B (Patient-rated severity of nine common symptoms, each scored from 0 to 10 today): The Edmonton Symptom Assessment System asks the patient to rate nine symptoms, such as pain, tiredness, breathlessness and well-being, from 0 to 10. It is not a performance scale, a caregiver tool or a summed quality-of-life score, so scores guide symptom management rather than prognosis.
  29. The correct answer is D (Date, equipment identifier, test or service done, findings, and the person who performed it): A maintenance log shows that a specific device was inspected or serviced, what was found and who did the work. A use log records treatments, not the unit's condition. Purchase and warranty details are asset records. A schedule of due dates helps planning but does not prove that any test was done or what it found.
  30. The correct answer is D (Stop exercise, seat him, and give fast-acting sugar by mouth, rechecking glucose after fifteen minutes): Glucose below 4.0 mmol/L with symptoms is treated as hypoglycemia, and a client who is awake and able to swallow should stop exercise and receive fast-acting carbohydrate. Glucose is rechecked in about fifteen minutes. A protein snack acts too slowly, waiting lets it worsen, and withholding food is reserved for someone who cannot swallow safely.
  31. The correct answer is A (Anterior disc displacement with reduction, shown by a reciprocal click and a deviation that corrects): A reciprocal click on opening and closing, with near-normal opening and a deviation that straightens out, indicates the disc is displaced at rest and recaptured on opening. Without reduction, opening is limited and deviation persists. Hypermobility shows excessive translation, and degenerative disease is marked by crepitus rather than a click.
  32. The correct answer is B (Gains plateaued from week five, so more sessions need a revised plan with new measurable goals): Pain fell from 7 to 4 and disability from 42 to 26 percent, but both measures stayed flat from week five. A funder report should show real improvement, state the plateau honestly, and justify any further care with a changed plan and goals. Claiming continued gains or no change misrepresents the data.
  33. The correct answer is B (Grade II partial tear, because bruising with reduced but present strength shows incomplete fibre disruption): A grade II strain is a partial tear with bruising, pain, and measurable loss of strength but some preserved function and no palpable gap. Grade I involves little fibre damage with minimal strength loss, and grade III is a complete rupture with a defect and near-absent function. An avulsion would produce a proximal defect and marked weakness.
  34. The correct answer is C (Human rights law requires accommodation short of undue hardship, so other room options must be explored): Providers of services, including private clinics, must accommodate disability up to the point of undue hardship, which is a high standard. That requires exploring practical alternatives such as a ground-floor room, a shared accessible space or home visits. Building age and phase-in timelines do not excuse inaction, a single alternative adopted without looking at others does not meet the duty, and the law does not demand the costliest fix.
  35. The correct answer is C (Plus one, because the client passed 300 metres but did not reach 400 metres): Goal attainment scaling assigns the highest level whose description has been fully reached. The client surpassed the +1 descriptor of 300 m but fell short of the 400 m descriptor for +2. Extra distance beyond the expected outcome is scored, and rounding to a higher level is not permitted.
  36. The correct answer is B (The physiotherapist remains accountable for the assessment, plan, supervision and the care the assistant provides): Delegating a task does not delegate accountability. The physiotherapist who assigns the work remains responsible for the assessment, the plan, the choice of task, supervision and the care that results. Splitting the duty between plan and performance, shifting supervision to a manager, or sharing it equally with an employer all move part of the registrant's professional responsibility to someone else.
  37. The correct answer is B (Reduced breath sounds with a dull percussion note and diminished chest expansion on the left): Collapsed lung tissue is airless and transmits breath sounds poorly, so breath sounds are reduced, percussion is dull and chest expansion is diminished on that side. A hyperresonant note with a shift away suggests a tension pneumothorax, and a stony dull note with a shift away suggests a large effusion. Wheezes with normal percussion point to airway obstruction.
  38. The correct answer is C (Pause the case discussion, ask the person to identify themselves, and remove them if unauthorized): An unidentified listener could receive personal health information without authority. Pausing and verifying who is present protects privacy while allowing the meeting to resume with only authorized people. Continuing, ending without explanation, or giving less detail while the person stays on the line does not resolve the exposure.
  39. The correct answer is C (Immobilize the wrist and repeat imaging, because the scaphoid has a fragile retrograde blood supply): Early radiographs miss a large share of scaphoid fractures, and snuffbox tenderness with painful axial thumb compression keeps the suspicion high. Retrograde blood supply makes the proximal pole prone to nonunion and avascular necrosis, so protection and repeat or advanced imaging are needed. Loading or treating it as a sprain risks displacement.
  40. The correct answer is A (A 3 cm increase, below the expected 5 cm, which suggests reduced lumbar flexion): The marks start 15 cm apart, so a reading of 18 cm is an increase of 3 cm. A healthy adult usually gains more than 5 cm on the modified Schober, so 3 cm suggests restricted lumbar flexion, consistent with spinal involvement. The 18 cm figure is the final distance, not the change, and the test measures lumbar flexion rather than chest expansion.
  41. The correct answer is C (Guidelines based on systematic reviews rank above single trials, which rank above case reports): Guidelines built on systematic reviews pool many studies and sit highest, a single randomized trial comes next, and a case report describing one client sits lowest because it cannot show cause and effect. A trial does not outrank a guideline built on many trials, and a case report is a weaker design than a controlled trial even though it is detailed. Recency does not change design rank.
  42. The correct answer is B (The post may identify the client, so confidentiality could be breached without consent): Details such as a sport, an injury and a local community can identify a client even without a name, and the physiotherapist remains responsible for confidentiality. Marketing and advertising rules are secondary, and the follower's comment does not remove her own duty.
  43. The correct answer is D (Joint line discomfort or a catching sensation, because rotation under load pinches a torn meniscus): The Thessaly test loads the menisci with rotation in weight-bearing, and a positive response is joint line pain, locking or catching. Anterior pain suggests a patellofemoral source, giving way suggests ligament insufficiency, and pes anserine pain would be tender below the joint line on palpation.
  44. The correct answer is A (Refer for medical investigation, because worsening night pain despite improving activity pain suggests a non-mechanical cause): Activity pain is easing while night pain climbs, which is the reverse of the expected mechanical response, and new functional decline with poor appetite adds systemic warning signs. These findings call for medical investigation now. Waiting two weeks delays a possible systemic cause, a further mechanical search ignores the pattern, and a dietitian referral treats the appetite without explaining the pain.
  45. The correct answer is C (Help her roll onto her left side and monitor her until symptoms resolve): The presentation suggests supine hypotension from the gravid uterus compressing the inferior vena cava, so turning to the left side relieves the compression. Continuing the exercise or staying supine worsens it. Leg elevation does not remove the compression, and further supine work should be avoided.
  46. The correct answer is C (Symptoms suggest a possible cardiac source, so urgent medical assessment is needed before musculoskeletal treatment begins): Exertion-related shoulder and jaw ache with sweating and nausea, relieved by rest, in a client with a major risk factor fits cardiac ischemia. A full, pain-free shoulder examination does not support a local cause. Radiculopathy and cuff overload would be provoked by neck or arm movement, and gallbladder referral would follow meals rather than exertion.
  47. The correct answer is A (Give one short step at a time using simple words and gentle gestures): People with moderate dementia process one simple instruction at a time, and gestures add meaning. Repeating the full explanation keeps the same overload, a phone call does not help at the moment of the transfer, and abandoning the task may be unnecessary if the instructions can simply be simplified.
  48. The correct answer is C (Hold the group session and advise same-day medical assessment, because infection can progress quickly while immunosuppressed): Immunosuppressive drugs blunt fever and other signs, so a low-grade temperature with a new cough may signal serious infection. He should not exercise in a group and needs prompt medical assessment. Treating individually or waiting a week delays care, and attributing the fever to steroids falsely reassures him.
  49. The correct answer is C (Passive shoulder motion within surgeon limits, because active cuff contraction stresses the healing repair): During the protection phase the repaired tendon must not be loaded by the cuff muscles, so only passive motion inside the surgeon's range is appropriate. Pulleys and active elevation both require cuff contraction, and resisted rotation adds load to a repair that has not yet healed to bone.
  50. The correct answer is A (Left unilateral spatial neglect, because attention to the left is impaired despite intact vision when cued): She sees and names objects on the left once cued, so the visual field is intact, and she drifts back to the right without prompting, which points to an attention problem. A person with a true field cut learns to turn the head spontaneously. Apraxia and extinction do not explain ignoring half of the visual environment.
  51. The correct answer is C (Supraspinatus tendon, because elevation in the scapular plane with internal rotation stresses it most): Resisted elevation in the scapular plane with the thumbs pointing down is the empty can position, which loads supraspinatus most. Subscapularis and infraspinatus are tested in rotation positions rather than elevation, and the biceps is better tested with supinated forearm positions.
  52. The correct answer is A (Stop the session and arrange urgent medical assessment, because he may have a major thigh bleed): A swollen, tense, darkly bruised and increasingly painful thigh after a fall in a person on an anticoagulant suggests ongoing bleeding, with risk of compartment syndrome and major blood loss. Same-day medical assessment is needed. Waiting a week, applying cold and compression, or marking the bruise and carrying on all leave possible hemorrhage unassessed.
  53. The correct answer is D (Teach protected weight-bearing with a walker and avoid resisted or twisting loads on the left hip): A lytic lesion in the femoral neck with load-related pain carries a high risk of pathological fracture, so protected weight-bearing with a walker and avoiding resisted or twisting loads is safest until the oncologist decides on fixation. A cane gives too little offloading, and muscle activation adds joint force across the lesion. Strict bed rest causes deconditioning without being needed.
  54. The correct answer is A (Seek confidential care now, since early treatment protects both her health and her practice): Early confidential care protects both the clinician and her clients, and seeking treatment for grief does not by itself end a career. Notifying the college is not required when practice is unaffected. Informal talk with a colleague or friends may help but does not replace assessment of persistent low mood. Waiting for performance to decline lets the condition worsen.
  55. The correct answer is A (Withhold NMES and notify the physician urgently, because deep vein thrombosis may be present): A swollen, warm, tender calf with shortness of breath after joint surgery raises concern for deep vein thrombosis and possible pulmonary embolism. Muscle stimulation could dislodge a clot, so it is withheld and the physician told urgently. Moving the stimulation to another muscle group, treating with ice, or reporting later does not address the immediate risk.
  56. The correct answer is C (Walk 50 metres with a four-wheeled walker without help to reach the bathroom within two weeks): A SMART goal names a measurable behaviour, a condition and a time frame. Only the correct goal gives a distance, a device, the level of help and a deadline. The wrong goals describe general improvement, an unmeasured increase or a vague outcome that cannot be scored as met or not met.
  57. The correct answer is C (Demonstrate it, then let each assistant practise with feedback, because rehearsal builds skill and confidence): Adults change practice when they rehearse a skill and receive feedback on their own performance. Reading policy, handouts and videos pass on information but give no chance to correct errors in technique. With a painful, weak shoulder, handling has to be physically practised under observation.
  58. The correct answer is B (Below the level of the bladder and off the floor, with tubing free of kinks): The bag stays below bladder level so urine drains by gravity and does not flow back, and off the floor to prevent contamination. Tubing should be unkinked and secured. Raising the bag causes backflow and infection risk.
  59. The correct answer is D (Keep supine active-assisted elevation, because hitching shows poor cuff control for upright lifting): The upward hitch means the cuff cannot yet control the arm against gravity, so loading in the gravity-reduced supine position is safer and builds control first. Resistance bands are premature, and wall slides still demand upright cuff control. Going back to the sling is unnecessary because pain is low and the surgeon has cleared active-assisted motion.
  60. The correct answer is B (Decline to alter the findings, and report her measured lifting capacity and job demands): A report must reflect the physiotherapist's own findings and cannot be changed to suit a third party. Changing the wording or deleting the job demands misleads the reader, and inviting the insurer to rewrite it hands over control of a professional document.
  61. The correct answer is A (Pain-limited walking and gentle isometrics now, postponing deep stretching and heat while the tissue is irritable): In the first days after a muscle strain, early pain-limited movement and isometrics promote healing without overloading the repair. Aggressive stretching and heat can provoke further bleeding and pain. Prolonged avoidance of contraction slows recovery, and strict hourly icing with no walking is not supported by evidence.
  62. The correct answer is A (Swim only with a trained observer aware of her epilepsy, because seizures in water risk drowning): Swimming is encouraged in epilepsy, but a seizure in water can cause drowning even near the edge. She needs someone who knows about her condition and can watch and reach her quickly. A lane beside the wall does not help a person who is unresponsive, a vest does not reliably keep the airway clear, and a lifeguard watching many swimmers does not know to watch for seizures.
  63. The correct answer is C (Bag it where used without shaking it, hold it away from your clothing, then launder it): Soiled linen is handled with minimal agitation so that microorganisms are not released into the air, bagged at the point of use, and kept away from the body and clothing on the way to laundering. Spraying does not replace laundering, and storing or shaking it spreads contamination.
  64. The correct answer is B (Lower him safely into the chair or to lying, then raise his legs and monitor): These are signs of impending syncope from orthostatic hypotension after prolonged bed rest. The priority is preventing a fall, so he is lowered to a chair or the floor and his legs are raised to restore blood flow to the brain. Staying upright or walking on risks fainting and injury, and checking his pressure can wait until he is safe.
  65. The correct answer is B (Stop testing and refer for imaging, because an empty end-feel after trauma suggests possible fracture): An empty end-feel means pain, not tissue, is limiting the movement, and with a recent fall and severe pain it raises concern for fracture or another serious injury. Further passive testing or mobilization could cause harm, so imaging through the physician pathway comes before any treatment. Exercise to prevent adhesions is premature until a fracture is excluded.
  66. The correct answer is C (Report the falling trend to her insulin prescriber, because dose changes are outside physiotherapy scope): Glucose after exercise has fallen from 6.4 to 3.8 mmol/L while insulin stayed the same, so the pattern needs review by the person who prescribes her insulin. A physiotherapist can adjust exercise but cannot change medication or tell a client to self-manage the gap. Dietitian input may help later, but it does not replace medical review of the trend.
  67. The correct answer is C (Decide from clinical findings, discuss them with her parents, and inform the coach only with consent): Swelling and pain on hopping show the ankle is not ready, and the decision should rest on clinical findings rather than team pressure. She is a minor, so her parents are involved, and the coach receives information only with consent. Bracing does not resolve the unhealed injury, and sharing findings without consent breaches privacy.
  68. The correct answer is B (Avoid heat and forceful techniques over the spine, and consult the oncology team first): Heat increases blood flow and may promote tumour growth, and forceful spinal techniques risk a pathological fracture through weakened bone. Low doses or avoiding the vertebrae directly do not remove these risks over a region with metastases. The oncology team should guide safe options such as gentle active exercise and pain management.
  69. The correct answer is D (Long-sitting, head turned 45 degrees right, then lowered quickly to the right Dix-Hallpike position): The Epley maneuver begins like the diagnostic test: sitting with the head turned 45 degrees toward the affected right ear, then moved quickly into the right Dix-Hallpike position with the neck slightly extended. Turning the head to the unaffected side treats the wrong canal. Lowering slowly with the head level or turning 90 degrees does not place the posterior canal in the plane needed to move debris.
  70. The correct answer is A (Locked cabinets in a room with controlled key access and a sign-out log for charts): Safeguards for paper records combine physical barriers with accountability: locked storage, limited key access and a log of who removed each chart. A key left near reception, unlocked cabinets, or reliance on a cleaner's agreement leaves charts reachable by people with no need to see them. Safeguards must work when staff are not watching.
  71. The correct answer is B (Ortolani test, positive: a dislocated hip has been reduced into the acetabulum): The Ortolani maneuver abducts and lifts the thigh to relocate a dislocated femoral head, producing a palpable clunk of reduction. The Barlow maneuver adducts and pushes backward to provoke dislocation of a located hip. A true clunk is a positive finding and not a benign click, so urgent referral for ultrasound is indicated.
  72. The correct answer is C (Left hand, so it advances with the painful right leg and offloads that hip): A cane is held on the side opposite the painful or weak leg and moves together with that leg, which shifts load away from the hip and mimics normal reciprocal arm and leg timing. Holding it on the painful side removes this offloading effect. Hand dominance matters less than the offloading mechanics.
  73. The correct answer is D (Express condolences, rebook with flexible timing, and ask about transportation barriers rather than discharging her): Culturally safe care recognizes mourning and ceremony as legitimate priorities and uses policy flexibly so that it does not become a barrier to care. Condolences, flexible rebooking and an inquiry about travel solve the real problem. Discharge or a warning punishes her for a funeral, and moving to home exercises alone assumes what she can manage without her input.
  74. The correct answer is C (Weigh whether attending creates a dual relationship that could compromise objectivity or the client's care): Boundary decisions rest on the physiotherapist's own judgment about power, objectivity and the risk of harm in a dual relationship. Colleges do not forbid every social contact, so an automatic refusal or an automatic acceptance both replace judgment with a rule. A public setting does not remove the issue, and discharge does not always end the duty to protect the former client.

Practice Test 6 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is A (Thirty-degree tilted side-lying, supported with pillows behind the back and between the knees): A thirty-degree tilt places load on the fleshy buttock rather than the sacrum or the trochanter. Full side-lying loads the trochanter, and any supine position keeps pressure on the sacrum, with a raised head of bed adding shear. Floating the heels protects the heels but does nothing for the sacral wound.
  2. The correct answer is D (Meet the mandatory reporting duty to the college while supporting the client through the process): Regulated health professionals have a duty to report suspected sexual abuse of a client by another regulated professional.
