The client's wish for no action does not remove that duty, though she should be supported and told what will happen. Waiting, confronting or leaving it to the client leaves other clients at risk.
  3. The correct answer is A (A 24-hour postural plan with supported sitting, lying and standing, plus regular hip imaging): Children at GMFCS level V are at high risk of hip migration, which is often painless early on, so routine hip surveillance and a 24-hour postural management plan are advised. Supporting the hips in abduction across sitting, lying and standing limits asymmetry. Stretching alone does not control migration, prolonged upright sitting without support worsens asymmetry, and waiting for pain delays detection.
  4. The correct answer is C (Grade II, neck complaint with musculoskeletal signs such as reduced range of motion and point tenderness): Neck complaints plus musculoskeletal signs, such as restricted motion and tenderness, define grade II. Grade I has no physical signs, grade III includes neurological deficit, and grade IV requires a fracture or dislocation. Her negative C-spine rule and normal neurological findings exclude the higher grades.
  5. The correct answer is D (Wash with soap and running water, because alcohol does not reliably kill the spores): Alcohol-based hand rub does not reliably destroy Clostridioides difficile spores, so soap and running water are used to physically remove them. Gloves reduce but do not eliminate contamination, so clean-looking hands still need washing. Repeating the rub or adding a wipe does not solve the problem and wipes are not meant for skin.
  6. The correct answer is B (Say honestly that it may feel tight, and teach him a simple breathing game): Children trust clinicians who describe sensations truthfully, and false reassurance damages that trust for later sessions. Pairing an honest preview with a coping tool prepares him. Hiding the sensation or sending the parent out removes support and does not address the honesty problem.
  7. The correct answer is D (Stop the exercise, keep her with staff, and arrange urgent same-day obstetrical assessment): New hypertension with headache, visual disturbance and facial swelling are warning signs of pre-eclampsia, which can progress quickly to seizures. She needs urgent same-day assessment and must not exercise or be left alone. Resting briefly, treating this as a routine headache, or waiting a week all delay emergency care.
  8. The correct answer is D (Focal seizure with impaired awareness, supported by the aura, automatisms and postictal confusion): An aura, then reduced awareness with automatisms such as lip smacking and picking, followed by prolonged confusion, is the typical sequence of a focal seizure with impaired awareness, often arising in the temporal lobe. Absence seizures last seconds, begin and end abruptly, and leave no confusion. Myoclonic seizures are brief jerks with preserved awareness.
  9. The correct answer is A (113 to 138, calculated as 70 to 85 percent of a maximum of 162): Age-predicted maximum heart rate is 220 minus 58, or 162 beats per minute. Seventy percent of 162 is about 113 and 85 percent is about 138. Using 200 minus age, or a different percentage band, gives zones that are too low or too high for the intensity the physiotherapist chose.
  10. The correct answer is C (Assess her back on clinical findings and discuss weight only if invited, to build trust): Weight stigma delays care and erodes trust, so assessment should follow the presenting complaint and clinical findings, with weight discussed when she invites it. Raising weight uninvited or making a weight plan a condition of assessment repeats what kept her away. Ignoring the topic and giving generic advice skips a proper assessment, and body size does not prevent a safe examination.
  11. The correct answer is C (Train volunteer leaders and embed the program in the centre's regular schedule with shared costs): Sustainability comes from local capacity: trained volunteer leaders, a place in the centre's regular schedule and costs shared among partners. Plans that depend on uncertain funding, on fee income from older adults or on outside research staff end when that support ends. The centre already offers free space and willing volunteers, which a lasting program should build on.
  12. The correct answer is D (Previous cancer diagnosis, because it is the most predictive single history item for spinal malignancy): A prior cancer diagnosis is the strongest single history predictor of spinal malignancy, and breast cancer commonly spreads to the spine. Night pain and weight loss raise concern, but each is less specific on its own because benign and inflammatory conditions also cause them. Age over 50 adds some risk but has little predictive power by itself.
  13. The correct answer is B (Disclose the error promptly, apologize, and report it through facility processes): Canadian patient safety standards and professional codes support prompt, honest disclosure with an apology, plus reporting so the system can learn. Delaying, leaving it undiscussed or waiting for management approval withholds information the patient is entitled to and tends to damage trust more than the error itself.
  14. The correct answer is D (A 73-year-old with rheumatoid arthritis, taking prednisone and a biologic, with a prosthetic knee): Septic arthritis risk rises with prosthetic joints, existing joint disease, older age and immunosuppression from corticosteroids or biologic drugs. The 73-year-old combines all of these. A recent joint injection, a remote hip replacement and diabetes each add some risk but fewer factors, so each ranks below the client with several overlapping risks.
  15. The correct answer is C (Arrange urgent medical assessment, because these central signs may indicate a posterior circulation stroke): Direction-changing nystagmus, severe truncal instability without hearing loss and vascular risk factors are central warning signs consistent with a cerebellar or brainstem stroke. This requires emergency medical assessment before any rehabilitation. Exercises, canalith repositioning or rest at home would delay time-sensitive care.
  16. The correct answer is B (Hormonal laxity and increased load can stress pelvic joints, and symptoms often improve after delivery): Pelvic girdle pain in pregnancy is linked to hormonal ligament laxity, a shifting center of mass and added joint load, and most women improve after birth. With no trauma, fever or neurological signs, there is no reason to suspect damage or autoimmune disease. Core weakness alone does not explain it, and intensive loading may aggravate symptoms.
  17. The correct answer is A (Lower the screen so its top edge is near eye level, and keep wrists neutral): A screen with its top edge near eye level avoids sustained neck extension, and a keyboard height that keeps the wrists straight reduces tendon strain. Tilting the screen up or resting the wrists in extension keeps both faulty postures. Raising the seat or moving the screen closer does not correct the height mismatches observed.
  18. The correct answer is D (Frequency near 50 Hz, strong visible contraction, about 10 seconds on and 50 seconds off): Strengthening with NMES needs a frequency around 50 Hz and an intensity high enough to produce a strong tolerated contraction, with long rest periods such as a 1 to 5 on-off ratio to limit fatigue. A short rest period causes rapid fatigue, and low frequencies or sensory-only intensity do not produce strengthening contractions.
  19. The correct answer is A (A written home maintenance program and clear advice on when to seek reassessment sooner): When goals are met and function is symmetrical, discharge is appropriate if the client leaves with a maintenance program and knows when to return. Extra visits, routine orthoses or months of bracing add cost and dependence without evidence of benefit in a recovered ankle.
  20. The correct answer is C (A community group program that combines balance and strength training, led by a qualified exercise instructor): Fall prevention in older adults is best supported by ongoing balance and strength training, and a community group program provides it with social support and lower cost once goals are met. Indefinite one-to-one care is not needed. Water classes and walking alone do not train balance in a targeted way.
  21. The correct answer is C (Seat her safely and alert the nurse now, because fever after chemotherapy is an emergency): A fever in a patient likely to be neutropenic after chemotherapy is a medical emergency because sepsis can develop within hours. Exercise does not explain a temperature of 38.4 with tachycardia and lowish pressure. Delaying notification to rest, walk or warm her risks serious harm.
  22. The correct answer is D (Limit beer and organ meats, drink water regularly and lose weight gradually, alongside prescribed medication): Cutting beer and organ meats, staying hydrated and losing weight gradually all lower the chance of attacks, and the prescribed medication should continue as directed. Spirits also raise uric acid, rapid weight loss can trigger flares, and stopping medication when attacks pause removes the protection against recurrence.
  23. The correct answer is D (Stabilize the tibia and pull the calcaneus forward with the ankle in slight plantarflexion): The anterior drawer test stresses the anterior talofibular ligament with the tibia fixed and the foot translated forward in slight plantarflexion, where that ligament is most taut. Dorsiflexion locks the talus in the mortise. Inverting in neutral is a talar tilt that stresses the calcaneofibular ligament, and the squeeze test checks the syndesmosis.
  24. The correct answer is A (Three charts lacked pain ratings, which delayed planning. What made them hard to complete this week?): Good feedback names the specific behaviour and its effect without judging the person, then invites the graduate's view so the cause can be found. A directive with no question gives specifics but no dialogue. A vague comment about caseload does not identify the problem, and a general remark about new graduates leaves the real issue unnamed.
  25. The correct answer is A (Right hand, advancing the cane together with the left leg, because it offloads the painful limb): A cane held opposite the painful limb moves with that limb, so the force through the cane widens the base and reduces the joint load through the affected knee. Holding it on the painful side shifts the body toward that side and increases load. Moving it with the opposite leg loses the offloading effect.
  26. The correct answer is C (Omit ultrasound over the lumbar and pelvic area, using positioning, exercise and a support belt instead): Therapeutic ultrasound is avoided over the lumbar spine, abdomen and pelvis in pregnancy because the effects on the fetus are unknown and thermal or mechanical effects cannot be ruled out. Pulsed mode or lower doses do not remove this precaution. Positioning, exercise, education and a support belt address her pain safely.
  27. The correct answer is B (Fix her gaze on the card while turning the head side to side, keeping it clear): In the x1 viewing exercise the target stays still while the head moves, and the patient keeps the letter in focus to challenge the vestibulo-ocular reflex. Moving the target against the head is the x2 version, which is a progression. Eye-only movement trains saccades, and closing the eyes removes the visual error signal that drives adaptation.
  28. The correct answer is A (Long-lasting pain can come from an overprotective nervous system even after tissues have healed): Pain neuroscience education explains that pain is produced by the nervous system as protection and can persist, and become more sensitive, after tissues heal. Mild age-related imaging findings are common without pain. This message reduces fear and supports gradual return to movement, which avoiding bending would block.
  29. The correct answer is D (Remove the post, then inform the client and review consent practices before posting similar content): A visible face can identify a client, and posting without consent breaches privacy and professional standards. Restricting the audience or relying on a colleague's view still leaves the image online without the client's agreement. Removal, disclosure to the client and a better consent process address the harm.
  30. The correct answer is A (Test sensation over the lumbar skin and inspect it often, because impaired sensation hides early burns): Diabetes can impair temperature and pain sensation, so sensation is tested at the treatment site and the skin inspected during the application. Relying on his report alone depends on the sensation that may be reduced, and towel layers help but do not replace checks. Heat is not contraindicated when sensation is confirmed, and avoiding it for glucose reasons is not supported.
  31. The correct answer is A (Walk to moderate claudication pain, rest until it eases, and repeat for 30 to 60 minutes): Supervised programs that take the calf to moderate pain, followed by rest and repeated bouts, give the greatest gains in walking distance. Stopping at the first twinge gives too little stimulus, severe pain is unnecessary, and pain-free cycling does not train the ischemic response.
  32. The correct answer is D (To protect the public by setting standards and holding registrants accountable for safe, ethical care): Colleges exist to serve and protect the public interest through registration, practice standards, quality assurance and a complaints and discipline process. Advocacy for members, fee negotiation and professional representation belong to the professional association. Program accreditation and examination content are handled by other national bodies, and liability protection comes from an insurer or association.
  33. The correct answer is D (Slightly behind and to the weaker side, holding a gait belt fitted around her waist): Guarding from behind and slightly to the weaker side lets the physiotherapist control the trunk through a gait belt if the client loses balance. Walking backward in front limits the ability to catch a fall and risks a trip. A grip on the elbow alone gives little control of the trunk, and standing several steps away is too far to prevent a fall.
  34. The correct answer is A (Reactive tendinopathy, a short-term adaptive response to a sudden load increase that can settle): A young athlete with no prior symptoms, a recent spike in load and diffuse swelling matches the reactive stage, which is an adaptive response that often settles when load is managed. Disrepair and degenerative stages develop over longer periods of overload, and a reactive episode on a degenerative tendon is seen in older or previously symptomatic tendons.
  35. The correct answer is A (Increase wearing time gradually each day and check the skin for redness after removal): A new rigid orthosis needs gradual build-up so the skin can adapt to pressure and the client can report problems early. Redness that fades quickly after removal is acceptable, while lingering redness means the fit needs adjusting. A sock should be worn to limit friction, and restricting use to exercise would block its benefit for walking.
  36. The correct answer is D (Rheumatoid arthritis, which usually causes symmetrical inflammation of small hand joints with prolonged morning stiffness): Rheumatoid arthritis typically affects the metacarpophalangeal and proximal interphalangeal joints on both sides, spares the distal joints, and causes morning stiffness lasting longer than 30 to 60 minutes. Hand osteoarthritis involves the distal joints with short stiffness, gout is usually a single acute joint, and psoriatic arthritis tends to be asymmetrical.
  37. The correct answer is B (An increase of 3 points, from an average of 3 at baseline to 6): The Patient-Specific Functional Scale is reported as the average across activities. Baseline is (4 + 2 + 3) divided by 3, which is 3, and follow-up is (6 + 5 + 7) divided by 3, which is 6, so the change is 3 points. Summing the items, comparing extremes, or converting to a percentage does not follow the scoring method.
  38. The correct answer is C (Ensure the client receives care, then report the concern through the employer's process and the college): The initial duty is to make sure the injury is treated. A repeated pattern of unsafe practice after a dismissed incident should be reported through the employer's process and to the regulatory college, and the decision cannot be left to the employer or the client. Informal feedback has already failed to change the practice.
  39. The correct answer is A (No foot movement when the relaxed calf is squeezed, because the tendon cannot transmit the squeeze): A positive Thompson test is absence of plantar flexion when the calf is squeezed, because a ruptured Achilles tendon cannot transmit the force to the calcaneus. Weak seated plantar flexion comes from the deep flexors and peroneals and does not rule out rupture. Movement on squeezing would indicate an intact tendon, and the other responses describe different structures.
  40. The correct answer is A (40 percent, because he is mainly in bed and requires mainly assistance for self-care): A rating of 40 percent describes mainly being in bed, inability to do most activity and mainly assistance with self-care. Sixty percent means reduced ambulation with occasional assistance, 50 percent mainly sitting or lying with considerable assistance, and 30 percent total bed rest with total care.
  41. The correct answer is D (Chest deep water walking, because buoyancy offloads the hip while allowing continuous aerobic work): Chest deep water offloads a large share of body weight through the hip, so she can sustain aerobic work with far less joint stress. Knee deep water gives little buoyancy and jogging keeps impact forces high. Overground walking and stair climbing both place her full body weight through an arthritic hip, which works against her goal.
  42. The correct answer is D (Daily spinal extension and thoracic mobility exercises with deep breathing, plus regular aerobic activity): Inflammatory back pain improves with movement, and regular extension, thoracic mobility, breathing and aerobic exercise preserve posture and chest expansion in ankylosing spondylitis. Rest worsens stiffness. Flexion-based work reinforces the stooped posture the disease tends to produce.
  43. The correct answer is B (Place a pillow lengthwise under both calves so the heels float free without knee hyperextension): Floating the heels with a pillow under the calves removes pressure from the heel completely, and the knees stay slightly flexed to avoid hyperextension. Ring cushions create a pressure ridge, massage can damage fragile tissue and bandages add little offloading. His neuropathy means he cannot feel early damage.
  44. The correct answer is D (Thomas test for hip flexion contracture, with the pelvis stabilized and the lumbar spine flat): Prolonged sitting with the limb supported on a pillow promotes hip flexion contracture, which prevents upright prosthetic standing. The modified Thomas test, with the pelvis fixed and the spine flat, detects it. The Ober test, Ely test and straight leg raise assess other structures that are not the main contracture risk.
  45. The correct answer is B (Whether hip, calf and pelvic floor strength tolerate impact tasks, because running loads healing tissue): Return to running depends on measurable capacity of the pelvic floor, trunk, hip and calf to absorb repeated impact, not on how fit she feels or how far she ran before. Past mileage does not show current tissue readiness. Lactation lowers estrogen and may reduce tissue tolerance rather than protect it.
  46. The correct answer is D (Clients generally may access their record, with limited exceptions, and custodians must respond within time limits): Health privacy laws give individuals a right to see and obtain a copy of their own health record, with narrow exceptions, and require the custodian to respond within a set period. Viewing rights without a copy, a summary in place of the notes, and a clinician's general judgment about upset do not match the statutory right. A harm exception exists but is narrow and does not let the physiotherapist screen what the client may read.
  47. The correct answer is D (Give summary feedback after sets of trials and ask her to self-evaluate beforehand): Frequent immediate feedback can make a learner depend on it, so performance falls when it is removed. Summary feedback after sets, preceded by self-evaluation, builds the error-detection skills she needs at home. Feedback only after errors or continuous cues keeps the guidance external to her.
  48. The correct answer is A (Abnormal postural reflexes, because needing more than two steps or being caught indicates instability): A normal response to the pull test is recovery in one or two steps, and taking more steps or needing to be caught shows impaired postural reflexes (retropulsion). This is a clinically important sign of fall risk. Stepping is a normal strategy only when balance is regained promptly.
  49. The correct answer is C (As soon as possible and no later than 30 days after receiving the request): Ontario's health privacy law requires the custodian to respond as soon as possible and within 30 calendar days, with a limited extension only in specified situations. Counting business days or allowing 60 days stretches the legislated period. Holding the record until care ends has no basis in the law, because the right of access applies while treatment is ongoing.
  50. The correct answer is C (Raise activity to 60 minutes daily and limit recreational screen time to 2 hours): The guidelines for children and youth recommend at least 60 minutes of moderate to vigorous activity daily, no more than 2 hours of recreational screen time and 9 to 11 hours of sleep for his age. His sleep is appropriate, but activity falls short and screen time is above the limit.
Case CHB4

Mrs. Dubois is an 84-year-old woman admitted to a medical unit with pneumonia and deconditioning. She has moderate dementia and is oriented to person only. Her daughter holds the power of attorney for personal care. Mrs. Dubois says clearly that she does not want to walk with the physiotherapist because it hurts, but she agrees to sit in the chair. Yesterday she walked 20 metres with a walker, and her oxygen saturation stays above 94 percent.

  1. The correct answer is D (Explain the walking proposal simply and check that she understands it and can weigh its consequences): Capacity is judged for the particular decision by checking that the person understands the information and can appreciate how it applies to her situation and the consequences of choosing. A screening score and disorientation describe cognition in general, not this decision. A relative's opinion does not replace the clinician's assessment.
  2. The correct answer is C (Her mother's known prior wishes and values, then her best interests if no wishes are known): A substitute decision maker must follow the capable wishes the person expressed earlier, and only when none are known decide in the person's best interests. The daughter's own preferences and the team's convenience are not the standard. A physician's order does not override the substitute's role in consenting to treatment.
  3. The correct answer is C (Stop, address her pain and distress, and review the plan with her daughter before trying again): Even with a substitute's consent, a patient who is distressed and in pain should not be forced, and her resistance signals a problem that can be addressed. Better pain control and revisiting the plan with the daughter respects both. Proceeding anyway risks harm and loss of trust, and discharge abandons a patient who can still benefit.
  4. The correct answer is D (Stop treatment and send her for emergency medical assessment, because cauda equina compression is possible): Saddle numbness, new difficulty starting urination and weakness in both legs together are red flags for cauda equina syndrome, which needs emergency assessment within hours to prevent permanent loss of bladder, bowel and leg function. An urgent appointment within the week or a specialist bladder referral treats the picture as routine and loses critical time. Biofeedback and a one week reassessment would continue treatment in the presence of an evolving neurological emergency.
  5. The correct answer is D (Document the unsafe stair performance, tell the manager, and seek more funding or an alternative service): Discharging a client who cannot yet manage the stairs in her own home would be unsafe, so the clinical need and the funding limit have to be addressed together. Recording the finding, informing the manager and pursuing more funding or another service protects the client and the record. Discharging on a visit count or treating unofficially do not.
  6. The correct answer is B (Draw one line through the error, initial and date it, and record the correct side): A correction must leave the original entry legible and show who changed it and when, which a single line with initials and date accomplishes. Striking the line without initials or a date does not identify the author of the change. A separate late entry leaves two conflicting statements in the chart with nothing marking the original as wrong, and blacking out the error hides what was first written.
Case CHB5

Mrs. Leila Haddad, 79, is on a medical unit after pneumonia. She lives alone in a third floor walk-up apartment, has type 2 diabetes, and has lost 5 kg in six weeks. She says she skips meals because cooking tires her. The physiotherapist measures a Timed Up and Go of 19 seconds with a front-wheeled walker. Her daughter lives in another province.

  1. The correct answer is D (An occupational therapist, because performing daily self care and home tasks safely is that profession's focus): Assessing how a person performs kitchen and bathroom tasks, and recommending adaptations, is central to occupational therapy. Nursing, dietetics and social work all contribute to her care, but none of them is the usual lead for a functional home task assessment.
  2. The correct answer is D (A dietitian, because assessing nutritional status and designing intake plans is that profession's core role): Unintentional weight loss with falling strength calls for a nutritional assessment and an intake plan, which dietitians provide. Her own account points to fatigue with cooking, not chewing or swallowing difficulty, so a speech-language assessment is less relevant. Nursing tracks intake day to day but does not design the nutrition plan.
  3. The correct answer is C (A social worker, because linking clients to funding, community services and caregiver support is that role): Funding, community service access and caregiver support are core social work functions. An occupational therapist can recommend equipment and home changes, and a nurse can teach diabetes care, but neither is the usual lead for arranging services and finances. The physician confirms medical readiness rather than coordinating community supports.
  4. The correct answer is A (Call emergency services now, because a convulsion lasting five minutes or longer is status epilepticus): A generalized convulsion that lasts five minutes or more is treated as status epilepticus, a medical emergency, so emergency services should be called immediately. Objects placed in the mouth and restraint can cause injury and do not help the airway. Waiting longer risks brain injury and respiratory compromise.
  5. The correct answer is D (Remove the gel, then wipe the head and cable and allow the surface to dry): Gel is organic material that reduces the action of disinfectant, so it is removed first and the surface is then disinfected and allowed to dry for the labelled contact time. A dry towel alone does not disinfect, and cables and transducers are not designed for immersion.
  6. The correct answer is B (Submaximal low-load exercise with rest breaks, because overwork can worsen weakness in recovering nerves): During recovery from Guillain-Barre, muscles weakened by demyelination fatigue easily, and overwork can cause a setback in strength. Submaximal, low-load exercise with frequent rests preserves function without overloading. Inactivity leads to deconditioning, and high-intensity or exhaustive exercise risks overwork weakness.
  7. The correct answer is A (Propose equipment, caregiver training, and added support so the team can test a supported return home): His preference for home matters and the safety gap is real, so the best step is to explore ways to close the gap, such as lift equipment and training, and test the plan. Recommending placement overrides his wishes before alternatives are tried. Discharging without solving the transfer problem ignores the risk, and the destination is a shared team decision.
  8. The correct answer is B (Obtain written consent, then share only abilities and restrictions, because diagnosis is not needed for duties): Provincial health privacy legislation, such as PHIPA in Ontario, and the College's standards require consent before disclosure, and the information shared should be limited to what the purpose needs. For modified duties, the employer needs abilities and restrictions, not the diagnosis or entire record. Verbal disclosure and employer payment do not remove the consent requirement.
Case CHB6

Mr. Alvarez, 68, is on postoperative day 2 after a right cemented total knee arthroplasty for osteoarthritis. He lives alone in a two-storey house. Active knee flexion measures 45 degrees and the extension lag is 10 degrees. The knee is warm with moderate swelling and the incision is dry. His calf is soft and not tender. Temperature is 37.2 C and he is receiving thromboprophylaxis.

  1. The correct answer is B (Compare active with passive knee extension in supine, then record the difference between them in degrees): An extension lag is the difference between the passive extension the knee can reach and the active extension the quadriceps can achieve, so both are measured in the same position. Flexion measurement, heel height alone, and passive overpressure comparisons describe other findings and do not quantify quadriceps lag.
  2. The correct answer is A (Hold limb mobilization and notify the surgeon or nurse for urgent assessment of deep vein thrombosis): A new unilateral increase in calf circumference with warmth in the early postoperative period raises strong suspicion of deep vein thrombosis, so the clot must not be disturbed by exercise until medical assessment. Homans sign is neither sensitive nor specific, so a negative result cannot clear the client. Compression and elevation do not replace diagnosis.
  3. The correct answer is B (Continue frequent flexion and extension exercises with ice and elevation, because early motion limits stiffness): After uncomplicated arthroplasty, frequent active motion with swelling control is recommended because early mobility prevents arthrofibrosis and improves function. Prolonged rest allows stiffness to develop. Cemented implants do not need a flexion limit, and forceful passive stretching can provoke more swelling and pain.
  4. The correct answer is D (Her condition is stable and predictable, so her response to the task can be anticipated): Delegation depends on how predictable the client's response is and how low the risk of the task is, and a healed incision, steady vital signs and expected progress make the outcome foreseeable. Task simplicity and client motivation matter but do not replace a stable, predictable condition. Time since surgery alone does not remove precautions, and the physiotherapist still decides suitability for each client.
  5. The correct answer is B (Use the professional interpreter for the consent discussion, and let her grandson stay if she wishes): Informed consent requires accurate, impartial interpretation of risks, and a young family member may omit, soften or misunderstand clinical information. A qualified interpreter protects accuracy and her privacy, and she can still choose to have family present. A handout afterward or gestures alone cannot confirm that she understood before treatment began.
  6. The correct answer is A (The 7-point gain is below the minimal clinically important difference of 9 points): The minimal clinically important difference of the Lower Extremity Functional Scale is 9 points, so a 7-point gain may be within measurement error. Treating any rise, or a score above the midpoint, as meaningful overstates the improvement. The report should say change is uncertain and rely on other outcomes.
  7. The correct answer is A (Quadriceps neuromuscular electrical stimulation with active extension work, plus prone hang to regain terminal extension): Early priorities after reconstruction are full extension and quadriceps activation. Electrical stimulation with active contraction improves quadriceps output, and prone hang restores terminal extension. Heavily loaded open-chain extension strains the graft near extension, heavy hamstring work stresses the harvest site, and jogging at three weeks is far too early.
  8. The correct answer is C (Advise her to contact her family physician or the covering rheumatology service promptly about the flare): Several days of warm, swollen joints suggest an active flare that needs medical review, and a covering provider can be reached while the specialist is away. Exercise can be modified in the meantime. Waiting two weeks, resuming unaided, or adjusting medication timing is outside the physiotherapist's role and delays proper care.
  9. The correct answer is B (Imaging is not routinely needed, and staying active usually leads to faster recovery than rest): For acute non-specific low back pain without red flags, guidelines advise against routine imaging because findings correlate poorly with symptoms, and they recommend staying active. Bed rest slows recovery. The suggested timelines for imaging or delaying activity are not supported.
  10. The correct answer is A (Add a new entry dated today, marked late, describing Monday's session and the delay): A late entry must show the actual date and time it was written, state that it is late, and refer to the date of the event. Inserting text into an earlier entry or backdating makes the record appear contemporaneous when it was not. Omitting it leaves no evidence of care.
  11. The correct answer is C (Document findings, counsel against driving, and notify his physician or licensing authority as provincial rules permit): Neglect and slowed reaction time make driving a risk to him and others, so the physiotherapist should document, give clear advice and use the notification route that provincial rules allow. Simply respecting his decision ignores public safety. Delay or involving family to remove keys bypasses the proper professional and legal process.
  12. The correct answer is A (Bucket-handle motion, because the lower ribs lift and swing outward, widening the thoracic cage): Lower ribs elevate like a bucket handle, swinging outward and increasing the transverse diameter of the lower thorax. Upper ribs move mainly in pump-handle fashion and increase the anteroposterior diameter. The costovertebral joints permit the rib to rotate about its neck rather than slide upward on the vertebral bodies, and rotation about the vertical axis is not what widens the lower thorax.
  13. The correct answer is D (Hold treatment until the referral record number is verified and corrected with the referring service): The record number on the referral differs from the wristband and chart, so the only order for the program may belong to another patient. The discrepancy must be resolved with the referring service before treatment starts. Agreement of the other identifiers does not clear a mismatched order, treating first leaves the risk in place, and changing the referral yourself is not within the physiotherapist's authority.
  14. The correct answer is B (Positive for neural tension, because ankle movement changed symptoms without altering hamstring length): A straight leg raise is positive when it reproduces familiar radicular pain between roughly 30 and 70 degrees. Changing the ankle position alters nerve load but not hamstring length, so the pain change points to neural tissue. Hamstring tightness gives a pulling sensation that does not change with ankle position.
  15. The correct answer is C (Prone arm raise at 120 degrees, thumb up, because lower trapezius activates with less upper trapezius): A prone arm raise in line with the lower trapezius fibres, thumb up, produces high lower trapezius activity with relatively low upper trapezius activity. A shrug is dominated by the upper trapezius. The wall push-up plus targets serratus anterior and side-lying external rotation targets the cuff, so neither addresses the lower trapezius goal.
  16. The correct answer is B (A rating of 12 to 13, described as somewhat hard, matching moderate intensity): On the Borg 6 to 20 scale, 12 to 13 (somewhat hard) corresponds to moderate intensity for most adults. Ratings of 9 to 10 are very light effort, while 15 and above reflect vigorous work. Ratings of 17 to 18 approach maximal effort and are not suitable as a routine walking target.
Case CHB7

Mr. Hassan is 61 and 6 days after uncomplicated coronary artery bypass grafting through a median sternotomy. He takes a beta blocker. Resting heart rate is 68 beats per minute and blood pressure is 118/72. He walks on the ward without an aid and reports mild incision soreness at 3 out of 10. There is no arrhythmia on the monitor.

  1. The correct answer is D (Splint the chest with a pillow when coughing, and follow the surgeon's limits on lifting): Pillow splinting lets him cough and clear secretions while protecting the incision, and surgeon-set limits on lifting and pushing guide arm use. Total avoidance of overhead movement is not required. Binders are not routine, and avoiding coughing raises the risk of chest complications.
  2. The correct answer is B (A rating of perceived exertion of 11 to 14 on the 6 to 20 scale): A beta blocker caps heart rate, so predicted heart rate zones overstate or misrepresent intensity. A rating of perceived exertion of 11 to 14 corresponds to light to somewhat hard effort that is safe early in rehabilitation. A rating of 15 to 17 is too hard, and speaking only single words signals excessive effort.
  3. The correct answer is D (Stop exercise, seat or lay him down, and monitor blood pressure and symptoms until they recover): A fall in systolic pressure with dizziness and pallor during exercise is a reason to stop, and sitting or lying him down protects against syncope while his pressure is monitored. Standing still keeps him upright with a low pressure and risks pooling and fainting. Walking on slowly or continuing the planned walk after fluids exposes him to a fall.
  4. The correct answer is B (Support the forearm on a lap tray with the shoulder neutral and the elbow forward): A flaccid arm hanging under its own weight stretches the joint capsule and encourages subluxation and pain, so the forearm should be supported in neutral alignment. Overhead pulleys load an unstable shoulder and are discouraged. A tight sling promotes tone and poor movement patterns, and forcing the shoulder into abduction adds stress to the joint.
  5. The correct answer is A (The interval includes 1.0, so the study cannot exclude no effect despite the apparent benefit): A risk ratio of 1.0 means no difference, and an interval that spans 1.0 means the data are compatible with benefit, no effect or slight harm. A point estimate alone does not establish significance. A wide interval reflects imprecision, not bias, and 0.70 means a 30 percent relative reduction, not 70.
  6. The correct answer is C (Stop impact loading and arrange imaging with her physician, because a stress fracture is likely): A rapid mileage increase with night pain, a focal 2 cm point of tenderness, and pain on hopping are features of a tibial stress fracture rather than medial tibial stress syndrome, which causes diffuse tenderness along the posteromedial border. Impact must stop and imaging is needed. Stretching, taping, or modalities would not protect the bone.
  7. The correct answer is D (Choose equipment that best fits the unit's main client needs and is supported by clinical evidence): Funds should go to the equipment that serves the unit's main client group and has evidence of benefit, which here means walking rehabilitation after stroke. Versatility, novelty and staff preference are common reasons but none shows that the purchase will improve outcomes for these clients.
  8. The correct answer is D (Restrain the left hand most of the day, with several hours of daily shaped practice): Constraint-induced therapy pairs prolonged restraint of the less affected hand with intensive shaped, task-specific practice of the weaker limb, and he meets the usual wrist and finger extension criteria. Restraint limited to therapy time or paired with bilateral tasks or passive stretching delivers too little forced use and practice, and machine strengthening is not task specific.
  9. The correct answer is C (Stage 3, because spasticity peaks and voluntary movement occurs only within the synergy pattern): In the Chedoke-McMaster stages, stage 3 is marked by voluntary movement within the synergy and spasticity at its peak. Stage 2 has only minimal voluntary movement, while stages 4 and 5 involve movement outside the synergy with falling spasticity. This woman cannot yet leave the pattern, so stage 3 fits.
  10. The correct answer is D (Add wearing time gradually and check skin each time, because insensate skin gives no pain warning): Without sensation she cannot feel rubbing or pressure, so injury can develop before she notices. Short, graded wearing periods with skin inspection after each use catch redness early. Relying on pain reports fails with insensate skin, and extra padding can increase pressure within the orthosis while weekly checks are too infrequent.
  11. The correct answer is A (Grade II partial tear, because bruising and mild laxity with preserved walking indicate incomplete disruption): Moderate swelling, bruising, and mild laxity with some function retained fit a grade II partial ligament tear. Grade III would show marked laxity with an absent end-feel and major loss of function, and grade I has little swelling and no laxity. Syndesmosis injury produces pain above the joint and a positive squeeze or external rotation test, not lateral ligament tenderness.
  12. The correct answer is C (Providers agree on goals and roles, communicate regularly, and involve the client in setting the plan): Shared care rests on agreed goals, defined roles, regular communication and active client participation in the plan. A shared chart with separate goals still produces conflicting advice, and referral with feedback describes coordination between independent providers. Planning at meetings and informing her afterwards leaves out the central feature that she helps set the plan.
  13. The correct answer is B (Subscapularis, because this position tests the main internal rotator of the shoulder joint): Inability to lift the hand off the lower back is a positive lift-off test, which isolates subscapularis, the principal internal rotator. Supraspinatus is tested in elevation, and infraspinatus and teres minor are external rotators. A fall onto the shoulder can tear subscapularis.
  14. The correct answer is C (Request an occupational therapy home assessment and practise stairs with her while the team plans modifications): Her only bathroom is upstairs, so she needs both a home assessment for modifications and supervised stair practice before she goes home. Moving the bed does not solve bathroom access. The partner is away during the day, and waiting for outpatient care leaves her unsafe during the first days at home.
  15. The correct answer is C (Current mobility level, transfer assistance needed, precautions, equipment used, and the goals agreed with the patient): A transfer summary should tell the next team what the patient can do now, what help and equipment are needed, and what goals matter to him. A session-by-session log buries this information. Medical data and administrative details belong to other team members and do not guide the first rehabilitation sessions.
  16. The correct answer is A (Identifying a safe place to go, trusted contacts, and key documents to keep ready): A safety plan respects the client's pace and prepares for danger by identifying a refuge, contacts and documents. Pressuring her to leave removes her control and can increase risk. Contacting the partner could provoke further violence, and ending the discussion misses the chance to offer practical support.
  17. The correct answer is C (A temporary worsening of existing deficits from a raised core temperature, because it resolved with cooling): This is Uhthoff phenomenon: a rise in core temperature slows conduction in already demyelinated axons, so old deficits briefly reappear and then fade as the body cools. A true relapse produces new or worsening deficits lasting more than 24 hours, which did not happen here. Medication reactions and ischemic attacks do not track so closely with heat exposure and cooling.
  18. The correct answer is A (Explain the examination, ask about touch and chaperone preferences, and offer alternatives): Informed consent includes explaining what will happen and inviting the client to state her preferences, which may include a chaperone, a different clinician or modified positioning. Routine draping and a brief explanation do not give her a real chance to speak. Rebooking her with a female clinician assumes a preference she has not stated and removes her choice.
  19. The correct answer is B (Strike one line through the error, add the correction, then date and initial the change): A single legible line through the error, followed by a dated and initialed correction by the author, keeps the original visible and shows who changed it. An addendum elsewhere leaves the wrong entry unmarked in the Friday note, and a colleague cannot initial a change on the author's behalf. Correction fluid hides the original and makes the chart look altered.

Practice Test 7 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is C (Report to the child protection agency promptly and explain the limits of confidentiality to her): Sexual contact between an adult in a position of trust and a 14-year-old gives reasonable grounds to suspect a child is in need of protection, so a report is required even against her wishes. Privacy rules include exceptions for mandatory reporting. Delaying for her parents or referring elsewhere does not satisfy the personal duty.
  2. The correct answer is D (A written mobility plan stating the transfer technique, walking aid, supervision level, and fall prevention strategies): Care home staff need clear, written, practical instructions that every shift can follow, such as the transfer method, aid and supervision level. A verbal report to one nurse is easily lost across shifts. Raw notes are hard to apply, and a strengthening program does not match his current needs or cognition.
  3. The correct answer is B (Use early morning or evening outdoor sessions with sun protection, because ultraviolet light can trigger flares): Photosensitivity is a recognized lupus trigger, so exercising outside the peak sun hours with protection lets her stay active while lowering flare risk. Cutting activity to a minimum is not needed because graded exercise is encouraged, and fatigue is not a sign of progression by itself. Raising intensity adds stress during a possible flare, and a new cheek rash should not be dismissed as deconditioning.
  4. The correct answer is C (Keep the joint straight during splint changes, because any flexion reopens the healing gap): A mallet injury needs continuous extension of the DIP joint for many weeks, and even one brief episode of flexion can stretch the healing tendon and restore the extensor lag. Hygiene can be done with the fingertip supported flat. Night-only wear or intermittent flexion is premature at three weeks.
  5. The correct answer is B (Ice can give short-term pain relief, but good evidence that it speeds tissue healing is lacking): Cold can reduce pain temporarily, but trials have not shown that it accelerates healing, and inflammation is an early part of normal repair. Claims that ice shortens recovery, prevents scarring or restores blood flow are not supported. Relative load management is usually more useful than stopping all activity.
  6. The correct answer is B (Return to the previous weight-bearing level and report the pain change to the surgeon promptly): Pain had fallen steadily, then rose sharply after loading increased and now includes deep night pain, which suggests the fracture or fixation is being overloaded. Dropping back to the tolerated level and informing the surgeon protects healing and gets the plan reviewed. Pushing on with ice, adding modalities, or waiting silently delays recognition of a possible problem.
  7. The correct answer is A (Resisted great toe extension, because extensor hallucis longus is the most L5 dominant muscle): Extensor hallucis longus is the muscle most reliably tested for L5. Plantarflexion mainly reflects S1, knee extension reflects L3 and L4, and hip flexion reflects L2 with some L3. Foot catching during gait fits L5 weakness.
  8. The correct answer is C (Early morning in an air-conditioned clinic, because rated fatigue is lowest at that time of day): The chart shows fatigue lowest at 8 a.m. on the cool day, and it climbs steadily through the day, much faster on the hot day. An early session in a cooled setting uses her lowest-fatigue window and avoids heat. Midday and late afternoon values are higher on both days, and a hot morning already starts above the cool-day value.
  9. The correct answer is A (At least 10 to 20 beats per minute below 150, about 130 to 140): Exercise heart rate should stay a safe margin below the detection rate so that sinus tachycardia is not mistaken for an arrhythmia, so 130 to 140 here. Using an age-predicted maximum alone ignores the device. Training right at the threshold risks an inappropriate shock, and under 100 is needlessly low.
  10. The correct answer is D (Age of 65 years or older, because age is a high-risk factor in the rule): Age 65 or older is a high-risk factor, so she requires imaging without further range testing. A fall from standing height is not a dangerous mechanism in the rule, which requires a fall from about one metre or five stairs. Neck pain and slow walking are not high-risk criteria.
Case CHB8

A 62 year old man with severe community acquired pneumonia has been intubated and mechanically ventilated for 3 days in a medical intensive care unit. He is on a low dose norepinephrine infusion, and has a radial arterial line, a central venous catheter, a urinary catheter and a feeding tube. He opens his eyes to voice and follows simple commands. Mean arterial pressure is 72 mmHg, heart rate 98, SpO2 94 percent, FiO2 0.40, PEEP 8.

  1. The correct answer is D (Stable blood pressure and oxygenation on modest ventilator settings, with commands followed): Common safety criteria for starting mobilization are stable circulation, acceptable oxygenation on modest ventilator settings and the ability to cooperate. Imaging improvement does not show physiological readiness, and a monitoring line only makes observation easier. Low dose vasopressors may be acceptable in some units but their presence does not prove that his circulation is stable enough.
  2. The correct answer is A (Assign one person to secure the endotracheal tube and ventilator circuit while others manage lines): A defined team with one person dedicated to the airway and circuit reduces the risk of accidental extubation and line dislodgement. One nurse covering the tubes and every line cannot watch them all during a position change. Clamping invasive lines interrupts monitoring and infusions, and deeper sedation works against participation without securing anything.
  3. The correct answer is C (Stop the activity, return him to a supported position, and monitor until values recover): A fall to 87 percent with a large heart rate rise crosses commonly used stop thresholds, so the activity should end and the client be supported while values are watched. Pushing on or raising oxygen to continue treats the number rather than the cause. Flat positioning and percussion are not indicated without evidence of secretion retention.
  4. The correct answer is D (Propose reviewing the protocol so visit frequency follows individual need and outcome data): Stewardship of resources means matching service to need using evidence, and a protocol review with the team does this while keeping care safe. Alternate day visits for all clients trade one fixed rule for another and may leave slower clients undertreated. Informal shift decisions use clinical impression without outcome data, and unchanged daily visits spend time on clients who no longer need it.
  5. The correct answer is D (Steppage gait, caused by dorsiflexor weakness often linked to common peroneal nerve involvement): Excessive hip and knee flexion in swing to clear a dropped foot, followed by foot slap at loading, defines steppage gait from weak ankle dorsiflexors. Prolonged leg crossing is a recognized cause of common peroneal nerve compression at the fibular head. Antalgic, circumduction and vaulting patterns have different features and are not defined by foot slap.
  6. The correct answer is B (Advise no weight bearing, arrange urgent medical assessment, because findings suggest slipped capital femoral epiphysis): Knee pain with a normal knee examination, a limp, obligatory external rotation with hip flexion and limited internal rotation in an adolescent with obesity points to a slipped capital femoral epiphysis, which needs urgent imaging and protected weight bearing. Stretching, ice or strengthening would load an unstable growth plate and delay the diagnosis.
  7. The correct answer is B (Cushion his head, clear nearby objects, time the seizure, and call 911 for a first seizure): Seizure first aid protects the person from injury without restraining him or putting anything in his mouth, and the time is noted for responders. A first seizure, especially after a head injury, needs emergency services regardless of duration. Waiting for a ten minute threshold applies to people with known epilepsy, not to a first event.
  8. The correct answer is C (Begin pelvic floor training and teach exhaling during lifting, avoiding breath holding and straining): Pelvic floor training improves support and symptoms in mild to moderate prolapse, and exhaling on effort with no straining lowers downward pressure on the pelvic organs. Bracing with a held breath raises intra-abdominal pressure and repeats her current habit. Crunches also load the pelvic floor, and a pessary is a reasonable later option but not a reason to delay exercise.
  9. The correct answer is A (A pulsatile, expansile mass felt just left of the midline above the umbilicus): An expansile pulsating mass in the epigastric or periumbilical area, felt slightly left of midline where the aorta lies, is the key palpation sign. The right iliac fossa finding suggests appendiceal or psoas irritation, the paraspinal band is a musculoskeletal finding, and a suprapubic dull mass points to a distended bladder.
  10. The correct answer is B (A negative likelihood ratio of 0.15 produces a large shift, so the probability falls substantially): A negative likelihood ratio well below 1 lowers the probability meaningfully, which is useful for rule-out thinking. It does not drop the probability to zero, so a result in a moderate risk client still needs clinical judgment. The positive ratio of 1.5 is irrelevant to a negative result and is itself a weak value.
  11. The correct answer is B (Cover the door glass, offer a tank top or gown, and drape the remaining chest area): Adequate visualization of the scapula does not require exposing the breast, so a tank top or gown with draping meets both needs. Covering the glass removes visibility from the corridor. Assessing through thick clothing limits observation, and unnecessary exposure breaches dignity.
  12. The correct answer is D (The index is about 87 percent, below the criterion, so strengthening continues before full return): The limb symmetry index divides the injured limb value by the uninjured limb value: 130 divided by 150 is about 87 percent. This is under 90 percent, so further strength work is needed. Dividing the other way gives a false 115 percent, and 13 percent is the deficit, not the index.
  13. The correct answer is C (At least 150 minutes of moderate to vigorous activity, two strength days and balance activities): For adults 65 and older the guidelines call for at least 150 minutes of moderate to vigorous activity weekly, muscle strengthening on at least two days, and activities that challenge balance. One strength day and balance work reserved for after a fall fall short of the recommended frequency and purpose. Light walking and reduced volume do not supply the same strength or bone stimulus.
  14. The correct answer is D (Report her functional findings and risks objectively, respect her informed choice, and help plan home supports): She has been found capable, so the decision is hers even if the sons are concerned. The physiotherapist contributes honest findings and risks and helps reduce them with supports. Mild impairment does not remove her authority, and staying silent withholds the functional information the family needs to understand the risks.
  15. The correct answer is C (Stop treatment and send him to the emergency department today for suspected cauda equina syndrome): Urinary difficulty, saddle numbness and bilateral leg weakness are red flags for cauda equina syndrome, a surgical emergency in which delay can cause permanent bladder, bowel and motor loss. Same-day emergency assessment is needed. Waiting days for imaging, adding traction or documenting first all postpone decompression.
  16. The correct answer is D (Split chores into short blocks with planned rests before fatigue builds, spread across the week): Pacing breaks tasks into manageable portions with scheduled rests taken before symptoms force them, which prevents the boom and bust cycle. Doing everything on good days and then collapsing repeats the pattern. Waiting for severe fatigue and ignoring symptoms on bad days also lead to flare-ups and inactivity.
  17. The correct answer is B (Limit activity to light supervised bedside mobility and consult oncology, because spontaneous bleeding risk is high): At a platelet count below about 20 ×10⁹/L the risk of spontaneous bleeding is high, so only light, supervised activity of daily living level mobility is reasonable and the team should guide it. Lack of bruising does not lower the risk. Complete bed rest causes deconditioning and is not required.
  18. The correct answer is A (A maximum of 24 kg on the scale, which is 30 percent of body weight): Thirty percent of 80 kg is 0.30 multiplied by 80, which gives 24 kg. The reading of 56 kg is the load that remains unloaded, not the limit. Using the percentage as kilograms ignores body weight, and 16 kg corresponds to 20 percent of body weight, a lower limit than ordered.
  19. The correct answer is D (Grade 2, a weak contraction with no appreciable lift of the examining finger): On the Modified Oxford Scale a weak squeeze with no lift is Grade 2, whereas a flicker is Grade 1 and a moderate squeeze with lift is Grade 3. Grade 0 means no contraction at all. This woman has a palpable squeeze, so Grade 0 and Grade 1 underestimate her strength, and the absent lift excludes Grade 3.
  20. The correct answer is C (Negotiate shared priorities, linking stair and chair strength work to the golf goal he values most): Goal setting is a collaborative process that respects the client's values while using assessment findings to sequence steps. Linking lower limb strength and transfer ability to golf keeps the client's priority at the centre and explains why the building blocks matter. Ignoring either source of information, or waiting for pain control, weakens engagement.
Case CHB9

Mrs. Dubois is an 84-year-old woman on day 4 of an admission for urosepsis. She lives alone and previously walked with a cane. She takes eight regular medications, including lorazepam and oxybutynin. Today she is drowsy, inattentive and picking at her gown, though she was alert and conversational yesterday. Heart rate is 98 bpm, blood pressure 104/62 mmHg, SpO2 94 percent on room air, and temperature 37.4 C.

  1. The correct answer is A (Acute change from yesterday with fluctuating attention during a medical illness): Delirium develops over hours to days, fluctuates, and centres on impaired attention, usually triggered by illness or medications. Dementia progresses slowly with relatively stable attention until late stages. Disorientation and evening worsening can occur in both conditions, so neither separates them, and a long slow decline describes dementia rather than her presentation.
  2. The correct answer is C (Acute onset with fluctuating course and inattention, plus disorganized thinking or altered consciousness): The Confusion Assessment Method requires acute onset with fluctuating course and inattention, plus either disorganized thinking or an altered level of consciousness. Memory loss, hallucinations, agitation and sleep disturbance may accompany delirium but are not the defining features of a positive screen.
  3. The correct answer is D (Supervised short bedside mobilization, with her sedating medications and drowsiness reported to the team): Early supervised mobility reduces delirium duration and deconditioning, and the team should hear about sedating drugs and drowsiness that may contribute. Physiotherapists do not adjust medications, so that task belongs to the prescriber. Waiting for the delirium to clear promotes weakness, and unsupervised repetition by the family is unsafe when she needs hands-on help to stand.
  4. The correct answer is B (Stop the stretch now, because consent can be withdrawn at any time, then discuss her concerns): Consent is ongoing, and a capable client who asks to stop must be heard immediately, especially when her ratings have climbed to a very high level. Pausing to resume, rechecking pain before continuing, or finishing gently all treat her request as negotiable. After stopping, the physiotherapist can explore her concerns and agree on a modified plan.
  5. The correct answer is D (Keep him non-weight-bearing and arrange urgent orthopedic assessment for suspected slipped capital femoral epiphysis): An obese adolescent with knee pain, a limp and obligatory external rotation on hip flexion fits slipped capital femoral epiphysis, which can displace further with weight bearing. Hip pain is often referred to the knee, so knee imaging alone could miss it. Stretching or reassurance risks worsening the slip and its complications.
  6. The correct answer is D (Continue treatment and add sport-specific testing, because her goal activity is still severely limited): A 22-point LEFS gain is large, but the patient-specific item shows the activity she cares about is still severely limited, and the LEFS does not test cutting or pivoting. Return-to-sport decisions need sport-specific testing and a program that targets her goal. A general health survey is less specific, and repeating the same measure adds little.
  7. The correct answer is B (Wrap it in six to eight layers of toweling and check the skin after five minutes): A pack from the heating unit is very hot, so about six to eight layers of dry toweling are needed to control heat transfer. The skin should be checked early, around five minutes, because burns develop quickly. Damp towels conduct heat faster and increase burn risk, and one thin layer gives too little protection.
  8. The correct answer is B (Transversus abdominis activation with exhalation, because it builds tension across the midline without doming): Management of diastasis recti focuses on restoring deep abdominal control and tension through the linea alba, avoiding movements that produce visible doming. Sit ups, rotational crunches and fatiguing planks raise intra-abdominal pressure and often dome the midline, so they are poor starting points. Load can be progressed once the midline stays flat during effort.
  9. The correct answer is C (Arrange prompt factor replacement, then rest, ice and elevate the joint, because early treatment limits bleeding): A suspected joint bleed worsens with every hour that passes without factor replacement, so treatment is arranged promptly and the joint is rested, iced and elevated. Heat and stretching increase bleeding into the joint. Strengthening or forced extension is unsafe until the bleed is controlled and the joint has settled.
  10. The correct answer is C (Stop exercise, give 15 g of juice or glucose tablets, and recheck in 15 minutes): Mild hypoglycemia in an alert client is treated with 15 g of fast-acting carbohydrate, with the glucose rechecked after 15 minutes. Chocolate contains fat that slows absorption, and 30 g risks a rebound high. Continuing exercise lowers glucose further and delays treatment.
  11. The correct answer is B (Stop weight-bearing exercise on that foot, inspect it and arrange wound care via his physician): With loss of protective sensation a blister can progress to an ulcer without pain, so lack of pain does not mean low risk. The foot should be offloaded, inspected and referred for wound care before weight-bearing exercise resumes. A dressing with cushioned shoes or a return to walking next session keeps loading the heel, and cycling still presses on it.
  12. The correct answer is C (Implied consent lets her share needed information with providers in his circle of care): Under PHIPA, health information custodians may rely on implied consent to share information needed for care with others in the client's circle of care, unless the client has withheld consent. Written consent and privacy officer approval are not required for routine team communication, and the rule does not limit sharing to one profession.
  13. The correct answer is A (SpO2 fell from 95 to 85 percent on walking and recovered at rest, suggesting exertional desaturation): The Assessment states what the physiotherapist concludes from the data, here a ten-point exertional fall that recovered with rest and should be reviewed. Declaring that heart failure has worsened or that medications need changing is a medical judgment beyond her role. Calling the fall expected removes the reason to report, and quick recovery does not make the drop during walking normal.
  14. The correct answer is C (Keep her at rest, call emergency services for suspected pulmonary embolism, and avoid manipulating the leg): Calf swelling and redness after surgery suggest a deep vein thrombosis, and new pleuritic chest pain, breathlessness and a fast pulse indicate a possible pulmonary embolism, which is life-threatening. She is kept still and emergency services are called. Compression, ankle pumps, walking or a delayed probability score could dislodge the clot or delay urgent care.
  15. The correct answer is A (Role clarification, because it concerns understanding and using each member's responsibilities and abilities): Role clarification is the domain about knowing and valuing one's own and others' roles and using that knowledge to divide work. Team functioning and leadership address group processes and direction, and communication addresses how information is exchanged. The description given matches role clarification.
  16. The correct answer is B (Consult a health professional and combine workload changes with support, because burnout has system causes): Burnout results from chronic workplace stress, so recovery usually needs both personal care and changes to workload, control and support. Rest, resilience tools and fewer hours alone leave the underlying causes in place, which her unchanged symptoms after leave already suggest. A health professional can also screen for depression and guide the plan.
  17. The correct answer is B (Ask the client to explain or demonstrate the instructions in her own words): Teach-back checks how well the clinician explained, by having the client say or show the instructions back. Asking for questions, repeating slowly or collecting a signature all depend on the client self-reporting understanding, which people with limited health literacy often do not do.
  18. The correct answer is C (About 23 hours daily, removed only for hygiene and skin care, until the scars mature): Pressure garments only reduce hypertrophic scarring when worn nearly continuously, about 23 hours a day, for many months until the scar matures. Part-day or exercise-only wear gives too little sustained pressure. Stopping when redness fades is premature because the scar is still active.
  19. The correct answer is D (Use the least exposure needed, in a familiar private space, with simple explanation at each step): People with dementia retain dignity needs and can often assent to simple, stepwise explanations. A familiar private space and minimal exposure reduce distress. Family may support, but assessment still seeks her assent, and exposure in a shared area is never justified by memory loss.
  20. The correct answer is D (Reports remaining fall risk, walker use and community exercise, with a follow-up contact): A discharge summary must state current function, ongoing risks and who to contact, since the next providers rely on it. A general statement of improvement or a list of exercises does not tell them how he is doing now. Giving the test time without advising follow-up, or leaving the fall risk for someone else to find, can let a high-risk client slip through.
  21. The correct answer is B (Moderate submaximal exercise stopped before fatigue, avoiding eccentric overload and exhaustion in affected muscles): In early ALS, moderate submaximal exercise can maintain function and fitness, but overwork weakness is a risk, so sessions stop before fatigue and avoid eccentric or exhaustive loading. Fatigue lasting a day signals too much. Complete rest causes disuse weakness, and heavy training to failure risks damaging already vulnerable muscle.
  22. The correct answer is C (Reply by the deadline with a factual written account, and consult her liability protection provider): Registrants must cooperate with the college and respond within the time given, so the reply is due by the stated deadline. A factual written account, with advice from the liability provider, protects the public process and her own position. Waiting for a withdrawal does not change the deadline, a phone call does not replace a written response, and the duty to reply is personal rather than the employer's.
  23. The correct answer is D (Objective findings, the treatment provided and response, the clinical concern, and the specific question or request): A useful referral letter is brief and focused: what was found, what was done and how he responded, what worries the physiotherapist, and what is being asked. Full session notes are too long to be useful. Physiotherapists do not prescribe medication, and background details do not support a decision about investigation.
  24. The correct answer is A (Reduce the March 24 plan payment to $60 and raise the client portion to $30): The plan has paid $96, $72 and $72 on the first three visits, a total of $240, so only $60 of the $300 maximum remains for the last visit. The client therefore owes $30 on March 24. The other changes would misstate coverage or fees that are correct as listed.
  25. The correct answer is A (Continue the exercises, because mild dizziness that settles within minutes is expected and drives compensation): Vestibular rehabilitation relies on provoking mild to moderate, short-lived symptoms to drive habituation and adaptation. Symptoms that settle within ten minutes are an expected training response, not harm. Stopping, reducing the dose or avoiding provoking movements limits the stimulus needed for compensation.
  26. The correct answer is B (Relieve pressure every 15 to 30 minutes for one to two minutes, using a timer): Without sensation, she needs a scheduled routine rather than relying on discomfort, and frequent short relief periods of one to two minutes every 15 to 30 minutes are taught to restore tissue perfusion. Hourly or two-hourly intervals leave tissue loaded too long, and waiting for discomfort or redness fails because sensation is absent and redness appears late.
  27. The correct answer is A (Securely overwrite or physically destroy the drive, and keep a record of the method and date): Deleting files or reformatting leaves data that can often be recovered with common tools, so verified overwriting or physical destruction is needed before disposal. A destruction record shows the clinic met its safeguard duty. Copying files to a USB stick does nothing to protect the data still on the drive being recycled.
  28. The correct answer is D (Demonstrate the transfer, then supervise her practising it and give feedback until she performs it correctly): Hands-on skills are learned by watching, practising with supervision and receiving feedback until the performance is safe. Handouts, a single demonstration and verbal repetition build knowledge but do not confirm that she can physically perform the transfer with her mother.
  29. The correct answer is A (Use a platform that meets provincial privacy requirements and obtain consent for virtual care): Health information custodians must use tools that meet provincial health privacy and security requirements, and the client should consent to the virtual format and its limits. A waiver cannot replace compliance. Avoiding recordings does not address how the platform handles data, and a website claim is not evidence of meeting the law.
  30. The correct answer is C (Ask what would make the exercises more important to her and what small step feels doable): Motivational interviewing draws out the client's own reasons and confidence through open questions, rather than persuading or directing. Asking about importance and a doable step respects her autonomy and builds commitment. Warning, scheduling for her, or simply cutting the program imposes the therapist's solution.
  31. The correct answer is C (Silicosis, from inhaled crystalline silica causing nodular fibrosis mainly in the upper lung zones): Years of cutting quartz expose a worker to respirable crystalline silica, which produces small upper-zone nodules and often eggshell calcification of hilar nodes. Asbestosis tends to affect the lung bases and pleura, and coal workers' disease follows coal dust exposure rather than stone fabrication. Hypersensitivity pneumonitis gives diffuse patchy change after antigen exposure, not discrete nodules.
  32. The correct answer is A (Give functional restrictions in practical terms, such as lifting limits and overhead time, without diagnosis): Employers need to know what the worker can and cannot do, such as lifting and overhead limits, to plan modified duties. The diagnosis and full findings are more than the consent and the task require. A vague fitness statement or a total restriction gives no usable guidance and does not match his measured abilities.
  33. The correct answer is D (A peer chart audit comparing documentation with the regulatory college's record keeping standards): A chart audit measures actual practice from real client records against a defined standard, and it can be repeated each year. Reviewing education certificates shows hours completed, not what is in client records. Satisfaction surveys and case conferences rely on opinion and selected examples, so they do not reveal systematic gaps in documentation or care.
  34. The correct answer is A (Attach both pads as pictured, follow the prompts, and keep everyone clear during rhythm analysis): The AED should be used as soon as it is available, with pads placed on bare dry skin as the diagrams show. Nobody may touch the person while the device analyzes the rhythm or delivers a shock, because movement can disturb analysis and endanger rescuers. Delaying the pads for more compressions or a long pulse check lowers the chance of survival.
  35. The correct answer is D (Press perpendicular until the filament bends for about one second, then ask her to report touch): The filament is applied perpendicular to the skin with just enough force to bend it, held for about one second, with the client unable to see the site. Sliding, tapping or testing on callus gives unreliable results because the standard force and contact are lost.
  36. The correct answer is C (Wearing eyewear rated for the laser wavelength, because direct or reflected light can damage the retina): Laser light, direct or reflected from shiny surfaces, can injure the retina, so eyewear rated for the specific wavelength is needed for the client, the therapist and anyone else present. Ordinary clear glasses are not rated for the wavelength and may not block it, and a low power setting does not remove the eye hazard. Keeping the arm still helps dose accuracy but protects no one's eyes.
  37. The correct answer is B (Monday, March 30, which is four weeks after the date the referral was received): Semi-urgent means within four weeks, and four weeks after Monday, March 2 is Monday, March 30. March 9 applies to the urgent category, April 6 is five weeks, and May 25 applies to the routine category. The category assigned at triage sets the time limit.
  38. The correct answer is C (Immobilize the ankle in slight plantarflexion and arrange urgent medical assessment for suspected tendon rupture): A sudden snap, a palpable gap, failed single-leg heel raise and a positive calf squeeze test all point to a complete Achilles rupture. The limb should be protected in slight plantarflexion and the client sent for prompt medical assessment. Loading a ruptured tendon with isometrics or eccentric drops could worsen the injury and delay care.
  39. The correct answer is B (Refer for medical investigation of possible non-mechanical pathology before continuing the exercise program): Pain that rises at night while activity-related pain stays flat, together with unexplained weight loss, does not behave like mechanical osteoarthritis and raises concern for malignancy or another systemic cause. Progressing loading or adding manual therapy treats the wrong problem. Waiting for walking pain to change delays investigation of a possible serious condition.
  40. The correct answer is C (Call emergency services immediately, note the time symptoms began, and stay with him while monitoring): Facial droop, arm drift and slurred speech are signs of stroke, a time-critical emergency in which treatment depends on the time of onset. Emergency services are called at once, the onset time is recorded and the physiotherapist stays with him. Checking glucose, booking a physician visit or arranging private transport each delays access to stroke care.
  41.
