The correct answer is B (Level III, because she walks with a hand-held device indoors and uses a wheelchair outdoors): Level III describes children aged 6 to 12 who walk with a hand-held mobility device indoors and use wheeled mobility for longer distances outdoors. Level II children walk without a device in most settings, and Level I children walk without limits on basic activities. Level IV children rely mainly on wheeled mobility with assistance, which does not match her indoor walking.
  42. The correct answer is B (Keep her still, call emergency services, and monitor her vital signs until help arrives): A shortened, externally rotated leg with severe groin pain after a fall suggests a hip fracture, so the client is kept still and emergency help is called right away while vital signs are monitored. Straightening or bracing the limb risks displacing the fracture, an analgesic is outside what a physiotherapist supplies, and a private car cannot safely manage an unstable injury.
  43. The correct answer is A (A rigid foot that cannot reach neutral passively, with a deep posterior ankle crease): True congenital talipes equinovarus is a structural deformity, so the foot is rigid and will not reach neutral passively, and a deep posterior crease is typical. Inversion at rest and a smaller foot can occur in both a structural and a positional foot, so they do not separate the two. A positional foot reaches neutral with gentle motion and has normal skin creases.
  44. The correct answer is B (Stop the walk, seat her, and increase her oxygen only within the prescribed range): A reading below the prescribed minimum of 90 percent means stopping exertion, resting her and adjusting oxygen only within the order or contacting the responsible clinician. Removing oxygen or pushing on worsens hypoxemia.
  45. The correct answer is B (Heat slows conduction in demyelinated nerves, so pre-cooling and exercising in cooler settings can help): A small rise in core temperature further impairs conduction in demyelinated axons, producing temporary symptoms that resolve when she cools (the Uhthoff phenomenon). Pre-cooling, cool fluids and exercising at cooler times of day are the recommended strategies. These transient changes do not mean new damage, so avoiding exercise is not needed, and fitness or fluids alone do not fix a heat-related conduction problem.
  46. The correct answer is C (Reflect her constraint without arguing, then invite her to suggest workable shoe options): Resistance signals a mismatch with the physiotherapist's approach, so the response is to roll with it and return the problem-solving to the client. More education or confronting the discrepancy typically increases defensiveness. Dropping the topic gives up a key modifiable factor without exploring her ideas.
  47. The correct answer is B (Lock the screen or log out so the record cannot be viewed or altered by others): Each user is accountable for activity under her credentials, so the session should be locked or ended whenever she steps away. A minimized window or a colleague watching still leaves access possible, and an eventual automatic logout leaves a window of exposure.
  48. The correct answer is D (Ask for a private talk, hear the physician's reasons, then share findings supporting early mobilization): A respectful private conversation that explores the physician's concerns and presents evidence is the most likely way to change the order. Charting a disagreement does not resolve it, working against an active order is unsafe and unprofessional, and a manager does not overrule a physician's clinical decision.
  49. The correct answer is D (Repeated loaded forward trunk flexion such as sit-ups, because it raises vertebral body compression): Loaded forward flexion concentrates compressive force on the anterior vertebral bodies and is linked to new compression fractures in people with osteoporosis. Walking is a recommended weight-bearing activity, gentle back extension strengthening is encouraged, and supervised balance training reduces fall risk.
  50. The correct answer is A (Six weeks, because the list shrinks by a net eight clients each week): Each week the clinic removes 12 clients and adds 4, a net reduction of 8, so 48 divided by 8 gives six weeks. Dividing by the booking rate alone overlooks that new referrals refill the list, and dividing by referrals or by their sum uses numbers that do not describe the net change.
  51. The correct answer is C (Secure the runner and improve stair lighting, because these are low-cost hazards at the riskiest spot): Home hazard modification is most effective when it targets identified, modifiable hazards, particularly at high-risk locations like stairs. A loose runner and poor lighting can be fixed cheaply and quickly. A stair lift or relocating living space are costly or disruptive and she cannot afford them, and vision referral matters but does not remove the immediate hazard.
  52. The correct answer is C (Stop activity, arrange urgent medical assessment, and use a telephone interpreter): Exertional chest tightness needs urgent medical review, and safety information must be conveyed accurately, which a telephone interpreter can provide immediately. Continuing the assessment, relying on gestures or simply rebooking delays care and risks miscommunication about a potentially serious symptom.
  53. The correct answer is A (Fit a neutral night splint and refer for electrodiagnostic testing, since wasting suggests axonal loss): Thenar wasting and weak abduction indicate motor axon involvement, which is a severe sign of carpal tunnel syndrome that needs prompt referral for confirmation and possible surgical opinion, along with a neutral splint. Conservative care with a delayed review risks permanent loss. Neck findings are negative, so cervical treatment is not supported.
  54. The correct answer is D (A man two weeks after stent placement for infarction, pain-free, with stable rhythm and blood pressure): A stable client with a recent coronary event and no ongoing symptoms is the typical candidate for cardiac rehabilitation. Unstable angina, acutely decompensated heart failure with rapid weight gain and orthopnea, and uncontrolled tachyarrhythmia are conditions that need medical stabilization before supervised exercise starts. Each of the other three should be sent back to the responsible physician.
  55. The correct answer is C (Date, explanation given including risks and alternatives, client questions, and the client's verbal agreement): Documentation should show that the consent process happened: what was explained, including material risks and alternatives, the client's questions, and the form of agreement. A bare statement or a general form does not show what the client was told. Attendance alone is not a record of agreement to a specific technique.
  56. The correct answer is D (Check that two fingers slide under the belt, then hold it with an underhand grip): A gait belt sits snugly at the waist over clothing, tight enough to stay in place but loose enough for two fingers to fit under it. An underhand grip lets the physiotherapist support the belt and guide her centre of mass. A belt that is too tight can restrict breathing, and holding under the arms or clothing risks injury and loss of control.
  57. The correct answer is A (Slow stretch avoids the velocity-dependent stretch reflex, so resistance rises less than with quick movement): Spasticity is velocity dependent, so a quick movement triggers a stronger stretch reflex and more resistance. A slow, sustained stretch keeps reflex activity low and allows the tissue to lengthen. Spindle fatigue and direct fibre lengthening do not explain the technique, and capsule protection is not the reason here.
  58. The correct answer is B (Stay with her on her side and monitor breathing until she wakes, because airway risk persists): After a convulsion stops, the person is in the postictal state with reduced consciousness, so the recovery position keeps the airway open and lets secretions drain. Someone should stay, watch breathing and responsiveness, and call for help if breathing is poor or she does not recover. Sitting her up risks a fall, and leaving her alone or walking her around removes monitoring while she is still impaired.
  59. The correct answer is B (Analyzes what happened, identifies a knowledge gap in vestibular testing, and sets a learning goal): Effective reflection analyzes causes, names a gap in her own knowledge and commits to a specific learning action. Describing the history and outcomes only records events, and comparing results with a unit average gives reassurance rather than a learning need. Naming a knowledge gap without a committed plan stops short of the goal setting that supports continuing competence.
  60. The correct answer is A (Review the plan beforehand, stay within arm's reach, and step in if safety is at risk): A client with recent falls carries a real risk, and the educator remains responsible for client safety during student care. Staying close and reviewing the plan lets the student practise while the educator can intervene at once. Distance or leaving the student alone puts the client at risk.
Case CHB10

A 67-year-old woman is six weeks after an anterior myocardial infarction treated with a stent. Her ejection fraction is 35 percent. She is starting outpatient cardiac rehabilitation and takes metoprolol and ramipril. Resting heart rate is 78 beats per minute and blood pressure is 124/76 mmHg. She is comfortable at rest, but climbing one flight of stairs makes her tired and short of breath.

  1. The correct answer is A (Class II: comfortable at rest, but ordinary activity causes fatigue or breathlessness): NYHA class II describes slight limitation of physical activity: the person is comfortable at rest, but ordinary activity such as climbing a flight of stairs causes fatigue, palpitations or dyspnea. Class I has no symptoms with ordinary activity, class III has symptoms with less than ordinary activity such as walking on the flat, and class IV has symptoms at rest. Her ejection fraction does not determine the class.
  2. The correct answer is B (3.3 METs, found by dividing the oxygen cost by 3.5): One MET equals 3.5 mL/kg/min, so the workload is the oxygen cost divided by 3.5, and 11.5 divided by 3.5 is about 3.3 METs. The resting component is already part of the oxygen cost from the walking equation, so removing it before dividing understates the workload. Adding 1 to the quotient or subtracting rather than dividing gives values that do not fit slow level walking.
  3. The correct answer is B (Systolic pressure falling from 124 to 106 mmHg as workload increased, with light-headedness): A fall in systolic blood pressure of more than 10 mmHg as workload increases, especially with symptoms, suggests the heart cannot raise output and is a standard reason to stop exercise. A heart rate rise to 104 with an RPE of 13 is an expected response, mild breathlessness with full sentences and 94 percent saturation is acceptable, and mild calf tiredness that eases is common. She should sit or lie down and be assessed by a physician before continuing.
  4. The correct answer is A (Stop the session, keep her safe and tell the nurse or physician immediately about the decline): The falling score with increasing drowsiness and worsening headache suggests neurological deterioration, such as bleeding, which needs urgent medical review. Treatment should stop and the team be told at once. Extra screening, waiting until the end of the day or waiting until tomorrow all delay a time-critical response.
  5. The correct answer is C (Sit to dress, pace tasks with rests, and schedule the hardest task for his best time): Energy conservation means sitting for tasks, pacing with planned rests, and scheduling demanding activities for when energy is highest. Doing everything in one session or hurrying raises oxygen demand, and avoiding showering removes a manageable task instead of adapting it.
  6. The correct answer is D (344 metres, adding the 14 metres of the partial length to 11 full lengths): The six-minute walk distance is the total ground covered, so 11 full lengths of 30 m (330 m) plus the 14 m already walked gives 344 m. Rounding the partial length up to a full length overstates the result, and ignoring it understates it. Standardized recording uses the partial distance measured to the nearest metre.
  7. The correct answer is D (Confirm reliable connectivity, private space and consent before starting, because these determine safe virtual care): Virtual care depends on adequate connectivity, a private setting and informed consent including privacy limits, and these are confirmed before the session starts. Solving problems after beginning risks an unsafe or non-private session. Deferring care to a handout ignores that remote assessment is acceptable, and a full home program in one visit does not replace planning for access and safety.
  8. The correct answer is B (Sit him upright with legs lowered, loosen tight clothing, and check the bladder and bowel): High pressure, a slow pulse, headache and flushing above the lesion make up autonomic dysreflexia. Sitting him up lowers pressure by gravity, and removing the trigger starts with loosened clothing and checking bladder and bowel. Lying flat or binding the abdomen raises pressure further, and exercise does not explain these findings.
  9. The correct answer is B (Use the professional interpreter for the assessment and thank her son for his offer): Trained interpreters are bound by confidentiality and know medical terms, while family members may filter or alter information and may limit what the client says. Using the son even for simple questions risks the same problems. Relying on gestures in English cannot support a full history or informed participation.
  10. The correct answer is D (No, because amnesia continues until he can form reliable ongoing memories of day-to-day events): Post-traumatic amnesia ends when the person shows continuous memory for ongoing daily events, not simply correct orientation. Orientation can return while new learning is still failing. Memory for events before the injury is often never recovered and is not required to declare the end of the amnesia.
  11. The correct answer is C (Pain increased by passive great toe dorsiflexion while a medial-lateral calcaneal squeeze is painless): Passive great toe dorsiflexion tightens the plantar fascia through the windlass mechanism and reproduces fascial pain, while a painless calcaneal squeeze makes a stress fracture less likely. A painful squeeze with rest or night pain points toward bone stress injury. Posterior heel pain with resisted plantar flexion suggests an Achilles source.

Practice Test 8 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is D (Use the 33 to 42 cm cuff after five minutes seated, arm at heart level): The bladder should encircle most of the arm and be about 40 percent as wide as its circumference, which matches the cuff covering 33 to 42 cm for his 41 cm arm. A small cuff over-reads and a too large cuff under-reads. Five minutes of seated rest and an arm supported at heart level are required for accuracy.
  2. The correct answer is C (Negative, because disorganized thinking or altered consciousness is also required and neither is present): The Confusion Assessment Method requires acute onset with a fluctuating course, plus inattention, plus either disorganized thinking or an altered level of consciousness. Here the first two features are present but the client is alert and logical, so the algorithm is not met. This does not exclude delirium, so continued observation and reporting of the change are still reasonable.
  3. The correct answer is C (Full sit-ups with hands behind the head, because repeated spinal flexion loads the vertebral bodies): Loaded or repeated spinal flexion concentrates compression on the anterior vertebral bodies, where osteoporotic wedge fractures occur, so full sit-ups should be removed. Prone press-ups on the elbows are extension work and are generally encouraged. Hip abduction keeps the trunk neutral, and rising from a chair involves far less flexion load than repeated sit-ups.
  4. The correct answer is D (Defer exercise and advise him to contact his diabetes team promptly about the ketones): High glucose with moderate ketones and symptoms suggests insufficient insulin, and exercise can push him toward ketoacidosis. Exercise is deferred and his diabetes team is contacted. Light exercise still carries risk, and giving carbohydrate is wrong when glucose is already high.
  5. The correct answer is A (Withhold heat and exercise, and arrange urgent medical assessment, because a deep vein thrombosis is possible): Unilateral calf swelling, warmth and pain after prolonged immobility suggest a deep vein thrombosis, which needs urgent medical assessment. Heat, massage and exercise can worsen the problem or dislodge a clot. Cold and elevation treat a strain but delay diagnosis of a potentially dangerous condition.
  6. The correct answer is A (Confidentiality may be set aside as needed when a serious, imminent risk threatens an identifiable person): Privacy laws and professional ethics allow disclosure without consent when needed to prevent serious and imminent harm to an identifiable person, limited to what is necessary. Absolute secrecy is not required, and no court order is needed. Not every troubling statement meets that threshold, so judgment about seriousness and imminence still matters.
  7. The correct answer is B (Remove the superiority claim and describe the course accurately, because advertising must be truthful and verifiable): Advertising by a registrant must be accurate, not misleading and capable of being verified, so both the unverifiable best-in-city claim and the overstated credential must go. Client satisfaction does not support a superiority claim, a disclaimer cannot rescue an unprovable statement, and course titles are not exempt from truthfulness rules.
  8. The correct answer is A (Arm support fixes the shoulder girdle so accessory muscles lift the chest more efficiently): Leaning forward with the arms supported stabilizes the shoulder girdle, so accessory muscles can elevate the rib cage and the diaphragm works at a better length. It does not strengthen the diaphragm in days, change alveolar gas transfer through abdominal pressure, or increase oxygen through venous return.
  9. The correct answer is A (Stay calm, help him lean forward supported, and aim a handheld fan at his face): A calm presence, a supported forward lean and airflow from a handheld fan to the face are simple, evidence supported ways to reduce the sensation of breathlessness. Extra rescue doses beyond the written plan are not hers to direct, and breath holding worsens air trapping. Calling paramedics for each episode ignores the action plan already agreed with his physician.
  10. The correct answer is B (Do scheduled weight shifts or push-ups and check skin over the ischial areas after the session): With reduced sensation he cannot rely on discomfort, so regular weight shifts and post-session skin checks are needed. Sitting still concentrates pressure, and a hard surface increases pressure over the ischial areas.
  11. The correct answer is B (About 4.8 kg, which is 5 percent of 96 kg, as a first goal): Five percent of 96 kg is 0.05 times 96, which equals 4.8 kg. A loss of at least 5 percent of body weight with exercise is linked to meaningful pain reduction in knee osteoarthritis. Ten percent is a larger target than stated, dividing by 5 gives a fifth of the weight rather than a twentieth, and 2.5 percent is only half the target.
  12. The correct answer is D (Pain fell from 7 to 2 out of 10 and met the goal of 3): The starting point on the chart is 7 and the final point is 2, which is below the goal line of 3, so the goal was met. Reporting an ending value of 3 misreads the last point, and a 50 percent drop miscalculates the change since 5 of 7 is about 71 percent. Describing the end value as above the goal reverses the meaning of a goal of 3 or less.
  13. The correct answer is C (Report the pattern to her physician for review, and set up a bedside commode): The falls follow a wearing-off pattern, and medication timing is a medical decision, so the physiotherapist reports the pattern to the physician and reduces risk with a bedside commode. Training in the off state exposes her to falls, a walker does not fix freezing and rigidity, and changing doses on her own is outside her scope and unsafe.
  14. The correct answer is C (Refer for physician assessment and nerve testing while splinting, because thenar wasting indicates advanced nerve damage): Reduced thumb abduction strength, visible thenar wasting and impaired two-point discrimination indicate motor and sensory axonal involvement, which warrants prompt referral rather than a prolonged trial of conservative care. Splinting can start in parallel. Exercise or mobilization alone would delay decompression assessment when the nerve is already losing function.
  15. The correct answer is B (Switch to pacing, keeping activity below the level that triggers symptoms, with no fixed increases): Symptoms that appear a day after activity and last for days indicate post-exertional symptom exacerbation, for which graded increases can cause relapses. Pacing within a symptom-free energy envelope is the safer approach. Pushing harder ignores the delayed crash, and total rest causes further deconditioning and removes necessary daily function.
  16. The correct answer is D (Give him a mask, move him out of shared areas, and arrange urgent medical assessment): The history raises strong suspicion of active pulmonary tuberculosis, so the priority is source control and removal from shared spaces while he is assessed, before any therapy continues. A mask alone in an open gym still exposes other clients, and hand hygiene does not address airborne spread. Delaying a week leaves a possibly infectious person untested and in contact with others.
  17. The correct answer is B (Positive J sign, indicating lateral maltracking, because the patella shifts out then returns medially): A J sign is lateral patellar deviation near terminal extension followed by medial return, tracing an upside-down J, and indicates maltracking. The apprehension sign tests passive lateral glide, and the Clarke test is a compression test with poor reliability. Lateral deviation in active extension is an abnormal finding in a person with pain.
  18. The correct answer is B (Release only the progress summary named in the consent, then document what was disclosed): Disclosure is limited to what the client consented to, so the progress summary may be released and nothing more. Payment for treatment does not create a right to the full chart. A verbal disclosure of diagnosis is still a disclosure beyond the consent, and withholding everything ignores the valid consent that does exist.
  19. The correct answer is D (Slow heel raises with toes extended on a towel roll, three sets, every second day): High-load plantar fascia loading uses slow, heavy heel raises with the toes extended over a towel roll to tension the fascia through the windlass mechanism, usually done every second day to allow recovery. Fast unloaded raises give too little load, stretching alone adds little, and hopping is a much later stage.
  20. The correct answer is A (Prescribe by perceived exertion, accepting a lower heart rate than land targets because of immersion effects): Immersion raises central blood volume and lowers heart rate at a given workload by roughly 10 to 15 beats per minute, so land-derived targets overestimate the intensity needed in water. His perceived exertion matched on land and in water, so exertion ratings are the more valid guide. Chasing the land heart rate would push him beyond the intended effort.
  21. The correct answer is A (Grade I to II small-amplitude oscillations within the pain-free range, aiming at pain relief): When pain dominates and irritability is high, small-amplitude oscillations in the early to mid range (Grade I to II) are used for pain modulation. Grades III and IV address stiffness at or into resistance, and a Grade V thrust is a manipulation that is unsuitable for a highly irritable presentation.
  22. The correct answer is C (Pause, check in about how she feels, and offer to modify or stop the hands-on work): Her heart rate was steady until touch began and then climbed, which suggests physiological arousal even though she says she is fine. Pausing, checking in and offering to modify or stop respects her autonomy and lets her choose. Moving to another region or continuing on verbal agreement ignores the trend, while ending the session and referring on removes her choice.
  23. The correct answer is D (The sacrum and heels, because the bony prominences compress thin skin directly against the mattress surface): In supine lying the sacrum, heels and occiput are the main weight-bearing areas where bone lies close to the skin. The knees are not in contact with the mattress in supine, and the shoulders and thoracic spine do not carry the same concentrated load.
  24. The correct answer is C (Share her findings with the team and request a case conference to explore supports or delay): Her safety is at risk, and objective findings should be put to the whole team in a respectful forum where alternatives such as home supports, a main-floor set-up or a short delay can be weighed. Leaving the date to her answer alone, writing a handout, or only recording concerns does not resolve the unsafe stairs in a way the team can act on.
  25. The correct answer is A (Inferior capsular laxity consistent with multidirectional instability, because the humeral head translates downward under traction): A sulcus sign appears when inferior traction translates the humeral head downward, revealing a lax inferior capsule and rotator interval. With generalized hypermobility and bilateral symptoms, multidirectional instability is likely. A cuff tear would migrate the head upward, and a stiff capsule would resist traction rather than allow a gap.
  26. The correct answer is D (Press the lid with the palm and carry the bag over the forearm, sparing finger joints): Joint protection reduces stress on small inflamed joints by using the palm, forearm and larger joints and by spreading load. A rubber pad helps grip, but turning the lid and carrying by the handles still loads the finger joints. Soaking helps stiffness but maximal effort and one-handed carrying add stress, and hooking a bag on the fingertips concentrates force on small joints.
  27. The correct answer is B (Train all staff, record attendance, and audit compliance after a set period): A policy changes practice when all staff are trained, attendance is documented and compliance is later checked so gaps can be corrected. One meeting with signatures, an informal demonstration by one therapist, or an email tracked by read receipts shows exposure but not that every employee applies the steps. Only the audit confirms consistent use within the month.
  28. The correct answer is A (GOLD 3, severe, because the post-bronchodilator FEV1 is between 30 and 49 percent of predicted): The GOLD grade is based on post-bronchodilator FEV1 percent predicted once the ratio confirms obstruction (below 0.70). GOLD 1 is 80 percent or more, GOLD 2 is 50 to 79, GOLD 3 is 30 to 49 and GOLD 4 is below 30. At 48 percent she is GOLD 3.
  29. The correct answer is C (Test serratus anterior and screen the long thoracic nerve, because medial winging implicates that pathway): Lifting of the medial border of the scapula during a push-up is the typical sign of serratus anterior weakness, which can follow long thoracic nerve compression from heavy pack straps. Spinal accessory palsy produces lateral displacement and shoulder droop instead. Surgery is premature before the cause is identified, and stretching does not address a weak, possibly nerve-related muscle.
  30. The correct answer is B (Stop exercise and arrange urgent medical assessment, because one sided calf signs suggest a clot): Unilateral calf pain, swelling and warmth in a pregnant client are classic signs of deep vein thrombosis, a hypercoagulable state risk, so exercise must stop and she needs urgent medical review. Massage could dislodge a clot. Checking blood pressure for the headache would delay assessment of the more urgent finding.
  31. The correct answer is A (Issue receipts with the true service dates, because records must reflect what actually occurred): A receipt is a record of a service that was delivered, so the dates must be the real ones even when that affects reimbursement. Whether the visits are eligible without a referral is the insurer's decision, and the client can ask it directly. Dating receipts later or withholding them misrepresents the record or denies the client a document she is entitled to.
  32. The correct answer is C (Week 3, when the pain score first reached the reference line of 5): The baseline score is 7, so a 2 point improvement corresponds to a score of 5, which the client reached in week 3. In week 2 the reduction was only 1.5 points, so the threshold was not yet met. Later weeks show continued change but not the first time the threshold was reached.
  33. The correct answer is C (Straight hand, hook fist, full fist, tabletop, straight fist, then return to a straight hand): The standard tendon gliding series moves from a straight hand through hook fist, full fist, tabletop and straight fist. Each position maximizes excursion of the flexor digitorum superficialis or profundus in turn. Rearranging the order loses the progressive differential glide that the exercise is designed to produce.
  34. The correct answer is A (Return to three 20 minute sessions, because raising two variables together flared his pain): In week four both frequency and duration increased and his next day pain rose from 4 to 6. Returning to the last tolerated load and then progressing one variable at a time protects the arthritic knee. Continuing or adding volume ignores the pain response.
  35. The correct answer is C (Stop the test, seat or lay him down, and monitor blood pressure and symptoms until recovery): A fall in systolic pressure of more than 10 mmHg despite a rising workload, together with lightheadedness, is a standard reason to end an exercise test. Continuing or only slowing risks syncope or an ischemic event. He should be seated or positioned supine with monitoring until pressure and symptoms settle.
  36. The correct answer is D (Defer vigorous exercise until medically controlled, because untreated thyrotoxicosis already raises cardiac workload at rest): Untreated hyperthyroidism produces tachycardia and increased cardiac demand, so vigorous exercise is postponed until medication brings the thyroid under control. Exercise does not lower the resting rate in this state, and a raised metabolism is not a fitness advantage. Heat intolerance is only one of several concerns, with cardiac load the most important.
  37. The correct answer is D (Sit at the child's eye level with an open posture and relaxed expression): Lowering yourself to the child's eye level with relaxed, open body language reduces the sense of threat and makes him part of the conversation. Speaking only to the mother leaves a shy child out, and steady eye contact with direct questions can feel intense. Entertaining with a toy and an animated voice may distract but does not build comfort with the physiotherapist.
  38. The correct answer is C (Incomplete, because preserved sensation at the anal segments counts as sacral sparing): An injury is incomplete if there is any sensory or motor function in the lowest sacral segments, and deep anal pressure counts as sacral sparing. Absence of limb function does not make an injury complete. Reflex activity in the legs does not count as preserved function for classification.
  39. The correct answer is A (Record a possible latex allergy and use latex-free gloves and equipment until it is clarified): Symptoms after exposure to latex products suggest sensitization, and reactions can progress, so precautions are taken until it is clarified.   40. Forgetting the protected time or the assessments inflates the number, and splitting the rest with extra documentation is not part of the schedule described.
  41. A combined list leaves the conflict in place. The physician's goals do not replace his, and one measurable goal ignores communication and daily tasks.
  42. The fourth intercostal level is the standard site for this measure.
  43. The correct answer is A (Assess the client promptly in person, because calf tenderness with swelling needs evaluation before more exercise): Calf tenderness with swelling raises concern about a possible deep vein thrombosis or other serious problem, which needs prompt physiotherapist assessment and not further delegated exercise. A scheduled review date should not override a change in findings. Girth measurements or ice do not replace assessment.
  44. The correct answer is A (Hold a case conference with her to clarify goals, share rationales, and agree one plan): Conflicting recommendations are best resolved by bringing the team and the client together, sharing the reasoning behind each position and aligning on her goals. Her wishes matter but are informed by the team's advice. Excluding her or declaring one profession the leader leaves the disagreement and the criticism in place.
  45. The correct answer is B (Early gains passed 10 points, but the plateau since week 4 warrants reviewing the plan): Her score fell 14 points by week 4, which exceeds the commonly cited 10-point threshold, but only 3 points more over the next four weeks, so progress has plateaued. A plateau is a prompt to reassess contributing factors and modify the program. The final score stays in the moderate band, yet the overall change from the severe range is meaningful, and the Oswestry can be repeated as often as needed.
  46. The correct answer is A (Cancel the session and direct her to urgent medical assessment, because fever may signal neutropenic infection): Fever shortly after a chemotherapy cycle may be febrile neutropenia, which is a medical emergency, so exercise is cancelled and she is sent for urgent assessment. Reducing the intensity, waiting for the temperature to fall or moving to a private room all delay the care she needs.
  47. The correct answer is D (Acknowledge the dizziness concern calmly, offer to review it together, and suggest meeting privately): Acknowledging the legitimate safety concern keeps the focus on the client and lowers tension, while offering a private discussion lets both parties address the tone and the clinical question.   48. The correct answer is B (Pain appears at a consistent walking distance and eases within minutes of standing still): Vascular claudication is reproducible: the same walking distance brings on ischemic muscle pain, and standing rest for a few minutes relieves it because oxygen supply catches up with demand. Neurogenic claudication varies with spinal posture, is relieved by flexion such as sitting or leaning forward, and is often worse downhill. Persistent dermatomal paresthesia points toward nerve root irritation.
  49. The correct answer is D (Features of central sensitization, with heightened responsiveness of pain pathways in the nervous system): Pain spreading beyond the injured area, pain to light touch (allodynia) and prolonged after-effects of testing are classic signs of an amplified central pain response. Ongoing tissue injury and acute inflammation would produce local, proportionate findings, and nerve root compression would follow a defined dermatomal distribution.
  50. The correct answer is A (Total of 8: eye 2, verbal 2 and motor 4 (withdrawal from pain)): Eye opening to pain scores 2, incomprehensible sounds score 2, and pulling the hand away from pressure is withdrawal, which scores 4, giving 8. Recognizable but inappropriate words would score 3 for the verbal part. Abnormal flexion (3) and extension (2) are posturing responses and not the same as withdrawal.
  51. The correct answer is B (Walk early and often, and perform ankle pumps while in bed or sitting): Early mobilization and ankle pumps use the calf muscle pump to move blood and add to drug prophylaxis. Staying still prolongs immobility and raises clot risk. Calf massage may dislodge a clot, and adding aspirin is not a measure she can take on a physiotherapist's advice.
  52. The correct answer is D (Progress to sustained end-range mobilization and stretching, because low irritability means pain no longer limits tolerance): Falling pain with a flat range curve indicates a stiffness-dominant, low-irritability stage, where sustained end-range work is tolerated and needed to change the capsule.   53. Early performance, client comments or comparison with earlier students do not measure this student against the competencies.
  54. Once the plan exists, carefully selected routine tasks may be assigned to a competent assistant. Teaching home exercises, applying set modalities and recording tolerance are tasks, not the professional decisions.
  55. Delaying stretching wastes the window, and stretching does not displace bound toxin. Active practice is encouraged, and casting with a gait delay removes the functional training.
  56. The correct answer is B (Escalate to the team leader through the organization's conflict process, because discharge is two days away): After direct attempts have failed and client safety is at stake, the next step is escalation through the organization's process, starting with the team leader. Giving in avoids the safety question, airing it in front of the family is inappropriate, and waiting is not workable with discharge in two days.
  57. The correct answer is C (Lhermitte sign, a common symptom of cervical cord demyelination that is not harmful in itself): A shock-like sensation provoked by neck flexion is Lhermitte sign, which arises from demyelinated dorsal column fibres in the cervical cord and is common in people with multiple sclerosis. With no new weakness or bladder change it does not signal a new emergency. Spurling testing involves extension and rotation and targets root compression, and the Babinski response is a reflex, not a provoked sensation.
  58. The correct answer is A (Sport-specific exercise such as running drills, with no activities that risk head impact): The graded return follows symptom-limited activity, light aerobic exercise, then sport-specific exercise without head impact risk. Each stage needs at least 24 hours without symptom return before progressing. Non-contact drills and full-contact practice come later, and repeating the aerobic stage for a week is unnecessary when she is symptom free.
  59. Adding sock ply restores total contact and can be reviewed with the prosthetist. Definitive socket fabrication is delayed until volume is stable, and a change this large exceeds measurement error.
  60.   61. The correct answer is B (46 percent, in the severe disability band, because the total is divided by 50): The Oswestry score is the total divided by the maximum of 50, multiplied by 100, giving 23 divided by 50, or 46 percent. In the commonly used banding, 41 to 60 percent is severe disability, while the highest categories begin at 61 percent. Reporting the raw total as a percentage understates his disability.
  62. The correct answer is D (Ask privately, with her partner out of the room, using open, non-judgemental questions): Asking about violence must be done when the person is alone, since a partner's presence can put the client at risk and prevent honest answers. Waiting for disclosure leaves many clients unasked. A brochure alone avoids the conversation that opens the door to help.
  63. A third reminder or a general poster does not address the pattern. Going straight to the college skips the employer's responsibility to act first.
  64. The correct answer is C (Immobilize in plantarflexion and arrange urgent orthopedic referral, because findings suggest complete Achilles tendon rupture): A sudden pop, a palpable defect, bruising and a positive Thompson test indicate complete tendon rupture rather than a tendinopathy flare. Loading or massage at the gap can worsen the tear.
  65. The correct answer is A (Raise the legs to 45 degrees for two minutes, then lower them over the bed edge): In the Buerger test, the legs are raised to about 45 degrees for around two minutes while the physiotherapist watches for pallor, then lowered into a dependent position to see how quickly colour returns and whether dependent rubor develops. Marked pallor on elevation and delayed filling suggest severe arterial insufficiency. Calf squeeze, pitting and nail-bed refill assess other problems or perfusion without positional colour change.
  66. The client then actively rotates 45 degrees to each side, and imaging is needed only if he cannot. Passive testing with overpressure is not part of the rule, and discharge without testing skips a required step.
  67. The correct answer is B (Blood carries less oxygen per litre, so the heart and lungs work harder for muscle demand): In anemia the saturation of the available hemoglobin is normal, but total oxygen content is reduced, so cardiac output and ventilation must rise earlier to supply working muscle. The oximeter reads saturation, not content, which is why it stays normal. Lung diffusion and muscle mitochondria are not the primary problem here.
  68. The correct answer is A (Regular moderate aerobic and resistance exercise, progressed gradually to match her tolerance): Supervised moderate aerobic and resistance exercise has the strongest evidence for reducing cancer-related fatigue. Rest and inactivity deepen deconditioning and worsen fatigue. Massage may help comfort but has weaker evidence, and education without exercise misses the most effective component.
  69. The correct answer is A (Short plain-language sentences, key actions in bullet points, and clear supporting pictures): Materials for lower reading levels use plain words, short sentences, a clear layout and pictures that show the action. Compact fonts and long paragraphs are harder to read even when the words are simple. Medical vocabulary adds reading load even when it is defined.
  70. The correct answer is D (Change to portable oxygen or secure the tubing so it cannot catch on the walker): Oxygen tubing stretched across the floor in front of the walker is a trip and disconnection hazard, so the set-up must be corrected before he stands. His saturation is adequate on the current flow, so increasing the flow or removing oxygen is not indicated. Baseline measures are useful but do not remove the hazard before he stands.
  71. The correct answer is B (Refer her to a pelvic health trained physiotherapist, because competence limits what is safe): Practising within competence means recognizing when a client's needs exceed current education and experience and referring on. General core exercises do not replace pelvic health training, treating while learning exposes the client to avoidable risk, and relying on familiar techniques until she fails to improve delays appropriate care.
  72. The correct answer is B (Remove the cord from the walkway and dry the spill before bringing the client in): The cord and wet floor are hazards the physiotherapist can remove, and removing them is better than managing them. Signs and close guarding still leave a client with a recent fall exposed to a trip or slip. Moving to the corridor changes the environment without addressing the room hazard.
  73. The correct answer is A (To document essential job tasks and physical requirements so worker capacity can be matched to them): A job demands analysis describes what the job actually requires, such as lifting weights, postures and frequencies, giving a benchmark for comparison with the worker's abilities. Measuring capacity is the role of a functional capacity evaluation, while causation and compensation decisions are made by other parties.
  74. The correct answer is A (You have attended every visit despite sore knees, which shows real commitment): An affirmation recognizes a strength, effort or past success of the client. The worried statement is a reflection, the question about worries is an open question, and the last statement is a summary. All four are valid skills, but only the attendance comment affirms.
  75. The correct answer is D (The physiotherapist stays accessible to the assistant and reassesses the client at planned intervals): The level of supervision should match the task, the client and the assistant's competence. Here a defined plan, accessibility and planned reassessment keep the physiotherapist accountable. Changing parameters is a clinical decision, and waiting for a complaint delays reassessment.
  76. Trialing teach-back and reassessing turns the feedback into improvement. Waiting another year or judging progress by her own impression repeats the blind spot, and extra observation before any change delays a simple, low-risk trial.
  77. The correct answer is C (Inhale slowly and deeply, hold for about three seconds at peak volume, then exhale and rest): Effective use of an incentive spirometer is a slow, deep inhalation held for several seconds at maximal volume, which sustains alveolar inflation. Case CHB11

Jordan, aged 15, attends a sports clinic alone after a knee injury in basketball. A parent paid the booking fee online. When the physiotherapist explains the proposed treatment and its risks, Jordan restates them accurately and asks that nothing be shared with the parent. The physiotherapist also wants to photograph the knee swelling for a student teaching presentation.

  1. The correct answer is D (Jordan's demonstrated ability to understand the information and appreciate the consequences of the decision): Consent depends on capacity to understand and appreciate the information, not on a fixed age, and a capable young person may consent for themselves. Paying the fee does not give the parent decision authority. Having taken a health course is not what establishes capacity for this decision.
  2. The correct answer is D (Specific consent for the photograph and its teaching use, which Jordan can decline without affecting care): Photographs used outside direct care need their own informed consent that names the purpose, and the client must be free to refuse. Treatment consent does not extend to teaching uses. A capable young person decides for themselves, and consent for one purpose does not stretch to unlimited later uses.
  3. The correct answer is C (Say she cannot share details without Jordan's consent, and invite Jordan to decide what to share): A capable young person controls their own health information, so disclosure to a parent needs Jordan's permission, even when the parent pays. Billing does not entitle the payer to clinical details. Privacy law does allow sharing with family when the client agrees, so a flat refusal to discuss anything is also wrong.
  4. The correct answer is D (Stiffness that settles within thirty minutes and involvement of a few asymmetric weight-bearing joints): Osteoarthritis typically causes short-lived morning stiffness and affects a few weight-bearing joints asymmetrically. Prolonged stiffness with symmetric small-joint swelling points toward inflammatory arthritis. An abruptly hot joint with fever raises concern for infection, and constant night pain with weight loss suggests serious pathology rather than degenerative change.
  5. The correct answer is C (Treat with 15 grams of fast carbohydrate, recheck in 15 minutes, and delay exercise until recovered): A reading below 4.0 mmol/L with symptoms is hypoglycemia, which is treated with 15 grams of fast carbohydrate and rechecked in 15 minutes before any exercise. Starting activity, a very large meal or a seated session does not treat the low glucose, and a sulfonylurea keeps lowering glucose.
  6. The correct answer is D (90 percent, calculated from the 90 true negatives among the 100 clients without the condition): Specificity is true negatives divided by all clients without the condition, here 90 of 100. The 60 percent figure is sensitivity, 75 percent is the positive predictive value and 82 percent is the negative predictive value. Each uses a different denominator.
  7. The correct answer is C (Key findings, working impression, plan, expected course and any need for further tests): A referral reply should be brief and give the physician what is needed for decisions: findings, impression, plan, expected course and any request for tests. Exercise detail belongs in the chart, and the full assessment form buries the key points. Goals and personal concerns matter but are secondary to what the physician needs to act on.
  8. The correct answer is A (Resistance exercise for major muscle groups on at least two days weekly, at a moderate effort): Canadian 24-hour movement guidelines for adults call for muscle-strengthening activities using major muscle groups on at least two days per week. Training every two weeks is too infrequent to keep strength. Maximal daily effort is unnecessary and risky, and aerobic work does not maintain strength.
  9. The correct answer is B (Delirium, because acute onset with fluctuating attention and arousal suggests a medical cause needing prompt reporting): Delirium is defined by an acute change with fluctuating attention and level of arousal, and it signals an underlying medical problem such as infection, pain, medications or electrolyte disturbance. Dementia and depression develop over weeks to months and do not fluctuate within a day this way. Dismissing the change as expected adjustment delays treatment of a reversible cause.
  10. The correct answer is C (Stop and sit or stand still, do quick pelvic floor squeezes, then walk calmly): Urge suppression uses stillness, quick pelvic floor contractions that inhibit the bladder reflex, and then calm walking at the point when the urge settles. Rushing reinforces the urge and bearing down worsens leakage. Cutting fluids concentrates the urine and irritates the bladder.
  11. The correct answer is C (Hold spinal loading and refer for medical assessment and imaging, since long-term steroids raise fracture risk): Long-term corticosteroid use reduces bone density, and new focal midline tenderness after minor loading suggests a vertebral compression fracture. Imaging and medical assessment come before any spinal loading. Mobilization and extension exercise could worsen a fracture, and pain-free ranges do not exclude one.
  12. The correct answer is B (Plan comfort positioning and assisted sitting for her goals, and explain this to her daughter): Palliative care follows the client's own goals, and a capable client has the right to direct her care. Comfort positioning and assisted sitting meet her stated aims, while explaining the reasoning supports the family. Treating the daughter's wish as decisive or abandoning physiotherapy would both fail the client.
  13. The correct answer is A (Raise the handles to wrist crease height, giving about 20 to 30 degrees of elbow flexion): A walker is correctly fitted when the handles sit at wrist crease height with the arms relaxed at her sides, which gives about 20 to 30 degrees of elbow flexion. A low frame pulls the trunk forward and straightens the elbows. Raising it to chest level would elevate the shoulders, and verbal cueing alone does not fix a faulty set-up.
  14. The correct answer is D (Reduce the treadmill speed now, since heart rate has passed the upper target of 127): At 60 percent of reserve the upper target is 70 plus 0.6 times 95, which is about 127. Her heart rate of 138 at ten minutes exceeds it, so intensity should come down. Staying under the age-predicted maximum is not the training limit, and a call to the physician is not needed for a controlled overshoot.
  15. The correct answer is A (Capacity is assessed for each decision, and he appears able to understand and appreciate this one): Capacity is specific to a decision and a moment, and a diagnosis does not remove it. Adults are presumed capable, and he shows understanding of the proposal and its consequences. A health professional proposing treatment must form a view about capacity for that treatment. Disagreeing with advice does not by itself show incapacity.
  16. The correct answer is B (A periodic audit of randomly selected charts using a checklist built from the standard): A random chart audit against a checklist drawn from the standard measures actual documentation, can be repeated with the same criteria, and allows year-to-year comparison. Satisfaction surveys and self-ratings measure perception rather than the content of notes. Reviewing only complaint-related charts gives a biased sample that cannot show overall compliance.
  17. The correct answer is D (Pause exercise and refer her to her neurologist, because persistent new symptoms may indicate a relapse): New neurological symptoms lasting more than 24 hours without heat, infection or fever suggest a possible relapse, which needs medical assessment before exercise continues. Pseudo-exacerbations from heat settle with cooling and rest, and she had no heat exposure. Adjusting intensity or adding exercises delays the medical review the situation needs.
  18. The correct answer is B (Upright posture and activity increase functional residual capacity, which reduces dependent atelectasis): Sitting up and moving increase functional residual capacity and ventilation of dependent lung regions, which prevents or reverses atelectasis after upper abdominal surgery. Endorphin release, a faster breathing rate and small changes in airway tone may occur but are not the main benefit. Opioids and pain encourage shallow breathing, so position and activity matter.
  19. The correct answer is A (An 18-point gain exceeds the minimal detectable change, so improvement is real and goals advance): The LEFS rose from 28 to 46, an 18-point change that is twice the 9-point minimal detectable change, so it is unlikely to be measurement error. The first four weeks gained only 6 points, below the threshold, and the later 12-point gain is also above it. Because the total change is real, goals and the program can be updated.
  20. The correct answer is A (Co-create a park play participation goal with the family and build therapy activities around it): Family-centred care treats parents as partners and builds goals around what matters to the family, here participation in park play. Activity-based therapy toward that goal is also supported by evidence over impairment-first approaches. Protocol-driven or externally set goals ignore the family's stated priorities.
  21. The correct answer is C (Brief protection, then early pain-tolerable walking and loading, because controlled stress supports healing and function): Current evidence favours short-term protection followed by early, pain-tolerable loading, which supports tissue remodelling and a faster return to function. Prolonged immobilization leads to stiffness and deconditioning in a grade I sprain. Ice may reduce pain briefly but has not been shown to speed ligament healing, and waiting for swelling to settle delays useful loading.
  22. The correct answer is D (You see some value in the exercises, yet you doubt they are worth the effort): A reflection restates the client's meaning, here both sides of the ambivalence, without adding advice. The first wrong response is education, the second is reassurance with a directive, and the last is a question that asks the client to defend inaction.
  23. The correct answer is C (Heart rate is blunted by the drug, so rely on perceived exertion and hold the workload): Beta blockers limit the heart rate response to exercise, so peak heart rate barely changes while effort rises, as the climbing perceived exertion shows. Intensity should be guided by the rating of perceived exertion and symptoms rather than by predicted heart rate targets. Increasing workload because heart rate seems low would push him toward excessive effort.
  24. The correct answer is A (Bone changes take one to two weeks to appear, so early radiographs can look normal): Bone destruction and periosteal reaction usually become visible on plain films after about ten to fourteen days, so early radiographs are often normal. Fever, a focal metaphyseal tenderness and refusal to bear weight keep osteomyelitis high on the list. Further imaging such as MRI and blood tests are needed.

Practice Test 9 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is B (Withhold manual therapy and arrange urgent medical assessment for suspected cervical arterial dysfunction or dissection): New neck pain with headache plus dizziness or visual symptoms raises concern for an arterial dissection or vertebrobasilar insufficiency. Positional tests lack validity to clear the artery, and mobilization or heat can add risk or delay care. Urgent medical referral takes priority over treatment.
  2. The correct answer is B (Quadriceps sets and straight leg raises in a locked brace, avoiding compressive load on the repair): With toe-touch weight bearing and a flexion limit, exercises should load the quadriceps without compressing or shearing the repair, which suits quadriceps sets and straight leg raises in extension. Leg press and mini squats load the joint, and resisted hamstring curls pull on the posterior horn through semimembranosus attachments.
  3. The correct answer is C (Raise the seat with a firm cushion so her hips stay above knee level): The photograph shows a low, deep seat that leaves the hips below the knees, forcing hip flexion past 90 degrees, the main dislocation risk after a posterior approach. A firm cushion or a higher chair keeps the hips above the knees. A knee pillow limits adduction but does not correct the flexion angle, and a footstool in front would increase flexion further.
  4. The correct answer is C (At least 150 minutes of moderate to vigorous activity weekly, accumulated in bouts of any length): Canadian guidelines advise adults to accumulate at least 150 minutes per week of moderate to vigorous aerobic activity, and any bout length counts. The ten-minute bout rule is from earlier guidance, and light activity or vigorous-only targets do not match the recommendation.
  5. The correct answer is D (A four-wheeled walker with hand brakes and a seat, allowing planned rests during walks): A four-wheeled walker lets him walk without lifting the frame, which saves energy, and the built-in seat gives a safe place to recover from breathlessness. His good balance and grip make hand brakes safe to use. A pick-up walker requires lifting each step and a cane or crutches give no seat for rests.
  6. The correct answer is C (Frequent small voids with high caffeine intake suggest bladder irritation or overactive bladder features): More than eight voids a day with small volumes and nocturia, alongside high caffeine and fluid intake, points toward bladder irritation or overactive bladder. The voiding pattern is clearly outside normal limits. The volumes are small rather than large, so nocturnal polyuria is not supported, and overflow would need a measured post-void residual before any catheter decision.
  7. The correct answer is A (Standardize the examiner, instrument, start position and landmarks, because this lowers measurement error): Goniometric error comes mainly from differences in landmark identification, positioning, and instrument use, so reliability improves when the same examiner uses the same method each time. Arm length does not remove those sources, selecting only the larger reading biases results, and changing examiners adds inter-rater error.
  8. The correct answer is B (Set a specific weekly walking goal he rates at least 7 out of 10 for confidence): Self-management action plans work best when the goal is specific and the client rates his confidence at about 7 out of 10 or higher, which predicts follow-through. A goal far above his current activity or one rated low in confidence tends to fail, and a goal chosen only by the team removes his ownership of the plan.
  9. The correct answer is C (Sweat sodium losses are higher in cystic fibrosis, so he needs extra salt and fluid): Cystic fibrosis causes abnormal chloride transport, so his sweat carries much more sodium and chloride than his peers' sweat, whatever the severity of his lung disease. During long sessions in the heat he needs added salt together with fluid. Plain water at thirst would leave him depleted, and a typical sports drink contains too little sodium to replace what he loses.
  10. The correct answer is A (Explain the protocol restriction and guide the student to explore why it protects healing tissue): Good passive range and low pain do not show that the repaired tendon has healed, so the protocol applies. Using the question as a teaching moment builds the student's reasoning. Allowing early progression breaks the surgical restriction, and simply overruling the student teaches nothing.
  11. The correct answer is A (Share the readings and symptoms, propose staged mobilization after physician review, and document the discussion): A drop of more than 20 mmHg systolic with symptoms is clinically significant, so the objective data should be shared and a medical review sought before progressing. A staged plan respects the nurse's goal of getting him up. Proceeding anyway, simply deferring, or withholding care all fail to address the safety concern together.
  12. The correct answer is B (Avoid electrical stimulation near the device, since current may interfere with its sensing): Electrical stimulation close to an implanted pacemaker can be sensed as cardiac activity and inhibit or disturb pacing, so electrical modalities over the neck, shoulder and upper back are avoided. Moving the electrodes slightly, changing body position or switching to TENS does not remove the risk because the same type of current is still delivered near the device. Exercise or manual therapy is chosen instead.
  13. The correct answer is B (Thank her for telling you, explore solutions with her, and ask to involve her mother): Building trust and problem-solving with the child respects her developing autonomy and addresses the actual barrier. Disclosing at once breaks her confidence, a warning about damage frightens without helping, and changing the splint silently leaves her concern unheard.
  14. The correct answer is A (Training improves skeletal muscle function and exercise tolerance, which reduces breathlessness at the same workload): Pulmonary rehabilitation leaves spirometry unchanged, but training improves peripheral muscle function and efficiency, so the same workload demands less ventilation and causes less dyspnea. Training does not meaningfully alter lung volumes or resting oxygen saturation. Better mood and confidence may help, yet the physiological gain in muscle function is the main reason for the benefit.
  15. The correct answer is B (Stop and seat her with supported breathing, because saturation has fallen below the agreed limit): At minute 6 her saturation is 89 percent, under the agreed stop limit, and it has declined steadily with rising heart rate. The activity should stop and she should be supported while values recover. Heart rate below the age predicted maximum or controlled pain does not override a saturation stop rule.
  16. The correct answer is B (Include back extension and hip strengthening, and avoid loaded forward flexion and trunk twisting): Extension and hip strengthening load bone with a lower vertebral compression risk, while loaded flexion and twisting raise the risk of vertebral compression fracture. Weight-bearing activity remains beneficial, so abandoning it is the wrong response, and heavy forward bending is a known hazard.
  17. The correct answer is C (Build the repetitions into a game, such as standing up to post toy letters): Embedding the movement goal inside a game keeps the task meaningful and fun for a school-age child while the repetitions continue. A sticker chart is a reward system rather than play, and it leaves the task itself unchanged. Cutting the practice short or relying on verbal coaxing from his father does not make the activity engaging in the moment.
  18. The correct answer is B (Phantom limb sensation, a painless awareness of the missing limb common soon after amputation): Phantom sensation is a non-painful perception of the missing limb and occurs in most people after amputation. Phantom pain is a painful perception in the absent part, and residual limb pain arises in the remaining tissues. The man describes no discomfort, so the experience is a sensation rather than pain.
  19. The correct answer is C (Stop the session, give 15 grams of fast-acting carbohydrate, and recheck glucose in about 15 minutes): His glucose has fallen below 4.0 mmol/L and he has symptoms, so exercise stops and 15 grams of fast-acting carbohydrate is given, with a recheck in about 15 minutes. Slow-release food acts too slowly, and water or a slower pace does not raise glucose while insulin is still active.
  20. The correct answer is C (Date of service, service provided, fee charged, and the physiotherapist's name and registration number): A receipt is a financial record that must show who provided what service, when, and at what cost, including the provider's registration number so insurers can verify eligibility. Diagnoses, progress and techniques belong in the clinical record, and a client signature or policy number is not what makes a receipt accurate.
  21. The correct answer is D (A committee reviews it, may investigate, and can resolve it or refer serious matters to discipline): A college committee screens and investigates complaints about members and can resolve them or refer serious allegations to a discipline panel. The process is regulatory and protects the public, so it is not limited to advice, a single official's decision or a private settlement, and the college can make findings about conduct. It also does not award damages, which is a matter for civil court.
  22. The correct answer is C (Ankle dorsiflexion, tested supine with resistance applied over the dorsum of the foot): The key muscle for L4 is the ankle dorsiflexors (tibialis anterior). Long toe extensors represent L5, knee extensors L3, and ankle plantarflexors S1, all of which are separate key muscles. Testing the wrong action would assign the wrong neurological level.
  23. The correct answer is D (Little finger extension past 90 degrees, elbow hyperextension past 10 degrees, thumb touching the forearm): The Beighton scale scores passive little finger extension beyond 90 degrees, thumb apposition to the forearm, elbow and knee hyperextension beyond 10 degrees, and palms flat to the floor with straight knees, each side where applicable. The other sets list normal or ordinary joint ranges that are not part of the scale.
  24. The correct answer is D (Pause, seat her, and gently ask how she feels, mentioning the tension you notice): Her words and body language disagree, and nonverbal signs of fear or fatigue during gait training can precede a fall or a cardiorespiratory problem. Stopping safely and checking in respectfully protects her and invites her to speak honestly. Continuing, delaying or quietly shortening the walk leaves the mismatch unexplored.
  25. The correct answer is C (Go to the nearest emergency department now to be assessed for possible cauda equina syndrome): Saddle numbness with new bladder dysfunction are red flags for cauda equina syndrome, a surgical emergency in which delay risks permanent loss of function. Same-day emergency assessment is required, and telephoning ahead helps the receiving team. Routine follow-up, even within days, is too slow.
  26. The correct answer is D (Thirty minutes, three days weekly, with heart rate held between 89 and 119 beats per minute): Estimated maximum is 220 minus 71, or 149 beats per minute, so 60 percent is about 89 and 80 percent about 119. Thirty minutes three days a week fits aerobic exercise recommendations for Parkinson disease. The range of 119 to 134 is 80 to 90 percent, which is too high a target here, ten minutes is a shorter session than recommended, and a range starting at her resting rate gives too little stimulus.
  27. The correct answer is C (Severe truncal ataxia with inability to sit unaided, out of proportion to the degree of vertigo): Marked trunk instability, so that the person cannot sit or stand without help even though vertigo is modest, points to a brainstem or cerebellar lesion. Fixation-suppressed, one-directional nystagmus, nausea with head movement, and mild lateral veering are all typical of an acute peripheral lesion such as vestibular neuritis. Recognizing the central pattern prompts urgent referral.
  28. The correct answer is C (Build lifting gradually with stoma support and no breath-holding, because straining raises hernia risk): The abdominal wall near a stoma stays at risk of parastomal hernia, so loading is progressed gradually with a support garment and breathing technique that avoids straining. Skin healing does not mean the fascia has regained strength, and sit-ups increase intra-abdominal pressure. A year of avoidance is unnecessary and promotes deconditioning.
  29. The correct answer is B (Pause wear, check the fit against his growth and arrange an orthotist review before reuse): Redness that persists 30 minutes after removal suggests pressure that has exceeded the skin's tolerance, and a growth spurt makes poor fit likely. Wear should pause while the orthotist reassesses the fit. A thicker sock can increase pressure in a tight shell, and continuing wear with reduced time or massage risks skin breakdown.
  30. The correct answer is D (Recommendations derived from a systematic literature review, with the strength of evidence graded for each): Guideline trustworthiness depends on transparent methods: a systematic search and an explicit grading of the evidence behind each recommendation. Expert endorsement, journal prestige and local habit are not substitutes for method. Each can coexist with weak or biased evidence.
  31. The correct answer is B (Random order of the three transfers within each session, with surfaces and heights varied): Once each skill is acquired, random ordering with varied surfaces forces him to retrieve and rebuild the solution each time, which improves retention and transfer even though performance within the session looks worse. Blocked, constant and massed schedules give better immediate performance but weaker carry-over, and trial-by-trial manual guidance prevents error detection.
  32. The correct answer is D (Guard from behind with a gait belt, clear the path, and use wide turns): Freezing is most likely during narrow, tight or fast turns, so wide turns on a clear path with belt guarding let him practise with a safety net. Visual and verbal cues help people with Parkinson disease overcome freezing, so removing them raises risk. Quick turns and a simultaneous counting task add demands that provoke freezing and falls rather than grading the challenge safely.
  33. The correct answer is D (PAINAD, because it scores breathing, vocalization, expression, body language and consolability by observation): People with advanced dementia cannot reliably self-report, so an observational tool such as PAINAD is used. The numeric rating scale, visual analog scale and McGill questionnaire all depend on the person understanding and describing their pain, so they are unsuitable here.
  34. The correct answer is A (Diastasis recti abdominis, because the gap exceeds two finger widths and tension is reduced): A gap wider than about two finger widths with poor linea alba tension and midline doming defines diastasis recti. A hernia is a localized defect rather than a wide midline separation, and a rectus tear follows trauma with bruising. A gap of this size with poor tension is not normal at ten weeks.
  35. The correct answer is D (Hip flexion beyond 90 degrees with adduction and internal rotation, which stresses the posterior capsule): After a posterolateral approach the posterior capsule is cut and repaired, so the dislocating combination is deep flexion with adduction and internal rotation. Extension with external rotation is the risk position after an anterior approach, and moderate abduction or low flexion does not drive the femoral head posteriorly.
  36. The correct answer is D (Possible femoroacetabular impingement, because the position pinches the femoral neck against the anterior acetabular rim): Flexion, adduction and internal rotation (FADIR) brings the femoral neck toward the anterior acetabular rim, so anterior groin pain suggests intra-articular pathology such as femoroacetabular impingement. The test is sensitive but not specific, so it supports rather than proves a diagnosis. Pain from the other structures would be felt elsewhere or with different provocation.
  37. The correct answer is A (Twelve and a half percent, from the 60 mL difference divided by the 480 mL uninjured): The side-to-side difference is 540 minus 480, which is 60 mL. Edema is expressed relative to the uninjured hand, so 60 divided by 480 is 12.5 percent. Using the injured volume as the denominator, reading millilitres as percent, or reporting the ratio itself gives incorrect values.
  38. The correct answer is A (Adhesive capsulitis, because passive range is limited as much as active range in a capsular pattern): Equal limits of active and passive motion, with external rotation most restricted, define the capsular pattern of adhesive capsulitis, and diabetes is a known risk. A cuff tear causes active limitation with preserved passive range, and subacromial pain keeps passive range full. Instability would present with apprehension rather than a stiff capsule.
  39. The correct answer is C (Pursed lip breathing with slow exhalation, because it slows her rate and reduces panic): Pursed lip breathing with a long exhalation slows the respiratory rate, reduces air trapping and gives the client a sense of control that settles panic. Forced deep inhalation increases effort, lying flat worsens breathlessness in heart failure, and stopping dressing removes function without addressing the symptom.
  40. The correct answer is B (Defer exercise, repeat the measurement after further rest, and consult the physician if it stays elevated): A resting systolic pressure above about 180 mmHg or diastolic above about 110 mmHg is a commonly used reason to defer exercise, and this reading is well above it. The reading is repeated after rest to rule out a transient rise, and persistent elevation needs medical input. Being symptom free or medicated does not make it safe to begin, even at low intensity.
  41. The correct answer is B (Brandt-Daroff exercises moving between sitting and side-lying positions, repeated several times daily to promote habituation): Brandt-Daroff exercises are a home habituation program for BPPV that repeatedly provoke symptoms until they lessen. Gaze stabilization treats vestibular hypofunction rather than canal debris. Avoiding provocation or wearing a collar does not treat the condition, and foam balance exercises address a different problem.
  42. The correct answer is D (Mini-BESTest, because it includes reactive, anticipatory and sensory tasks that challenge higher-level balance): A score of 55 of 56 on the Berg shows a ceiling effect, so it cannot capture the subtle deficits he describes. The Mini-BESTest adds dynamic and reactive tasks, sensory reorganization and gait with head turns, which probe the situations that make him unsteady. Timed Up and Go and gait speed are quick but also have ceiling effects in high-functioning clients.
  43. The correct answer is A (Gluteus medius and minimus tendons, because compressive load at the trochanter irritates their insertions): Greater trochanteric pain syndrome is now understood mainly as gluteal tendinopathy, with pain on side-lying, stairs and single-leg stance from compressive load on the tendon insertions. The bursa can contribute but is rarely the sole source. A labral or capsular source would produce groin pain with rotation, not focal trochanteric tenderness.
  44. The correct answer is C (Allodynia to light touch with temperature and sweating differences compared with the uninjured hand): Burning pain out of proportion to the injury with light touch intolerance and stiffness points to complex regional pain syndrome, which is supported by allodynia plus temperature and sweating differences between the hands. Local swelling, stiffness and end-range pain are expected after a fracture and do not separate this condition. Numbness with a positive tap over the superficial radial nerve suggests a focal nerve injury, which would not explain the widespread burning and autonomic changes.
  45. The correct answer is A (Week 5, the first measurement at or below the target of 4.0): The week 4 mean of 4.2 is still above 4.0, and the week 5 mean of 3.7 is the earliest value at or below it. Waiting for several measurements or only the final one is not required to say the target was reached. The steady fall after week 5 confirms the result.
  46. The correct answer is B (Mask the client, clean hands, and ask the nurse to confirm the precautions before treating): Routine practices include a point-of-care risk assessment, and a client with respiratory symptoms is a risk before any diagnosis is confirmed. Placing a mask on the client limits spread immediately, and the physiotherapist should confirm the precautions with nursing before treating. Waiting for an order or moving the client to a shared gym exposes others.
  47. The correct answer is D (Give situation, background, assessment and a specific request, such as a same-day ultrasound, using SBAR): A structured format such as SBAR delivers the key facts and a clear recommendation concisely, which makes agreement more likely and is safe for a time-sensitive concern. A long narrative or chart note delays action, and a blunt statement that the note is wrong invites defensiveness.
  48. The correct answer is B (Transverse myelitis, because a sensory level with brisk reflexes and bladder retention indicates cord inflammation): A thoracic sensory level, a tight band-like sensation, brisk reflexes and early urinary retention localize the lesion to the spinal cord, and the preceding viral illness fits post-infectious inflammation. Guillain-Barré causes ascending weakness with absent reflexes and no sensory level, while cauda equina syndrome produces lower motor neuron signs and saddle numbness. Without prior episodes, a first demyelinating relapse is less likely.
  49. The correct answer is A (Document findings objectively and report through the facility's mandatory reporting process, because suspected abuse requires reporting): Long-term care settings have mandatory reporting requirements for suspected resident abuse, and the duty applies even when the resident requests secrecy. Waiting for a meeting or confronting the staff member delays protection and may endanger her. Objective documentation of findings supports the report.
  50.
