The correct answer is D (Discuss the concern with the owner and review the college's standards on fair access): The physiotherapist's duty is to the client's access to needed care, and the usual first step is to raise the concern directly while checking regulatory standards on discrimination and access. Quietly declining referrals or referring away without telling the owner avoids the problem. Reporting before any discussion skips the step that could resolve it.
  51. The correct answer is A (Defer resistance training and call his physician about the INR, because bleeding risk is elevated): An INR of 4.2 is above the usual 2.0 to 3.0 target for atrial fibrillation, so the risk of spontaneous or exercise-related bleeding is raised and the physician needs to know before loaded exercise starts. Light work or fixed machines reduce injury risk but not the bleeding risk from a high INR. Changing a warfarin dose is a medical decision outside physiotherapy scope, so advising him to hold it is inappropriate.
  52. The correct answer is D (Ask privately how to address him, use his pronouns, and record legal details only where required): Asking privately avoids outing him in a public room, and using his name and pronouns is basic respect. Legal identifiers are still recorded where billing or law requires them, and the clinical record can use his name as appropriate. Calling out the legal name exposes him, and dropping legal details can break billing rules. His gender history is not needed for knee care.
  53. The correct answer is B (Report the suspicion promptly to the child welfare agency, without investigating or confronting the family): Reasonable grounds to suspect a child is in need of protection create a personal duty to report promptly to the child welfare agency, which then investigates. The duty does not depend on the child confirming abuse or on a manager's approval, and it cannot be met by documenting and suggesting voluntary services. Confronting or investigating further could endanger the boy and compromise the agency's work.
  54. The correct answer is C (Acknowledge her frustration, show the early gains on the table, and discuss options together): The table shows a 16-point gain on the LEFS by week 4, so she has made real progress that she is overlooking. Naming her frustration, showing the evidence and planning next steps together is honest and collaborative. Presenting only numbers lacks empathy, promising progress within two weeks cannot be guaranteed, and changing the program before sharing the scores leaves her out of the decision.
  55. The correct answer is D (Use a secure encrypted platform with a data agreement, and obtain informed consent to virtual care): Virtual care needs a platform that protects personal health information, usually with encryption and an agreement about how data is stored, together with consent that covers the virtual format and its risks. An encrypted platform without an agreement leaves data handling uncontrolled. Consent alone does not protect information sent over a consumer application, and storing recordings on a personal phone creates a new privacy risk.
  56. The correct answer is A (Ask him to explain the program in his own words and show the first week): Teach-back, where the learner explains and demonstrates the plan, shows whether the message was received and exposes gaps. Nodding, saying there are no questions or signing a form show only that teaching occurred, not that he understood. Repeating instructions without checking can simply repeat a message he has already misunderstood.
  57. The correct answer is B (Discharge her with a home plan and credit unused sessions, because treatment must be clinically justified): Care should continue only while it is clinically justified, and her goals have been met. Using up a prepaid package, or offering extra sessions because the price is already discounted, serves the clinic's financial interest rather than her need. Unused sessions should be credited or refunded according to clinic policy and consumer rules, so letting them lapse unfairly keeps her money.
  58. The correct answer is A (Active shoulder elevation is delayed until the surgeon permits it, while passive motion limits are followed): A large cuff repair has little tendon-to-bone strength early on, so active contraction of the repaired muscle is withheld for several weeks and passive range follows surgeon limits. Pain level is not a reliable indicator of healing, and early resisted or active work risks retear.
  59. The correct answer is B (Continue progressive loading and delay sprinting, because strength has not yet reached the 90 percent criterion): Return to sprinting should be criteria based, not date based. The chart shows 78 percent of the uninjured side, still under the commonly used 90 percent threshold, so pain free jogging alone is not enough. A fixed time frame ignores individual recovery and allowing sprinting before the criterion is met raises the risk of reinjury.
  60. The correct answer is A (The man going home, to assess his steps so discharge can proceed safely at noon): The discharge is time-bound, so the stair assessment must happen before noon or the discharge is delayed or unsafe. The knee replacement patient is cleared to mobilize at any point today, so her initial walk can follow without losing benefit. Updating placement forms for a stable patient is not urgent, and a planner's view of the day's discharges does not replace the clinician's own priority reasoning.
  61. The correct answer is D (The combination of axillary dissection and axillary radiation, because both impair lymph drainage): Removing axillary nodes and irradiating the axilla together damage the lymphatic route draining the arm, and this combination carries the highest risk. A lumpectomy without node surgery has little effect on arm drainage. Hormone therapy and age are not primary causes of lymphedema.
  62. The correct answer is B (Stop the movement, stabilize the tube, and call for the nurse and respiratory therapist): A displaced airway is an emergency, so the movement stops, the tube is held in place and the nurse and respiratory therapist are called at once. Moving him back or inspecting the circuit leaves the tube unsecured while help is delayed. Silencing the alarm ignores a possible loss of airway, and deflating the cuff can let the tube slide out completely.
  63. The correct answer is D (Social determinants of health shape access to care and recovery, because income and housing influence health): Income, housing and transportation are social determinants of health that affect access to care and the capacity to follow a rehabilitation program. Health literacy and motivation matter but do not account for structural barriers. Genetics contribute to risk but do not explain missed visits tied to cost and housing.
  64. The correct answer is A (Hold upright exercise and report the postural blood pressure drop to her physician the same day): A fall of 32 mmHg systolic and 16 diastolic on standing with symptoms is orthostatic hypotension, and it began with a new medication. Exercise should be held and the prescriber informed promptly. Stopping a medication is outside physiotherapy scope, and waiting a visit leaves her at risk of falling.
  65. The correct answer is B (Provincial legislation and college standards, which set whether and to whom a report must be made): Reporting duties for abuse of adults differ among provinces, so the physiotherapist follows provincial law and college guidance and consults when unsure. The client's wishes matter in how the response is planned but do not alone decide legal duties. The caregiver is the person of concern, and a single federal rule does not apply across provinces.
  66. The correct answer is B (Ask about his concerns, trial the lift with him, and agree on a safer transfer method): The husband is injuring himself and his wife is at risk, but his objection is about feeling impersonal, which can be addressed by listening and letting him try the equipment. Technique training cannot make a full-assist manual lift safe. Accepting the status quo leaves both at risk, and requesting placement skips simpler solutions.
  67. The correct answer is C (Discharge her with a home program, because further treatment is not clinically justified): The client has met her goals, so treatment decisions must follow clinical need and not revenue. Reduced frequency, extra consolidation visits or continuing on request would all keep billing for care that is no longer justified. A home program supports self-management and ends the episode appropriately.
  68. The correct answer is D (Stop the examination and arrange emergency transfer, because a cervical arterial dissection with stroke is possible): A sudden new severe headache with facial numbness and unsteadiness after neck trauma matches cervical arterial dissection with possible stroke, which is time critical. Position testing has poor diagnostic value and could provoke harm, so it does not replace emergency care. Sending her for a routine same-day visit or waiting to recheck signs delays treatment when minutes matter.
  69. The correct answer is B (Stop treatment and send him for emergency assessment, because the findings suggest acute compartment syndrome): Escalating pain, a tense swollen compartment, pain with passive stretch and new tingling indicate rising compartment pressure, which threatens nerve and muscle within hours and is a surgical emergency. Compression adds pressure, and waiting or scheduling outpatient imaging delays the urgent referral. Elevation and rechecking later does not relieve the pressure.
  70. The correct answer is A (As possibly respectful, so check understanding verbally rather than assuming disinterest or dishonesty): Eye contact norms differ across cultures, and his appropriate answers suggest engagement. The safest approach is to treat the cue as ambiguous and confirm understanding through words, for example with teach-back. Assuming distrust, confusion or disinterest imposes the physiotherapist's own cultural expectations and may lead to unnecessary changes in care.
  71. The correct answer is D (Provide short-term pain relief and maintain muscle activation so the athlete can keep training): Isometric holds can reduce tendon pain for a period and keep the quadriceps active during a season, creating a window for further loading. They do not deliver the fast stretch-shortening forces needed for collagen remodelling, do not lengthen tissue, and do not replace later heavy slow resistance and energy-storage loading.
  72. The correct answer is B (Semi-upright with the neck in neutral, because this lowers diaphragm pressure and keeps the airway open): A semi-upright position with the neck neutral reduces abdominal pressure on the diaphragm and keeps the airway aligned. Prone sleeping carries a risk in infants, neck extension can narrow a small airway, and head-down positions increase reflux and work of breathing.
  73. The correct answer is D (Hold taping until the skin heals, protect the area, and consider a lace-up brace instead): The photograph shows redness and blistering consistent with an adhesive skin reaction, so further tape on that skin would risk breakdown and infection. Protecting the skin and using a removable brace provides support in the meantime. An underwrap, lower tension or a different tape type still places adhesive over damaged skin.
  74. The correct answer is D (Arrange urgent medical assessment with spinal imaging and withhold spinal loading and manual therapy): Progressive night pain, fever, focal spinal tenderness, diabetes and a recent infection form a pattern suggesting spinal infection such as vertebral osteomyelitis or discitis. This needs urgent blood tests and MRI, and manual therapy or loading could worsen the problem. Presuming a muscle strain delays diagnosis of a condition that can cause cord compression.
  75. The correct answer is A (The physiotherapist remains accountable, so she must confirm competence and provide suitable supervision): Delegation assigns a task but does not transfer the regulated professional's accountability, so the physiotherapist must judge the worker's competence and supervise appropriately. A written acceptance does not move accountability to the worker, and the owner's role as employer is separate from clinical accountability. Accountability also covers delegated tasks she is not watching.
  76. The correct answer is B (Plan a short initial sitting period, such as thirty minutes, then check skin and offload): Because she cannot reposition and already has a stage 1 area, sitting time is built up gradually with checks and offloading. Waiting for discomfort is unreliable after a stroke with reduced sensation, and a cushion lowers but does not remove the risk.
  77. The correct answer is B (Shout for his wife to call 911 and bring an AED, then begin chest compressions): In an adult collapse, activating emergency services and getting an AED comes straight away, using a bystander if one is present, and compressions then start without delay. Compressions-first applies when the rescuer is alone with no way to call. Long pulse checks and rescue breaths before compressions waste time during which the brain receives no blood flow.
  78. The correct answer is D (No radiograph is needed, because none of the five rule criteria is present): The Ottawa knee rule calls for imaging only with age 55 or older, isolated patellar tenderness, fibular head tenderness, inability to flex to 90 degrees, or inability to bear weight for four steps. She meets none of these. Swelling, the timing of the injury and concern about ligament or occult bone injury are not criteria, and the age cutoff is 55.
  79. The correct answer is C (Stop the session, return her to bed and alert the physician, because signs suggest early sepsis): Fever, tachycardia, tachypnea, hypotension and new confusion together meet criteria for suspected sepsis, which needs urgent escalation. Continuing exercise or waiting stresses a failing circulation and delays treatment. Postoperative status explains some changes but not this combination.
  80. The correct answer is D (60 kg, which equals 75 percent of the maximum and suits eight to ten repetitions): Seventy-five percent of 80 kg is 60 kg, a load that matches the planned eight to ten repetitions. Lower percentages shift training toward endurance, and a load near 90 percent would permit only a few repetitions and be excessive for a client starting a program.
  81. The correct answer is A (Stop the exercise, sit her upright and help her use the reliever per her action plan): Chest tightness, wheeze, tachypnea and short phrases during exercise mean bronchoconstriction is occurring, so exercise stops and she is positioned upright with reliever use per her action plan. Slowing down, waiting, or nasal breathing while walking delays treatment. The earlier dose does not make a second dose inappropriate if symptoms continue.
  82. The correct answer is A (Refer for same-day medical assessment, because fever, cough and reduced breath sounds suggest a chest infection): Fever, productive cough and reduced breath sounds with pleuritic pain point to pneumonia or pleural disease, which needs prompt medical assessment. Partial pain reproduction with rib springing is a near miss, since chest wall tenderness can coexist with lung pathology. Mobilization, exercises or taping would delay appropriate care.
  83. The correct answer is B (Identify the affected clients, notify them, and report the breach as privacy law requires): A lost device holding unencrypted client information is a privacy breach, so the clinic must determine who is affected, notify those clients and report to the privacy regulator as the law requires. Waiting for an insurer or for police recovery delays duties that apply as soon as the breach is known. A remote wipe may limit further harm but does not remove the duty to notify when the data was unprotected and possibly viewed.
  84. The correct answer is A (The 7-point gain is under the 9-point clinically important threshold, so improvement is unproven): The commonly cited minimal clinically important difference for the LEFS is 9 points, so a 7-point gain may be real but is not yet shown to matter to the client. Passing the measurement error alone does not equal clinical importance. The LEFS captures function in daily activities, not strength impairment, so it can be interpreted without retesting strength.
  85. The correct answer is A (52 out of 100, because 10 yes answers give 40 and 6 sometimes answers give 12): Ten yes answers at 4 points contribute 40, six sometimes answers at 2 points contribute 12, and nine no answers contribute 0, for a total of 52 out of a possible 100. Counting sometimes as 1 or 4, or ignoring those answers, produces the wrong sums. Higher totals indicate greater self-perceived handicap from dizziness.
  86. The correct answer is D (Neck and scapular strengthening with mobilization, because manual therapy added to exercise improves short-term pain): For mechanical neck pain, exercise is the foundation, and adding manual therapy can give extra short-term pain relief. Manual therapy alone has weaker long-term effects, and therapeutic ultrasound, traction and collars lack strong support.
  87. The correct answer is C (Perform heel raises on flat ground and avoid dorsiflexion past neutral to limit insertional compression): At the insertion, dorsiflexion compresses the tendon against the calcaneus and worsens pain, so loading is done on flat ground within a neutral range. Deep drops from a step add the compression the client cannot tolerate, stretching into dorsiflexion adds more, and prolonged rest removes the loading stimulus the tendon needs.
  88. The correct answer is C (Contact the orthotist to review padding, because redness lasting beyond 30 minutes signals excess pressure): Mild redness that fades within about 30 minutes of brace removal is expected, but persistent redness points to a pressure area that the orthotist must adjust. Changing the prescribed wear time without the prescribing team undermines curve control. Creams do not address pressure, and ignoring a persistent mark risks a pressure injury.
  89. The correct answer is D (Yes, because tenderness at the base of the fifth metatarsal meets the midfoot criteria): The Ottawa ankle rules call for a foot series when there is bone tenderness at the base of the fifth metatarsal or the navicular, or when four steps cannot be taken. Her ability to walk does not override a positive tender point, and swelling alone is not the criterion.
  90. The correct answer is D (Put on a gown and gloves before entering, remove them inside the room, then clean hands): Contact precautions require gown and gloves donned before entry and removed before leaving the room, followed by hand hygiene, so contaminated items stay in the room. A mask is not needed for MRSA wound contact, and reusing a gown or wearing PPE in corridors spreads organisms to other patients and surfaces.
  91. The correct answer is C (Buoyancy, which unloads the limb in proportion to immersion depth and allows graded weight bearing): Buoyancy provides an upward force that offsets body weight, and deeper immersion unloads more, so loading can be graded by changing depth. Hydrostatic pressure affects edema and circulation rather than joint load. Viscosity gives resistance to movement, and warmth relaxes muscle, but neither changes body weight.
  92. The correct answer is C (Stop at once, check his symptoms, and respect his decision because consent can be withdrawn): Consent is an ongoing process and can be withdrawn at any point, so his request to stop must be honoured immediately. Dizziness during cervical treatment also needs monitoring and assessment for a possible vascular or other cause. A signed form or a referral does not override the client's withdrawal.
  93. The correct answer is B (Lead with the non-operated leg, then bring the operated leg and both crutches up together): Going up, the stronger non-operated leg leads and carries the body weight, and the operated leg follows with the crutches to share the load. Leading with the operated leg would place the full lift on a weak, partly weight-bearing limb. Moving the crutches first leaves the client unsupported on the lower step.
  94. The correct answer is B (Neurapraxia (Sunderland first degree), with full recovery expected over days to weeks as myelin repairs): A conduction block without axonal degeneration is neurapraxia, a Sunderland first degree injury in which the axon is intact and the myelin recovers within days to weeks. The other grades involve axonal disruption or loss of nerve continuity, which would show degeneration on testing and recover more slowly or incompletely.
  95. The correct answer is A (Resume normal activities early and perform gentle neck movements, rather than relying on a collar): For acute whiplash-associated disorder without red flags, early return to normal activity and active movement improves outcomes. Collars, prolonged rest and exclusively passive treatment are associated with slower recovery.
  96. The correct answer is D (About 85 percent, found by dividing surgical limb force by uninjured limb force): The limb symmetry index is surgical limb force divided by uninjured limb force, so 215 N divided by 253 N is about 85 percent. Inverting the ratio gives 118 percent, the deficit is 15 percent, and comparing different time points produces 43 percent, which does not describe current symmetry.
  97. The correct answer is B (Give specific, timely feedback on the observed behaviours and agree on how to correct them): Feedback works best when it is prompt, specific to what was observed and followed by an agreed plan. Waiting for an annual review delays correction of a safety-related habit, and general praise does not name the problem. The physiotherapist who delegates the task is responsible for monitoring its delivery.
  98. The correct answer is B (Use a visual cue, such as stepping over a strip of tape across the threshold): Freezing at thresholds responds best to external visual cues that give his attention a target, such as a line to step over. Dual-task counting increases cognitive load and worsens freezing, rapid small steps promote festination and further freezing, and leaning forward pushes his centre of mass ahead of his feet and risks a fall.
  99. The correct answer is C (Stop the session and arrange emergency assessment, because the findings suggest cauda equina syndrome): Saddle numbness with urinary retention are red flags for cauda equina syndrome, which can cause permanent bladder, bowel and sexual dysfunction without prompt decompression. A visit two days later or a lengthy screening examination postpones urgent care. Neural gliding treats a nerve root problem and could delay the emergency referral.

Practice Test 10 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is D (A superior labral lesion, because pain with the thumb down that eases supinated implicates the labrum): Deep pain that is provoked with internal rotation and eased with supination in the active compression test suggests a superior labral lesion in an overhead thrower. Negative apprehension and relocation tests make instability less likely, and normal strength with no joint line tenderness argues against cuff impingement or an acromioclavicular problem.
  2. The correct answer is B (Deep friction massage to the thigh, because vigorous early massage is linked with bone formation): Aggressive massage, heat and forceful stretching in the first days after a deep quadriceps contusion are associated with myositis ossificans. Compression with the knee held in flexion and gentle pain-free activation protect the muscle without provoking more bleeding, and brief cooling is a harmless option for comfort.
  3. The correct answer is A (Level III: walks with a hand-held mobility device indoors and uses wheeled mobility for longer distances): GMFCS level III describes children who walk with a hand-held mobility device such as a walker in most indoor settings and use wheeled mobility for longer distances. Level II children walk in most settings without limits indoors, level I children walk without limitations in all settings, and level IV children rely mainly on wheeled mobility. Her use of a walker indoors places her at level III.
  4. The correct answer is C (Specificity, because adaptations match the type of loading and movement pattern the athlete practises): The specificity principle states that the body adapts to the particular demands placed on it, so a jumper must practise jumping and landing. Overload and progression describe how much and how fast to load, and reversibility describes detraining; none of them explains choosing sport-like tasks.
  5. The correct answer is D (Supported arms let accessory muscles lift the ribs, and the diaphragm descends more easily): Forward lean with the arms supported fixes the shoulder girdle so accessory muscles can elevate the rib cage, and the abdominal contents shift to give the diaphragm a better length. The position is not a drainage position and does not work through vagal stimulation or lung perfusion changes.
  6. The correct answer is C (Ask him to demonstrate the exercises and explain them back in his own words): Teach-back checks how well the physiotherapist explained by having the client show and say the steps in his own words. Repeating names tests memory of labels, not the ability to perform or explain the exercises. A written handout suits poorly for someone who rarely reads, and relying on his wife to report problems later does not confirm his own understanding before he leaves.
  7. The correct answer is A (Start proximally at the neck and trunk, then treat the arm from upper arm to hand): Manual lymphatic drainage clears the proximal regions first so that fluid from the arm has somewhere to go, then works the arm in a proximal to distal order with light skin stretches. Firm, deep or brisk pressure collapses the fine lymph vessels, and the scarred axilla is not a drainage route.
  8. The correct answer is A (Keep well hydrated and avoid overheating, because dehydration and heat promote sickling and painful crises): Dehydration, heat, cold exposure and extreme exertion can promote red cell sickling and vaso-occlusive crises, so steady hydration and temperature control matter most. Thirst appears late and is an unreliable guide, and withholding fluids during a hot class makes dehydration worse. Moderate aerobic exercise is encouraged once cleared, so limiting her to stretching is unnecessary.
  9. The correct answer is C (In the second intercostal space along the right sternal border of the chest): The aortic area lies at the right sternal border in intercostal space two. The same space on the left is the pulmonic area, the left lower sternal border is the tricuspid area and the fifth intercostal space at the midclavicular line is the mitral area or apex. Correct placement helps localize murmurs and sounds to the valve of origin.
  10. The correct answer is D (Agree on one standard position, landmarks and end-point for the goniometer, then remeasure together): Large differences between raters usually come from different positions, landmarks or end-points, so standardizing the method and measuring again gives a reliable value. Averaging blends a possible error into the record, and choosing the larger or the senior value uses a rule of preference instead of fixing the cause.
  11. The correct answer is D (Venous ulcer, because a shallow irregular wound with brown staining near the ankle fits venous hypertension): A normal ankle-brachial index and palpable pulses make an arterial cause unlikely. Venous ulcers are usually shallow with irregular edges in the gaiter area near the medial ankle, with surrounding brown staining and edema. Neuropathic ulcers sit under pressure points of the foot, and pressure injuries sit over bony prominences.
  12. The correct answer is B (It sets evidence-based steps and timing to reduce variation, while allowing justified departures for individual needs): Care pathways standardize expected care based on evidence to reduce unnecessary variation and improve coordination. They are guides, so the clinician may depart from them when a client's needs differ, documenting and explaining the variance herself rather than seeking prior administrative approval. They do not replace clinical judgment, fix visits to a funding schedule or demand that every client hit each milestone on a set date.
  13. The correct answer is D (Add one point for 12 years or less of education, giving a normal score of 26): The Montreal Cognitive Assessment adds one point for people with 12 years or less of education to correct for education effects. His adjusted total of 26 reaches the usual normal cutoff of 26. Points are never subtracted, and the adjustment is a single point, so the other totals are miscalculated.
  14. The correct answer is C (Use routine standard precautions, because hand hygiene and barriers already control bloodborne risk): Standard precautions are applied to every client because infection status is often unknown, and they are sufficient for HIV when skin is intact. HIV does not spread through intact skin, casual contact or respiratory droplets, so extra contact precautions or scheduling him last add burden without benefit. His status is private health information and is shared only with those who need it for his care, not to inform staff about risk.
  15. The correct answer is B (Keep the unit upright and below chest level, and avoid clamping or pulling the tubing): The drain stays upright and below chest level so fluid cannot siphon back and the water seal is maintained, and the tubing is neither clamped without an order, which can cause a tension pneumothorax, nor pulled. Tipping the unit breaks the seal. Disconnecting the tubing opens the pleural space to air, even when the unit is otherwise held correctly.
  16. The correct answer is D (Offload the foot with non-weight-bearing, arrange urgent medical review for suspected Charcot, and monitor temperatures): A warm, swollen, painless foot in a neuropathic diabetic with a temperature difference of about 2 degrees or more suggests acute Charcot neuroarthropathy even when early radiographs look normal. Immediate offloading prevents collapse while the physician arranges further imaging. Protected weight bearing still loads a fragile foot, and a normal early radiograph does not exclude the condition. A thrombosis work-up and compression would not address the temperature difference and loading risk.
  17. The correct answer is A (Reduce swim volume to a pain-free level and substitute dryland strength work while gradually rebuilding): A sharp rise in training volume is the likely driver, so the load is reduced to a pain-free level and rebuilt in steps while fitness is maintained with other work. Swapping strokes at the same volume leaves the total load high. Total rest followed by a return to the peak load repeats the original error.
  18. The correct answer is D (In the objective section, because it is a measurement obtained by the physiotherapist): Measured or observed findings, such as range of motion, belong in the objective section. The subjective section holds what the client reports, the assessment holds the clinical impression drawn from the data, and the plan lists what will be done next.
  19. The correct answer is A (Reflect his frustration and ask which parts of past care failed him): He has just voiced a feeling of not being heard, so naming that feeling and asking about it shows he is being listened to before anything else happens. Reviewing goals and starting treatment are reasonable later steps, but doing them first repeats the pattern he complained about. Describing credentials answers a question he did not ask.
  20. The correct answer is D (Grade 2 (poor), because full movement occurs only when gravity is eliminated): Grade 2 means full range of motion with gravity eliminated. Grade 3 requires full range against gravity, grade 1 is a palpable contraction without movement, and grade 4 requires full range against gravity with some resistance.
  21. The correct answer is C (Send it through the clinic's encrypted messaging or record system to the verified recipient): Personal health information should move through secure, accountable channels to a confirmed recipient. A fax to an unverified number can reach the wrong office, and standard email, even with the name removed, is not secure or easy to audit. A paper copy carried by the client is slow and does not support timely shared care, even though the client agreed to the sharing.
  22. The correct answer is B (Ask the client what she already understands about her hip problem): In the SPIKES sequence, setting is followed by perception, where the clinician finds out what the client already knows and expects. This shapes how the information is then shared. Giving the findings, responding to emotion and planning strategy come later, and doing them early risks missing her own understanding.
  23. The correct answer is A (Postpone stairs, request physician review of the dizziness, and agree on when to reassess readiness): Unexplained dizziness on standing is a safety issue that the physiotherapist's own assessment may not have detected. Stairs should wait until the physician has reviewed it, with a plan to reassess. Guarding does not remove the risk, checking pressure afterward is too late, and the readiness decision is a shared one.
  24. The correct answer is D (It may be shared in the circle of care under implied consent unless the client objects): Health information custodians may rely on implied consent to share information among providers involved in the same client's care, unless the client has withheld or limited consent. Requiring a new verbal or written consent for each discussion goes beyond what the legislation asks within the circle of care. Privacy law also applies fully in hospitals, so sharing there is not unrestricted.
  25. The correct answer is A (Gently squeeze and lift around the urethra and anus while breathing normally): A correct pelvic floor contraction is an inward and upward squeeze around the openings with normal breathing and minimal use of other muscles. Gluteal, adductor and breath-holding strategies are common compensations that raise pressure and do not train the pelvic floor. Pushing down stretches the muscles.
  26. The correct answer is B (Respect his refusal, document the informed discussion, and explain how he can reconsider treatment later): A capable adult may refuse treatment after the risks and benefits are explained, and treating him against his wishes would be an assault. Documenting the discussion protects both parties, and leaving the door open allows him to change his mind. A refusal does not end the duty to provide information and ongoing safe care, and a surgeon cannot override a capable client's decision. Pressing for a trial session ignores the refusal he has already given.
  27. The correct answer is D (Provide training on the new lift and re-verify competence by observation before independent use): Competence is specific to the task and equipment. When equipment changes, the physiotherapist ensures training and verifies competence again by observation. An old sign-off, self-study or a limit on client type does not demonstrate safe use of the new device.
  28. The correct answer is D (Warm the hand, remove the polish or change the probe site, and recheck before acting): A normal respiratory rate, full sentences and no distress do not match a true saturation of 86 percent. Cold fingers and dark nail polish are known causes of falsely low readings, so the probe site should be corrected and the value rechecked. Treating or escalating a doubtful number is premature, and abandoning monitoring is unsafe.
  29. The correct answer is B (It must fit her college's scope and her competence, even if another profession also does it): Scopes of practice overlap by design, and a physiotherapist may perform a shared activity when it is within her college's standards and she is competent to do it. Employers and colleagues can organize work but cannot expand or remove regulatory scope. Competence can grow after graduation through training, so entry-level education does not set a permanent limit.
  30. The correct answer is B (0.63 metres per second, calculated by dividing the timed 10 metres by 16 seconds): Gait speed is distance divided by time over the timed segment only, so 10 metres divided by 16 seconds is 0.625, or about 0.63 metres per second. Using the whole walkway includes the acceleration and deceleration zones, and inverting the fraction gives seconds per metre, not speed.
  31. The correct answer is A (Grade 1+, a catch followed by minimal resistance through the rest of the range): A catch in mid range followed by minimal resistance for the remainder is grade 1+. Grade 1 has the catch only at the end of range. Grade 2 involves a more marked increase through most of the range, and grade 3 makes passive movement difficult.
  32. The correct answer is D (Call for urgent medical help immediately, because drowsiness and stridor at rest indicate severe airway obstruction): Stridor at rest, retractions and especially drowsiness and pallor signal severe croup with impending airway failure and fatigue. A quieter cough in this setting is a warning, not improvement. Calm positioning, cool mist and waiting are reasonable for mild croup, but this child needs emergency medical care now.
  33. The correct answer is D (Fever, neck stiffness and an altered level of consciousness or mental status): The classic triad is fever, neck stiffness and altered mental status, although fewer than half of adults present with all three and headache is also common. Joint swelling with rash, raised intracranial pressure signs and cervical radiculopathy each describe other conditions. Recognizing the triad prompts urgent medical referral rather than treatment.
  34. The correct answer is A (Six minutes daily, matching bad-day tolerance, then increase by small fixed amounts): Pacing sets a baseline she can achieve on both good and bad days, which breaks the boom and bust cycle, and then raises it by planned small steps. Using her best-day tolerance or an average still triggers flares on bad days. Stopping when pain begins is pain-contingent and keeps activity tied to symptoms.
  35. The correct answer is C (Report promptly to the child protection agency, because reasonable grounds to suspect trigger the duty): Professionals who have reasonable grounds to suspect a child is being abused must report promptly to the child protection agency; certainty or proof is not required. Waiting for more evidence or confronting the parent can place the child at greater risk. Referring to a physician does not discharge the duty to report personally.
  36. The correct answer is B (Moderate effort where she can still talk, because this level suits mother and fetus): For a healthy pregnancy without contraindications, moderate intensity is recommended, and the talk test is a practical way to monitor it. A fixed low heart rate cap is outdated and unnecessarily restrictive. Vigorous work near maximum is not advised for most women without specific supervision, and interval bursts offer no protective advantage.
  37. The correct answer is A (Camera angle and landmark placement reduce accuracy compared with in-person goniometry, so values are approximate): Remote measures depend on camera position, alignment and landmark identification, so they are useful for tracking trends but less precise than goniometry. A better camera does not remove positioning and landmark error, and the measure is not good enough to replace in-person assessment after surgery. Seated measures taken the same way each time can still show change across visits.
  38. The correct answer is C (Seek ophthalmology clearance first, then use lighter loads with exhalation on effort and avoid straining): Proliferative retinopathy carries a risk of vitreous hemorrhage with straining, breath-holding, heavy maximal lifts and head-down positions. Ophthalmology clearance comes first, followed by lighter loads with exhalation on exertion. Long-term benefits of strength training do not justify maximal lifting now.
  39. The correct answer is A (No independent walking at 20 months, because this is later than the typical window): Independent walking is expected by about 18 months, and this boy has still not started at 20 months, so his age is beyond the upper end of the window. Sitting at 7, crawling at 10 and standing alone at 15 months all fall inside their typical windows, so they are variations of normal and do not by themselves justify referral.
  40. The correct answer is B (Remove the drainage with a cleaning wipe, then disinfect and allow the full contact time): Visible body fluid must be cleaned off first because organic matter reduces disinfectant action, and the surface is then disinfected for the full labelled contact time before the next client uses it. Covering the patch hides contamination, and wiping immediately dry cuts the contact time short. Sterilization is not required for a non-critical surface.
  41. The correct answer is D (Approximately 21 kg on the scale, calculated as 25 percent of 84 kg body weight): Twenty-five percent of 84 kg is 0.25 x 84 = 21 kg. A reading of 63 kg reflects the 75 percent that should stay off the leg, 42 kg reflects half of body weight, and 25 kg wrongly treats the percentage as kilograms.
  42. The correct answer is D (Ask directly about self-harm thoughts and report to the team today, because the statement signals risk): A statement that he would be better off gone, with withdrawal, poor sleep and weight loss, raises concern for depression with possible suicidal thinking. Asking directly is safe and necessary, and the team must be told the same day so that a risk assessment can occur. Scoring a questionnaire first or waiting for the next session delays action on a potential safety issue.
  43. The correct answer is A (Withhold compression and refer her back to the physician for vascular assessment): An index of 0.4 with rest pain indicates severe arterial insufficiency, and compression can reduce blood flow further and cause tissue loss. She needs vascular assessment first. Lower-pressure bandaging or stockings are still compression, and checking weekly leaves her unprotected meanwhile.
  44. The correct answer is A (Jordan's Principle, which says the child receives needed services first and jurisdictions resolve payment afterward): Jordan's Principle is a child-first rule for First Nations children so that disputes between governments do not delay needed services. Joyce's Principle concerns racism-free access to health care and recognition of traditional practices, but does not address payment disputes. The duty to consult and Gladue principles belong to treaty and criminal law.
  45. The correct answer is C (Week 4, because her distance first reached a level 30 m or more above baseline): Her baseline was 310 m, so the target is 340 m or more. The first weekly value at or above 340 m is 342 m at week 4. At week 3 she had gained only 21 m, and later weeks are higher than needed but not the first time the goal was met.
  46. The correct answer is A (Keep her still and call for emergency medical assessment, because a hip fracture is likely): A shortened, externally rotated leg with severe groin pain after a fall is the classic presentation of a proximal femoral fracture. The safe response is to avoid movement and weight-bearing and to obtain emergency assessment. Lifting, straightening the leg or waiting an hour for analgesic to work can worsen displacement and delay care.
  47. The correct answer is A (Arrange same-day medical assessment for suspected septic arthritis and withhold exercise or manual therapy): A hot, swollen, erythematous joint with fever, pain on all movement, inability to bear weight and a recent skin break is septic arthritis until proven otherwise. It needs urgent aspiration and antibiotics because cartilage damage occurs within days. Exercise, manual therapy or delayed review risks joint destruction and systemic spread.
  48. The correct answer is C (Repetitive, progressively harder walking practice over varied distances and surfaces, targeting her mailbox route): Walking improves most with high-repetition, task-specific walking practice that is progressively challenged and matched to the real goal. Handling to normalize gait quality, impairment-based strengthening first, and bottom-up sequences of seated work all delay or dilute practice of the actual task.
  49. The correct answer is D (Interacting conditions and conflicting advice mean her goals and plans need alignment across professions): A case conference is most useful when several conditions interact and professionals are giving advice that conflicts. Aligning goals protects the client from mixed messages. Admissions do not by themselves show that the exercise dose is wrong, and age or frailty alone does not create a rule requiring team approval or full assessments from every profession.
  50. The correct answer is A (Hold the mobilization and report the deterioration urgently to the nurse and physician): New confusion, tachypnea, tachycardia, fever and a falling saturation on unchanged oxygen indicate clinical deterioration, possibly sepsis. The priority is escalation to the medical team, not mobilization, airway clearance or a delay of half an hour. Adjusting oxygen without orders or a protocol is outside safe practice.
  51. The correct answer is A (Choice and control: offer a gown, a drape, or a pause so she sets the pace): Trauma often involves loss of control, so giving the client real say about exposure, positioning and timing restores a sense of safety. Explaining the plan, reassuring her about privacy or adding a chaperone are useful ideas, but each keeps the pace and the order in the physiotherapist's hands while she is already distressed. She does not need to disclose her history to receive adapted care.
  52. The correct answer is C (Stop the test and seat her safely, because her systolic pressure fell as workload increased): A fall in systolic pressure of more than 10 mmHg despite increasing workload, here 164 to 132, with symptoms, is an indication to terminate the test. Heart rate below the target does not override an abnormal blood pressure response, and she needs monitoring after stopping.
  53. The correct answer is D (Stop the walk, seat her upright, coach pursed-lip breathing and recheck saturation until it recovers): A saturation below about 88 percent with marked breathlessness and accessory muscle use is a stopping criterion for exercise in chronic lung disease. Sitting upright and pursed-lip breathing reduce work of breathing while saturation is rechecked. Slowing down or resting on the feet does not give enough recovery, and a modest drop is only acceptable above the safe threshold.
  54. The correct answer is D (High frequency near 100 Hz, short pulses, and intensity giving strong comfortable tingling without muscle contraction): Conventional TENS uses high frequency, short pulse durations and an intensity that produces strong, comfortable paresthesia below motor threshold, targeting gate control. Low-frequency, motor-level settings describe acupuncture-like TENS, a different mode. Sub-sensory stimulation does not engage the intended mechanism.
  55. The correct answer is B (Regular moderate aerobic activity with resistance work is safe and reduces fatigue during treatment): Evidence supports moderate aerobic and resistance exercise during treatment for reducing cancer-related fatigue in medically stable people. Rest alone deconditions the client and increases fatigue, and nothing shows that training delays recovery or must wait until treatment is finished.
  56. The correct answer is D (Agree on one transfer method, post it at the bedside, and have all staff use it): Several professions legitimately teach transfers, so the problem is inconsistency, not overlap. A shared, documented method used by everyone gives a confused patient one clear message. Assigning the task to one profession removes useful hands, and a method chosen by one person and passed on verbally is easily lost. Separate teaching days still leave several techniques in play.
  57. The correct answer is C (Short moderate-intensity bouts on a recumbent cycle with rest intervals, increased gradually as tolerated): People with moderate disability benefit from seated or recumbent aerobic exercise in short bouts with rests, which limits fatigue and fall risk from foot drop. Intensity and duration are then progressed gradually. High-intensity continuous walking risks fatigue and falls, and avoiding aerobic and strength work gives up well-established benefits.
  58. The correct answer is B (The Durkan carpal compression test, which presses directly over the median nerve at the tunnel): The Durkan test uses sustained direct pressure over the carpal tunnel and is positive when median distribution symptoms appear. Tinel's sign uses percussion, Phalen's test uses sustained wrist flexion, and the reverse Phalen uses sustained extension. The stem describes pressure, so only the compression test matches.
  59. The correct answer is C (Medium risk, because the total is 4 or more but the psychosocial subscale is below 4): On the STarT Back tool a total of 3 or less is low risk, and a total of 4 or more with a psychosocial subscale of 4 or more is high risk. A total of 4 or more with a subscale below 4 is medium risk, which fits this client. The subscale only moves a client into the high group when it also reaches 4 or more.
  60. The correct answer is B (Cauda equina syndrome, because saddle numbness with urinary change suggests compression of sacral nerve roots): Bilateral leg weakness with saddle sensory loss and new urinary hesitancy points to compression of the cauda equina, which is a surgical emergency. Stenosis, a single-root disc herniation and spondylitis can all cause back or leg symptoms but do not explain bladder change with saddle numbness.
  61. The correct answer is B (Take fast-acting carbohydrate and recheck glucose, delaying exercise until it reaches her target range): With rapid-acting insulin active and a pre-exercise glucose near the lower end of the target range, exercise can push glucose into hypoglycemia. Diabetes guidance advises taking carbohydrate and rechecking until glucose is in the pre-exercise target range. Waiting for symptoms is unsafe because warning signs may lag, and a physiotherapist does not adjust insulin doses.
  62. The correct answer is A (Hold vigorous training and refer him for physician evaluation before progressing beyond light activity): Exertional chest tightness relieved by rest in a middle-aged man with hypertension may be angina, so he needs medical evaluation before vigorous exercise or testing. Resistance training also raises cardiac load. A clinic submaximal test would expose him to the very risk that needs prior assessment.
  63. The correct answer is B (A 66-year-old man with knee osteoarthritis and an arrhythmia whose medication changed last week): Immersion shifts blood centrally and raises cardiac workload, so a recently unstable rhythm disorder needs medical review first. Stable, controlled hypertension, a healed scar and anxiety about deep water do not require deferral and can be managed with screening and graded entry.
  64. The correct answer is A (Avoid crossing the legs or side-lying on that side, since compression irritates the trochanteric tendon): Gluteal tendons are compressed against the greater trochanter when the hip is adducted, so side-lying on the painful side and crossed legs aggravate symptoms. Stretching into adduction adds compression. Complete rest deconditions the tendon, and ice may ease pain briefly but does not alter tendon structure.
  65. The correct answer is D (Recognize her limited pediatric experience and refer him, because friendship does not change competence limits): A personal relationship does not alter the obligation to practise only where she is competent. Parental consent confirms agreement to care but cannot make an inexperienced provider competent. Adult experience does not transfer fully to pediatric neurological conditions, and a trial period still exposes the child to care outside her competence.
  66. The correct answer is D (Clinical urgency and risk of harm from delay, applied with the same criteria to every referral): Triage allocates scarce appointments by clinical need and risk, and applying the same criteria to everyone keeps the process fair. A recent hip fracture repair in a client living alone carries more risk from delay than chronic knee pain. Referral order, funding source and personal preference do not measure need.
  67. The correct answer is B (Allow a brief silence, then gently invite her to continue at her own pace): Purposeful silence gives her room to collect herself and signals that her story matters, and a gentle invitation lets her choose whether to continue. Summarizing, probing for the cause or ending the visit takes control of the conversation away from her. Moving on to the physical exam would also skip the emotional moment she has just opened.
  68. The correct answer is D (Level IV, confused-agitated, with heightened activity, bizarre behaviour and very short attention span): Level IV is defined by heightened activity, bizarre or aggressive behaviour and a very short attention span. Level V is confused but not agitated, and level VI shows goal-directed behaviour with cueing. Level III patients react inconsistently and are not this active.
  69. The correct answer is C (A third heart sound, reflecting rapid ventricular filling in a volume-overloaded ventricle): A low-pitched sound just after S2 is an S3 gallop, produced by rapid early ventricular filling into a dilated or volume-overloaded ventricle, which fits heart failure with new edema. An S4 occurs just before S1, a friction rub is scratchy and not gallop-like, and an opening snap is high-pitched. A new S3 should be reported to the medical team.
  70. The correct answer is D (Challenging, progressed balance exercise totalling about three hours weekly, because challenge and dose drive benefit): Systematic reviews show exercise reduces falls most when it includes balance training that is challenging, progressed over time and delivered at a sufficient dose, about three hours per week. Flexibility classes, walking alone and supported low-load machine work do not provide enough balance challenge. Group formats such as multicomponent classes or tai chi can deliver this dose.
  71. The correct answer is A (Move to a quiet room, face him closely, and speak clearly at normal volume): Reducing background noise, facing the person so lip movements and expressions are visible, and speaking clearly at a normal pace work best with hearing aids. Amplifying the aids in a noisy room also amplifies the noise and can be uncomfortable. Exaggerated speech from the side distorts the message, and written handouts alone may leave verbal safety instructions unclear.
  72. The correct answer is D (Measure lying and standing blood pressure, then discuss a medication review with his physician): Dizziness on standing with near falls and multiple medications raises concern for orthostatic hypotension, which should be checked and communicated to his physician before exercise progresses. Exercise is valuable for falls prevention, so avoiding it is wrong, but starting without addressing a treatable cause is not safe.
  73. The correct answer is A (Allow his cane and time a 3 metre walk, turn and return until he sits): The Timed Up and Go is performed with the person's usual walking aid, from an armchair, over a 3 metre path with a turn and return, timed until he is seated again at a comfortable and safe pace. Removing the cane or lengthening the distance changes the test. Timing only the outbound walk or requesting a fast pace does not reflect the standard protocol.
  74. The correct answer is C (She may perform it when legislation authorizes physiotherapists and she is competent and follows college standards): Controlled acts may be performed by a registrant only when the governing health profession legislation authorizes them, and the practitioner must also be competent and meet the college's standards. A referral or a delegation from a physician is not the source of her authority, and a prescription for each client is not required. Competence can be gained after graduation.
  75. The correct answer is B (Lower both crutches and the left leg together, then step down with the right leg): When descending, the crutches and the affected leg go down first, and the stronger leg follows, summarized as down with the bad. This keeps the supporting leg over the higher step until the crutches are secure. Leading with the stronger leg is the rule for going up and risks a forward fall on the way down.
  76. The correct answer is A (Bone tenderness along the posterior edge of the distal 6 cm of the fibula): The Ottawa ankle rules call for imaging when there is bone tenderness at the posterior edge or tip of either malleolus, or when the client cannot bear weight for four steps. Ligament tenderness alone does not meet the rule. The first metatarsal base and Achilles tendon are not rule landmarks, whereas the fifth metatarsal base and navicular are the midfoot landmarks.
  77. The correct answer is D (Disclose her ownership of the store and give the client other supplier options): Disclosure of the ownership interest plus real alternatives lets the client decide freely. Selling at cost still leaves her in a dual role as clinician and vendor. Public registration of a business is not the same as telling the client directly.
  78. The correct answer is D (Left side-lying, which relieves uterine compression of the inferior vena cava and restores venous return): In late pregnancy the uterus can compress the inferior vena cava in supine, reducing venous return and causing supine hypotension. Turning to the left side moves the uterus off the vein. Raising the legs while supine leaves the compression in place.
  79. The correct answer is C (13 clients, calculated as 1 divided by the absolute risk reduction of 0.08, rounded up): The absolute risk reduction is 20 minus 12, or 8 percentage points (0.08). Number needed to treat is 1 divided by 0.08, which is 12.5, and it is conventionally rounded up to 13. The other values come from the absolute reduction, the relative reduction and the event rate, which are not the number needed to treat.
  80. The correct answer is D (Add a small increment to the ankle cuff weight, because strength gains need progressive loading): The Otago programme progresses leg strengthening by gradually increasing ankle cuff weights once the current load feels easy. Added repetitions do not provide the progressive overload needed for strength gains. Moving to a gym is not part of the home-based programme, and strength in older adults at fall risk is rarely adequate.
  81. The correct answer is A (Use short choice questions, offer pictures or writing, and allow extra time for her own replies): In expressive aphasia comprehension is often preserved, so the aim is to reduce the demand on word retrieval while keeping her as the decision maker. Short questions, visual supports and extra time let her express her own goals. Open questions, even when slowly delivered, still demand the word finding she cannot do, and speaking for her or finishing her sentences removes her voice and can misrepresent what she meant.
  82. The correct answer is A (Function is improving while pain is stable, so continue the planned gradual progression): In chronic pain care, success is judged by function and goal achievement, not only pain scores. His walking time rose steadily from 5 to 20 minutes with pain steady around 5 to 6, which is a good response. Holding progression or switching to passive care would reinforce pain-focused behaviour.
  83. The correct answer is D (Begin light activity and a gradual school return with breaks, keeping symptoms mild and brief): After a short period of relative rest of one to two days, current consensus supports gradual, symptom-guided return to light activity and school, since prolonged strict rest is linked with slower recovery. Full school days at this symptom level are too much too soon, and a four-week activity ban has no support.
  84. The correct answer is A (Up to 20 minutes with skin checks, because longer exposure raises the chance of cold injury): Ice packs are generally applied for about 10 to 20 minutes with the skin and sensation checked, since longer exposure increases the risk of nerve and skin injury without extra benefit. Very short applications give little cooling, and waiting for numbness over 30 minutes exposes the superficial nerves to unnecessary cold.
  85. The correct answer is C (Ask about numbness around the genitals and anus, because saddle sensory loss supports cauda equina compression): Bladder change with bilateral leg pain calls for screening of other cauda equina signs, and saddle numbness is the key sign of sacral root compression. Questions about inflammatory stiffness, infection and fracture are valid red flag screens, but they do not clarify the urgent neurological threat that the urinary change suggests. Positive saddle findings would require emergency referral.
  86. The correct answer is D (Short sentences, plain words and large print, with a photograph of each exercise): Patient materials work best when they match the reader's literacy and vision: plain language, large type and pictures of each movement. Small print is hard for a client who tires with reading, and a web link depends on a device and help she may not have at home. Technical terms and a glossary raise the reading burden and make correct performance less likely.
  87. The correct answer is C (Adjust the comparison for client complexity before drawing conclusions, since the two caseloads differ): Case mix strongly affects expected change, so raw averages from different caseloads are not a fair comparison of skill. Adjusting or comparing like with like keeps the data useful. Ranking or common targets penalize the clinician who treats harder cases, while discarding the data wastes information that can still guide improvement.
  88. The correct answer is C (Wash hands with soap and water after glove removal, because alcohol rub fails against spores): C. difficile forms spores that alcohol-based rub does not reliably destroy, so hand washing with soap and water physically removes them. The organism spreads by the fecal-oral route, not through the air, so a respirator is unnecessary. Mobilization still helps her recovery and can proceed under precautions.
  89. The correct answer is A (A pelvic belt worn low across the hips, because compression reduces painful joint motion): Pain that eases with pelvic compression during the active straight leg raise suggests that a support belt will help, and it should sit low over the greater trochanters and pubic region. Prolonged bed rest causes deconditioning and does not treat pelvic girdle pain. Thrust manipulation is not appropriate in pregnancy with a hypermobile pelvis, and resisted adduction typically aggravates symphysis pain.
  90. The correct answer is D (Use compression and elevation with gentle movement, and avoid cold packs because of her vasospasm): Cold can trigger severe vasospasm in Raynaud phenomenon, causing pain, blanching and potentially tissue damage, so cold modalities are a contraindication. Compression, elevation and early gentle movement control swelling without cooling. Barriers, brief exposure or monitoring blanching only manage the consequences of a risk that can be avoided.
  91. The correct answer is B (Favour low-impact work and avoid collision or fall-prone tasks, because trauma can cause serious bleeding): Anticoagulation makes trauma more dangerous, so low-impact activity is chosen and collisions or fall-prone tasks are avoided while balance is trained safely. A therapeutic INR does not remove the injury risk of a hard fall. Avoiding walking and resistance work would give up the benefits of exercise without a clear reason.
  92. The correct answer is B (The 10:05 view by PT Brown, because no treatment relationship is documented for that client): Access is appropriate only when linked to assigned duties, and the 10:05 view is the only entry with no booking or referral behind it. PT Singh edited a note for a client on her own caseload, the receptionist viewed an invoice for a client checked in that day, and the assistant opened a plan assigned by the supervising physiotherapist.
  93. The correct answer is A (Respect the refusal today, offer another time or approach later, and document his response): Consent from a substitute decision-maker does not permit treating a person who is actively refusing, and a single refusal does not end the program. Trying a different time or approach and documenting is the respectful course. Refusal alone does not require a new capacity assessment.
  94. The correct answer is C (255 minutes, because one 45-minute class replaces ten 30-minute individual sessions totalling 300 minutes): Ten individual sessions of 30 minutes use 300 minutes a week, and the group class uses 45, so 255 minutes are saved. The saving is not the whole 300 minutes because the class itself takes time, and adding the two values or counting only the class length misreads what is being replaced.
  95. The correct answer is A (360 minutes of direct care, leaving 90 minutes for documentation, meetings and breaks): A 7.5 hour shift is 450 minutes. Eighty percent of 450 is 360 minutes of direct care, and the other 20 percent, or 90 minutes, remains for other duties. Swapping the two values or using the whole shift misreads the percentage.
  96. The correct answer is C (Tape may give small short-term pain relief but should supplement, not replace, active rehabilitation): Systematic reviews show kinesiology tape produces at most small, short-term benefits for pain and does not repair tissue or reliably change scapular mechanics. It can be an adjunct to exercise if the client finds it helpful. Claims of tissue repair are unsupported, and it is not harmful when skin is healthy.
  97. The correct answer is D (Hillcrest, because its wait is long and its service use is lowest): Unmet need is shown by a long wait together with low use of service, and Hillcrest has the lowest visit rate with a long wait and the highest share of residents without a vehicle. Oakdale has the longest wait but a high visit rate. Lakeview is intermediate, and Riverside has the shortest wait and highest use, so it has the least unmet need.
  98. The correct answer is A (The common extensor origin, especially extensor carpi radialis brevis, because resisted wrist extension loads that tendon): Pain on resisted wrist extension with local tenderness just distal to the lateral epicondyle points to the common extensor origin, most often extensor carpi radialis brevis. Ligament or annular involvement would be provoked by joint stress or rotation. Posterior interosseous nerve irritation is usually tender over the supinator, further distal.
  99. The correct answer is B (Respect the resident's decision, explore her concerns, offer modified activities, and document the discussion): A capable client may withdraw consent to any part of care, and the family cannot override that decision or be required to approve it. The physiotherapist should explore why she declines, look for acceptable alternatives that respect her goals, and record the exchange. Discharging her or delaying the program to wait for her to adapt both undermine her autonomy.
